# PART I: EVE OF THE NATIVITY OF CHRIST (ROYAL HOURS AND VESPERAL DIVINE LITURGY OF ST. BASIL)

## Order of the Royal Hours on the Eve of the Nativity of Christ

*It should be known concerning these Hours that if the Nativity of Christ happen to fall on a Sunday or Monday, we chant the Hours beforehand on Friday. But if the Nativity happen to fall on a Tuesday, Wednesday, Thursday, Friday, or Saturday, we chant the Hours on the very day of the Eve.*[^4]

*First Hour.*

*The Priest, having taken his stand before the Royal Doors, begins:*

**Priest:** Blessed is our God, always, now and ever, and unto ages of ages.

**Choir:** Amen.

Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee.

O Heavenly King, Comforter, Spirit of Truth, Who art everywhere present and fillest all things, Treasury of good things and Giver of life, come and dwell within us, and cleanse us from every stain, and save our souls, O Good One.

Holy God, Holy Mighty, Holy Immortal, have mercy on us. *(Thrice)*

Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.

All-Holy Trinity, have mercy on us; Lord, cleanse our sins; Master, pardon our iniquities; Holy One, visit and heal our infirmities for Thy name's sake.

Glory: Now and ever: Lord, have mercy. *(Thrice)*

Our Father, Who art in the heavens, hallowed be Thy name; Thy kingdom come; Thy will be done, on earth as it is in heaven. Give us this day our daily bread; and forgive us our debts, as we forgive our debtors; and lead us not into temptation, but deliver us from the evil one.

**Priest:** For Thine is the kingdom, and the power, and the glory, of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, now and ever, and unto ages of ages.

**Choir:** Amen.

Lord, have mercy. *(Twelve times)* Glory: Now and ever:

† Come, let us bow down before our King and God.

† Come, let us bow down before Christ, our King and God.

† Come, let us bow down and fall before the Lord Himself, Jesus Christ, our King and God.

*Psalm 5.*[^5]

Give ear unto my words, O Lord; understand my cry.

Attend to the voice of my supplication, my King and my God.

For unto Thee will I pray, O Lord; in the morning Thou wilt hear my voice.

In the morning I will stand before Thee and look up; for Thou art not a God Who desires iniquity.

Neither shall the wicked dwell with Thee, nor shall the transgressors abide before Thine eyes.

Thou hast hated all that work iniquity; Thou wilt destroy all that speak falsehood.

The Lord abhors the man of blood and deceit; but as for me, in the multitude of Thy mercy,

I will enter into Thy house; I will worship toward Thy holy temple in Thy fear.

O Lord, lead me in Thy righteousness; because of mine enemies make straight my way before Thee.

For there is no truth in their mouth; their heart is vain.

Their throat is an open sepulchre, with their tongues they have dealt deceitfully; judge them, O God.

Let them fall from their counsels; cast them out according to the multitude of their impieties, for they have provoked Thee, O Lord.

And let all that put their hope in Thee be glad; they shall rejoice unto the ages, and Thou shalt dwell in them.

And all that love Thy name shall boast in Thee; for Thou wilt bless the righteous.

O Lord, Thou hast crowned us as with a shield of good will.

*Psalm 44.*[^6]

My heart hath poured forth a good word; I speak of my works unto the King.

My tongue is the pen of a scribe writing swiftly.

Thou art beautiful in comeliness beyond the sons of men, grace is poured forth upon Thy lips; therefore God hath blessed Thee unto the ages.

Gird Thy sword upon Thy thigh, O Mighty One.

In Thy beauty and in Thy comeliness, bend Thy bow, proceed prosperously, and reign.

Because of truth, meekness, and righteousness; and Thy right hand shall guide Thee marvelously.

Thy weapons are sharpened, O Mighty One, the peoples shall fall under Thee, in the heart of the enemies of the King.

Thy throne, O God, is unto the age of the age; the sceptre of Thy kingdom is a sceptre of uprightness.

Thou hast loved righteousness and hated iniquity; therefore God, Thy God, hath anointed Thee with the oil of gladness beyond Thy fellows.

Myrrh, stacte, and cassia are from Thy garments, from the ivory palaces, whereby the daughters of kings have made Thee glad in Thine honor.

At Thy right hand stood the Queen, arrayed in vesture woven with gold, adorned in varied colors.

Hear, O daughter, and see, and incline thine ear; and forget thine own people and thy father's house.

And the King shall greatly desire thy beauty; for He is the Lord Thy God, and thou shalt worship Him.

And the daughter of Tyre shall come with gifts; the rich among the people shall entreat thy face.

All the glory of the daughter of the King is from within, arrayed in tassels of gold and adorned in varied colors.

The virgins in her train shall be brought unto the King; her companions shall be brought unto Thee.

They shall be brought with gladness and rejoicing; they shall be led into the temple of the King.

Instead of thy fathers, sons have been born unto thee; thou shalt make them princes over all the earth.

I will remember Thy name in every generation and generation.

Therefore shall the peoples give praise unto Thee unto the ages, and unto the age of the age.

*Psalm 45.*[^7]

God is our refuge and strength, a helper in afflictions that have come upon us exceedingly.

Therefore will we not fear when the earth is shaken, and the mountains are carried into the heart of the sea.

Their waters roared and were troubled; the mountains were shaken by His might.

The rush of the river gladdens the city of God; the Most High hath sanctified His dwelling place.

God is in the midst of her, she shall not be shaken; God shall help her at the break of dawn.

The nations were troubled, kingdoms wavered; the Most High gave forth His voice, the earth was shaken.

The Lord of hosts is with us; our protector is the God of Jacob.

Come and behold the works of God, what wonders He hath wrought upon the earth, taking away wars unto the ends of the earth.

He will break the bow and snap the spear, and the shields will He burn with fire.

Be still, and know that I am God; I will be exalted among the nations, I will be exalted upon the earth.

The Lord of hosts is with us, our protector is the God of Jacob.

Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Alleluia, alleluia, alleluia; glory to Thee, O God. *(Thrice)* Lord, have mercy. *(Thrice)*

*Glory: Troparion of the Forefeast, Tone 4:*

Mary was once registered with the elder Joseph in Bethlehem as being of the seed of David, bearing in her womb the seedless conception; and the time of the birth arrived, and there was no place in the inn; but as a fair palace, the cave was shown forth to the Queen. Christ is born, to raise up the image that fell of old.

*Both now: Theotokion:*

What shall we name thee, O full of grace? Heaven, for thou hast shone forth the Sun of Righteousness; Paradise, for thou hast blossomed forth the Flower of incorruption; Virgin, for thou didst remain incorrupt; pure Mother, for thou hast held in thy holy arms a Son, Who is the God of all: Him do thou entreat, that our souls may be saved.

*Then we chant these Troparia:*

*Tone 8:*

Bethlehem, make ready; let the manger be adorned; cave, receive; truth is come, the shadow hath passed away, and God hath appeared from the Virgin unto men, fashioned as we are, and deifying the flesh; therefore Adam is renewed with Eve, crying out: Good will hath appeared on earth, to save our race.

*Tone 6:*

*Verse:* God shall come from Theman, and the Holy One from the mountain overshadowed and densely wooded.[^8]

Now the prophetic utterance draws near to its fulfillment, which secretly said: And thou Bethlehem, land of Judah, shalt by no means be called the least among the rulers, preparing the cave; for out of thee unto Me shall come forth the Ruler of the nations in the flesh, from the Virgin Maiden, Christ God, Who shall shepherd His people, the new Israel: let us all give Him majesty.

*Verse:* Lord, I have heard Thy report, and was afraid; Lord, I considered Thy works, and was amazed.

*Now the prophetic utterance...*

*Then Glory: Tone 8:*

These things saith Joseph unto the Virgin: Mary, what is this deed that I see in thee? I am at a loss and wonder, and in mind I am dismayed; therefore depart from me in secret speedily. Mary, what is this deed that I see in thee? In place of honor, shame; in place of gladness, sorrow; instead of being praised, thou hast brought me reproach. I can no longer bear the reproaches of men; for from the priests of the temple of the Lord I received thee as blameless, and what is this that is seen?

*Both now:*

*These things saith Joseph unto the Virgin...*

*Then the Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*

The Lord said unto Me: Thou art My Son, this day have I begotten Thee.[^9]

*Verse:* Ask of Me, and I will give Thee the nations for Thine inheritance, and the uttermost parts of the earth for Thy possession.

*Then the Reading.*

*Reading from the Prophecy of Micah, Chapter 5:*[^10]

Thus saith the Lord: And thou Bethlehem, house of Ephratha, art thou too least among the thousands of Judah? For out of thee shall come forth unto Me a ruler, to be prince in Israel; and His goings forth have been from the beginning, from the days of eternity. Therefore shall He give them up, until the time that she that is in travail hath brought forth; and the rest of His brethren shall return unto the children of Israel. And He shall stand, and see, and shepherd His flock in the strength of the Lord; and they shall abide in the glory of the name of the Lord their God: for now shall He be magnified unto the ends of the earth.

*Reading from the Epistle of the Holy Apostle Paul to the Hebrews, Pericope 303:*[^11]

God, having of old spoken unto the fathers in the prophets in many portions and in many manners, hath in these last days spoken unto us in His Son, Whom He hath appointed heir of all things, through Whom also He made the worlds; Who being the brightness of His glory, and the express image of His person, and upholding all things by the word of His power, when He had by Himself made purification of our sins, sat down on the right hand of the Majesty on high; having become so much better than the angels, as He hath inherited a more excellent name than they. For unto which of the angels said He at any time: Thou art My Son, this day have I begotten Thee? And again: I will be to Him a Father, and He shall be to Me a Son? And again, when He brings the Firstborn into the world, He says: And let all the angels of God worship Him. And of the angels He says: Who makes His angels spirits, and His ministers a flame of fire. But unto the Son He says: Thy throne, O God, is unto the age of the age; a sceptre of uprightness is the sceptre of Thy kingdom.

Thou hast loved righteousness and hated iniquity; therefore God, Thy God, hath anointed Thee with the oil of gladness beyond Thy fellows. And: Thou, Lord, in the beginning hast laid the foundation of the earth, and the heavens are the works of Thy hands. They shall perish, but Thou remainest; and they all shall grow old as doth a garment; and as a vesture shalt Thou roll them up, and they shall be changed; but Thou art the same, and Thy years shall not fail.

*Reading from the Holy Gospel according to Matthew, Pericope 2:*[^12]

The birth of Jesus Christ was on this wise: When His mother Mary was betrothed to Joseph, before they came together, she was found with child of the Holy Spirit. Then Joseph her husband, being a just man, and not willing to expose her to public shame, was minded to put her away secretly. But while he thought on these things, behold, an angel of the Lord appeared unto him in a dream, saying: Joseph, son of David, fear not to take unto thee Mary thy wife; for that which is conceived in her is of the Holy Spirit. And she shall bring forth a Son, and thou shalt call His name Jesus; for He shall save His people from their sins. Now all this took place, that it might be fulfilled which was spoken of the Lord by the prophet, saying: Behold, a virgin shall be with child, and shall bring forth a Son, and they shall call His name Emmanuel, which being interpreted is, God with us. Then Joseph, being raised from sleep, did as the angel of the Lord had bidden him, and took unto him his wife; and knew her not until she had brought forth her firstborn Son; and he called His name Jesus.

*Then we say the Troparion:*

Order my steps according to Thy word, and let no iniquity have dominion over me. Deliver me from the false accusation of men, and I will keep Thy commandments. Make Thy face to shine upon Thy servant, and teach me Thine ordinances. Let my mouth be filled with Thy praise, O Lord, that I may hymn Thy glory, and Thy majesty all the day long.

*Then the Trisagion; and after the Our Father:*

*Kontakion, Tone 3:*

The Virgin this day comes to give birth ineffably in a cave unto the pre-eternal Word: rejoice, O inhabited earth, when thou hearest; glorify with the angels and the shepherds Him Who wills to appear, a young Child, the pre-eternal God.

*Then Lord, have mercy (40 times), and the prayer:*

Thou Who at all times and at every hour, in heaven and on earth, art worshipped and glorified, O good God, long-suffering, plenteous in mercy, most compassionate; Who lovest the righteous and hast mercy on sinners; Who callest all men to salvation through the promise of good things to come: do Thou Thyself, O Lord, receive also our supplications at this hour, and direct our life unto Thy commandments. Sanctify our souls, purify our bodies, set aright our thoughts, cleanse our minds, make us sober, and deliver us from every tribulation, evil, and sickness (and passions of the soul); encompass us with Thy holy angels, that guarded and guided by their host, we may attain unto the unity of the faith and unto the knowledge of Thine unapproachable glory; for blessed art Thou unto ages of ages. Amen.

Lord, have mercy. *(Thrice)*

Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.

More honorable than the Cherubim, and beyond compare more glorious than the Seraphim, who without corruption didst bear God the Word, very Mother of God, thee do we magnify.

In the name of the Lord, bless, father.

*The Priest exclaims:*

**Priest:** God be bountiful unto us, and bless us, and show the light of His countenance upon us, and have mercy on us.

*Then this prayer:*

O Christ, the true Light, Who enlightenest and sanctifiest every man that comes into the world: let the light of Thy countenance be marked upon us, that therein we may behold the unapproachable light; and direct our steps unto the fulfilling of Thy commandments, by the prayers of Thy most pure Mother and of all Thy saints. Amen.

*And the Dismissal.*[^13]

*Third Hour.*

† Come, let us worship: *(Thrice)*

*Psalm 66.*[^14]

God be bountiful unto us, and bless us, show the light of His countenance upon us, and have mercy on us.

That we may know Thy way upon earth, Thy salvation among all nations.

Let the peoples give praise unto Thee, O God; let all the peoples give praise unto Thee.

Let the nations rejoice and be glad, for Thou judgest the peoples with equity, and guidest the nations upon earth.

Let the peoples give praise unto Thee, O God, let all the peoples give praise unto Thee: the earth hath yielded her increase.

God, our God, bless us; God bless us; and let all the ends of the earth fear Him.

*Psalm 86.*[^15]

His foundations are in the holy mountains: the Lord loves the gates of Sion more than all the dwellings of Jacob.

Glorious things have been spoken of thee, O city of God.

I will make mention of Rahab and Babylon unto them that know me.

And behold, aliens, and Tyre, and the people of the Ethiopians, these were born there.

Mother Sion shall say: A man, and a man was born in her; and the Most High Himself hath established her.

The Lord shall reckon in the registers of the peoples, and of the princes: of these that were born in her.

As of all them that rejoice, so is the dwelling in thee.

*Psalm 50.*[^16]

Have mercy on me, O God, according to Thy great mercy.

And according to the multitude of Thy compassions, blot out my transgression.

Wash me thoroughly from mine iniquity, and cleanse me from my sin.

For I know mine iniquity, and my sin is ever before me.

Against Thee only have I sinned, and done evil before Thee, that Thou mightest be justified in Thy words, and conquer when Thou art judged.

For behold, I was conceived in iniquities, and in sins did my mother bear me.

For behold, Thou hast loved truth; the hidden and secret things of Thy wisdom hast Thou revealed unto me.

Thou shalt sprinkle me with hyssop, and I shall be cleansed; Thou shalt wash me, and I shall be made whiter than snow.

Thou shalt make me hear joy and gladness; the bones that have been humbled shall rejoice.

Turn away Thy face from my sins, and blot out all mine iniquities.

Create in me a clean heart, O God, and renew a right spirit within me.

Cast me not away from Thy presence, and take not Thy Holy Spirit from me.

Restore unto me the joy of Thy salvation, and uphold me with a guiding Spirit.

I will teach the lawless Thy ways, and the ungodly shall turn back unto Thee.

Deliver me from bloodguiltiness, O God, Thou God of my salvation; and my tongue shall rejoice in Thy righteousness.

O Lord, Thou shalt open my lips, and my mouth shall show forth Thy praise.

For if Thou hadst desired sacrifice, I would have given it; Thou wilt not delight in whole-burnt-offerings.

A sacrifice unto God is a broken spirit; a broken and a contrite heart God will not despise.

Do good, O Lord, in Thy good pleasure unto Sion, and let the walls of Jerusalem be built.

Then shalt Thou be pleased with a sacrifice of righteousness, oblation and whole-burnt-offerings; then shall they offer bullocks upon Thine altar.

Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Alleluia, alleluia, alleluia; glory to Thee, O God. *(Thrice)* Lord, have mercy. *(Thrice)*

*Glory: Troparion, Tone 6:*

Mary was once registered with the elder Joseph in Bethlehem as being of the seed of David, bearing in her womb the seedless conception; and the time of the birth arrived, and there was no place in the inn; but as a fair palace, the cave was shown forth to the Queen. Christ is born, to raise up the image that fell of old.

*Both now: Theotokion:*

O Mother of God, thou art the true vine that didst bud forth for us the Fruit of life: thee do we entreat, pray thou, O Sovereign Lady, with the holy Apostles, that our souls may be saved.

*Then we chant these Troparia:*

*Tone 6:*

This is our God, no other shall be compared unto Him, Who was born of the Virgin and lived among men; in a humble manger the Only-Begotten Son is seen lying as a mortal man, and swaddled in swaddling clothes is the Lord of glory, and a star proclaims Him unto the Magi for His worship; and we sing: O Holy Trinity, save our souls.

*Tone 8:*

*Verse:* God shall come from Theman, and the Holy One from the mountain overshadowed and densely wooded.[^17]

Before Thy birth, O Lord, beholding Thy mystery with trembling, the spiritual hosts were astonished; for Thou Who didst adorn the heavens with stars wast well pleased to be born as an infant, and Thou Who holdest the ends of all the earth in Thy palm liest in a manger of brute beasts; for by such a dispensation Thy tender mercy hath been made known, O Christ: great is Thy mercy, glory to Thee.

*Verse:* Lord, I have heard Thy report, and was afraid; Lord, I considered Thy works, and was amazed.

*And again we chant the same Troparion.*

*Glory: Tone 2.*

Tell us, Joseph, how is it that the Virgin whom thou didst receive from the Holy of Holies thou bringest with child to Bethlehem? I, saith he, have searched the prophets, and having received word from an angel, I am persuaded that Mary shall ineffably give birth to God; unto Whom the Magi from the East shall come to worship, serving Him with precious gifts: O Thou Who wast incarnate for our sake, Lord, glory to Thee.

*Both now: again the same Sticheron.*

*Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*

Unto us a Child is born, a Son, and unto us He is given.[^18]

*Verse:* Whose government was upon His shoulder.

*Reading from the Prophecy of Jeremiah, Chapter 3:*[^19]

This is our God, no other shall be compared unto Him. He hath found out every way of knowledge, and hath given it unto Jacob His servant, and unto Israel His beloved. Afterward upon earth...

He appeared, and lived among men. This is the book of the commandments of God, and the law that endures unto the ages: all they that hold fast to it are appointed unto life, but they that forsake it shall die. Turn thee, O Jacob, and lay hold upon it: walk toward the brightness over against the light thereof. Give not thine honor unto another, nor the things that are profitable for thee unto a foreign nation. Blessed are we, O Israel, for the things that are pleasing unto God are made known unto us.

*Reading from the Epistle of the Holy Apostle Paul to the Galatians, Pericope 208:*[^20]

Brethren, before faith came, we were kept under the law, shut up unto the faith which should afterwards be revealed. Therefore the law was our schoolmaster unto Christ, that we might be justified by faith. But after that faith is come, we are no longer under a schoolmaster. For ye are all the children of God through faith in Christ Jesus. For as many of you as have been baptized into Christ have put on Christ. There is neither Jew nor Greek, there is neither slave nor free, there is neither male nor female: for ye are all one in Christ Jesus. And if ye be Christ's, then are ye Abraham's seed, and heirs according to the promise.

*Reading from the Holy Gospel according to Luke, Pericope 5:*[^21]

In those days, there went out a decree from Caesar Augustus, that all the world should be registered. This first registration was made when Cyrenius was governing Syria. And all went to be registered, every one into his own city. And Joseph also went up from Galilee, out of the city of Nazareth, into Judea, unto the city of David, which is called Bethlehem, because he was of the house and lineage of David; to be registered...

...with Mary his espoused wife, being great with child. And so it was, that while they were there, the days were accomplished that she should be delivered. And she brought forth her firstborn Son, and wrapped Him in swaddling clothes, and laid Him in a manger, because there was no room for them in the inn. And there were shepherds in the same country abiding in the field, keeping watch by night over their flock. And lo, the angel of the Lord stood by them, and the glory of the Lord shone round about them, and they were sore afraid. And the angel said unto them: Fear not; for behold, I bring you good tidings of great joy, which shall be to all people: for unto you is born this day a Savior, Who is Christ the Lord, in the city of David. And this shall be a sign unto you: Ye shall find the Babe wrapped in swaddling clothes, lying in a manger. And suddenly there was with the angel a multitude of the heavenly host praising God, and saying: Glory to God in the highest, and on earth peace, good will toward men. And it came to pass, as the angels were gone away from them into heaven, the shepherd men said one to another: Let us go even unto Bethlehem, and see this thing which has come to pass, which the Lord has made known unto us. And they came with haste, and found both Mary and Joseph, and the Babe lying in a manger. And when they had seen it, they made known abroad the saying which was told them concerning this Child. And all they that heard it wondered at those things which were told them by the shepherds. But Mary kept all these sayings, pondering them in her heart. And the shepherds returned, glorifying and praising God for all the things that they had heard and seen, as it was told unto them.

*Then we say:*

The Lord God is blessed; blessed is the Lord day by day; the God of our salvation shall make our way prosperous; our God is the God of salvation.[^22]

*Then the Trisagion, and after the Our Father:*

*Kontakion, Tone 3:*

Today the Virgin comes to the cave to give birth ineffably to the Pre-eternal Word: dance, O inhabited earth, when thou hearest; glorify with the angels and the shepherds Him Who wills to appear as a young Child, the Pre-eternal God.

Lord, have mercy. *(40 times)*

*Prayer: Thou Who at all times and at every hour...*

Lord, have mercy. *(Thrice)*

Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.

More honorable than the Cherubim, and beyond compare more glorious than the Seraphim, who without corruption didst bear God the Word, very Mother of God, thee do we magnify.

In the name of the Lord, bless, father.

**Priest:** Through the prayers of our holy fathers, Lord Jesus Christ our God, have mercy on us.

*Then the Prayer:*

O Master, God, Father Almighty, O Lord, the Only-Begotten Son, Jesus Christ, and Holy Spirit: one Godhead, one Power, have mercy on me, a sinner; and by the judgments which Thou knowest, save me, Thine unworthy servant: for Thou art blessed unto ages of ages. Amen.

*And the Dismissal.*[^23]

*Sixth Hour.*

† Come, let us worship: *(Thrice)*

*Psalm 71.*[^24]

Give the king Thy judgment, O God, and Thy righteousness unto the king's son:
To judge Thy people with righteousness, and Thy poor with judgment.

Let the mountains receive peace for the people, and the hills righteousness.
He shall judge the poor of the people, and shall save the children of the needy, and shall humble the false accuser.
And He shall continue as long as the sun, and before the moon, from generation to generation.
He shall come down like rain upon a fleece, and like drops falling upon the earth.
In His days shall righteousness flourish, and abundance of peace, until the moon be removed.
And He shall have dominion from sea to sea, and from the rivers unto the ends of the earth.
The Ethiopians shall fall down before Him, and His enemies shall lick the dust.
The kings of Tharsis and the islands shall bring presents; the kings of Arabia and Saba shall offer gifts.
And all the kings of the earth shall worship Him; all nations shall serve Him.
For He has delivered the needy from the mighty, and the poor that had no helper.
He shall spare the poor and the needy, and shall save the souls of the poor.
He shall redeem their souls from usury and injustice, and precious shall His name be in their sight.
And He shall live, and unto Him shall be given of the gold of Arabia; and they shall pray for Him continually, all the day long shall they bless Him.
There shall be a support upon the earth on the tops of the mountains; His fruit shall be exalted above Lebanon, and they of the city shall blossom like the grass of the earth.

His name shall be blessed unto the ages; before the sun His name abides.
And in Him shall all the tribes of the earth be blessed; all nations shall call Him blessed.
Blessed be the Lord God of Israel, Who alone works marvels.
And blessed be the name of His glory unto the age, and unto the age of the age; and all the earth shall be filled with His glory: so be it, so be it.

*Psalm 131.*[^25]

Remember David, O Lord, and all his meekness.
How he swore unto the Lord, and made a vow unto the God of Jacob:
I will not enter into the tabernacle of my house, I will not go up upon the bed of my couch;
I will not give sleep to mine eyes, nor slumber to mine eyelids,
Nor rest to my temples, until I find out a place for the Lord, a tabernacle for the God of Jacob.
Lo, we heard of it in Ephratha, we found it in the fields of the wood.
We will enter into His tabernacles, we will worship at the place where His feet have stood.
Arise, O Lord, into Thy rest, Thou and the ark of Thy holiness.
Thy priests shall be clothed with righteousness, and Thy righteous ones shall rejoice with joy.
For Thy servant David's sake, turn not away the face of Thine anointed.
The Lord has sworn in truth unto David, and He will not annul it: Of the fruit of thy loins will I set upon thy throne.

If thy sons keep My covenant and these My testimonies which I shall teach them,
Their sons also shall sit upon thy throne unto the age.
For the Lord has chosen Sion, He has desired it for a habitation for Himself.
This is My rest unto the age of the age; here will I dwell, for I have chosen it.
Blessing I will bless her provision; I will satisfy her poor with bread.
Her priests will I clothe with salvation, and her righteous ones shall rejoice with joy.
There will I make the horn of David to bud; I have prepared a lamp for Mine anointed.
His enemies will I clothe with shame, but upon him shall My sanctification flourish.

*Psalm 90.*[^26]

He that dwells in the help of the Most High shall abide in the shelter of the God of heaven.
He shall say unto the Lord: Thou art my protector and my refuge; my God, and in Him will I trust.
For He shall deliver thee from the snare of the hunters, and from every troubling word.
With His pinions shall He overshadow thee, and under His wings shalt thou hope.
With a shield shall His truth encompass thee; thou shalt not be afraid for the terror by night,
Nor for the arrow that flies by day, nor for the thing that walks in darkness, nor for mishap and the noonday demon.
A thousand shall fall at thy side, and ten thousand at thy right hand, but unto thee it shall not come nigh.

Only with thine eyes shalt thou behold, and see the reward of sinners.
For Thou, O Lord, art my hope; Thou hast made the Most High thy refuge.
No evils shall come unto thee, and no scourge shall draw nigh unto thy dwelling.
For He shall give His angels charge concerning thee, to keep thee in all thy ways.
In their hands shall they bear thee up, lest at any time thou dash thy foot against a stone.
Thou shalt tread upon the asp and basilisk, and the lion and dragon shalt thou trample under foot.
Because he has set his hope on Me, I will deliver him; I will shelter him, because he has known My name.
He shall cry unto Me, and I will give ear unto him; I am with him in affliction; I will deliver him, and glorify him.
With length of days will I satisfy him, and show him My salvation.

Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Alleluia, alleluia, alleluia; glory to Thee, O God. *(Thrice)* Lord, have mercy. *(Thrice)*

*Glory: Troparion, Tone 4:*

Mary was once registered with the elder Joseph in Bethlehem as being of the seed of David, bearing in her womb the seedless conception; and the time of the birth arrived, and there was no place in the inn; but as a fair palace, the cave was shown forth to the Queen. Christ is born, to raise up the image that fell of old.

*Both now: Theotokion:*

Since we have no boldness on account of our manifold sins, do thou entreat Him Who was born of thee...

...O Virgin Mother of God: for the supplication of a Mother avails much unto the loving-kindness of the Master. Despise not the petitions of sinners, O all-pure one, for He is merciful, and able to save, Who also was pleased to suffer for our sake.

*Then we chant these Troparia:*

*Tone 1:*

Come, ye faithful, let us be lifted up divinely, and let us behold the divine condescension from on high made manifest unto us in Bethlehem; and being cleansed in mind, let us bring virtues instead of myrrh, faithfully preparing the entries of the Nativity, crying out from the treasures of our souls: Glory in the highest to God existing in Trinity, through Whom good will has appeared among men, delivering Adam from the primal curse, as the Lover of mankind.

*Tone 4:*

*Verse:* God shall come from Theman, and the Holy One from the mountain overshadowed and densely wooded.[^27]

Hear, O heaven, and give ear, O earth; let the foundations be shaken, let trembling lay hold on the depths of the nether regions: for God and Creator has clothed Himself in the bodily frame; and He Who created creation with a mighty hand is seen in the womb as a created being. O the depth of the riches both of the wisdom and the knowledge of God! How unsearchable are His judgments, and His ways untraceable!

*Verse:* Lord, I have heard Thy report, and was afraid; Lord, I considered Thy works, and was amazed.

*And again we chant the same Troparion.*

*Glory: Tone 5:*

Come, O Christ-bearing people, let us behold a wonder that astonishes every understanding and holds it fast...

...and praising piously, ye faithful, let us worship: today the Virgin, being great with child, comes to Bethlehem to give birth to the Lord; and the choirs of angels run before Her; and beholding these things, Joseph the Betrothed cried out: What is this strange mystery in thee, O Virgin? And how art thou about to give birth, O Heifer that hast not known a mate?

*Both now: we chant again the same.*

*Then the Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*

Out of the womb before the morning star have I begotten Thee; the Lord has sworn and will not repent.[^28]

*Then the Paremia.*

*Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah, Chapter 7:*[^29]

The Lord spoke again unto Ahaz, saying: Ask thee a sign of the Lord thy God, in the depth, or in the height above. And Ahaz said: I will not ask, neither will I tempt the Lord. And Isaiah said: Hear ye now, O house of David: Is it a small thing for you to weary men, but will ye weary my God also? Therefore the Lord Himself shall give you a sign: Behold, a Virgin shall conceive in her womb, and shall bring forth a Son, and they shall call His name Emmanuel. Butter and honey shall He eat, before He knows or chooses the evil, He shall choose the good: for before the Child shall know good or evil, He shall refuse the evil, and choose the good. And the Lord said unto me: Take thee a great new scroll, and write in it with the pen of a man: To make speed to plunder the spoils, for they are at hand. And make Me faithful men to witness, Uriah the priest, and Zechariah the son of Jeberechiah. And I approached...

...unto the prophetess; and she conceived, and bore a son. Then said the Lord to me: Call his name, Spoil quickly, plunder swiftly. For before the child shall know how to call his father or his mother, the power of Damascus and the spoils of Samaria shall be taken away before the king of Assyria. God is with us: understand, ye nations, and submit yourselves; give ear, even unto the ends of the earth; submit yourselves, ye mighty: for if ye again wax strong, again shall ye be conquered. And whatsoever counsel ye take, the Lord shall bring it to nought; and whatsoever word ye speak, it shall not abide in you, for God is with us.

*Reading from the Epistle of the Holy Apostle Paul to the Hebrews, Pericope 304:*[^30]

In the beginning, Thou, Lord, hast laid the foundation of the earth, and the heavens are the works of Thy hands. They shall perish, but Thou remainest; and they all shall grow old as does a garment; and as a vesture shalt Thou roll them up, and they shall be changed; but Thou art the same, and Thy years shall not fail. But unto which of the angels said He at any time: Sit on My right hand, until I make Thine enemies the footstool of Thy feet? Are they not all ministering spirits, sent forth to minister for them who shall be heirs of salvation? Therefore we ought to give the more earnest heed to the things which we have heard, lest at any time we should fall away. For if the word spoken by angels was steadfast, and every transgression and disobedience received a just recompense of reward: how shall we escape, if we neglect so great salvation; which at the first began to be spoken by the Lord, and was confirmed unto us by them that heard Him?

*Reading from the Holy Gospel according to Matthew, Pericope 3:*[^31]

When Jesus was born in Bethlehem of Judea in the days of Herod the king, behold, there came Magi from the East to Jerusalem, saying: Where is He that is born King of the Jews? For we have seen His star in the East, and are come to worship Him. When Herod the king had heard these things, he was troubled, and all Jerusalem with him. And when he had gathered all the chief priests and scribes of the people together, he inquired of them where Christ should be born. And they said unto him: In Bethlehem of Judea; for thus it is written by the prophet: And thou Bethlehem, in the land of Judah, art not the least among the rulers of Judah; for out of thee shall come a Leader, Who shall shepherd My people Israel. Then Herod, when he had privily called the Magi, inquired of them diligently what time the star appeared. And he sent them to Bethlehem, and said: Go and search diligently for the young Child; and when ye have found Him, bring me word again, that I may come and worship Him also. When they had heard the king, they departed; and lo, the star, which they saw in the East, went before them, till it came and stood over where the young Child was. When they saw the star, they rejoiced with exceeding great joy. And when they were come into the house, they saw the young Child with Mary His mother, and fell down, and worshiped Him; and when they had opened their treasures, they presented unto Him gifts: gold, and frankincense, and myrrh. And being warned in a dream that they should not return to Herod, they departed into their own country another way.

*Then we say the Troparion:*

Let Thy compassions quickly go before us, O Lord, for we are brought very low; help us, O God our Savior; for the glory of Thy name, O Lord, deliver us, and purge away our sins, for Thy name's sake.[^32]

*Then the Trisagion, and after the Our Father:*

*Kontakion, Tone 3:*

Today the Virgin comes to the cave to give birth ineffably to the Pre-eternal Word: dance, O inhabited earth, when thou hearest; glorify with the angels and the shepherds Him Who wills to appear as a young Child, the Pre-eternal God.

Lord, have mercy. *(40 times)*

*Prayer: Thou Who at all times and at every hour...*

Lord, have mercy. *(Thrice)*

Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.

More honorable than the Cherubim, and beyond compare more glorious than the Seraphim, who without corruption didst bear God the Word, very Mother of God, thee do we magnify.

In the name of the Lord, bless, father.

**Priest:** Through the prayers of our holy fathers, Lord Jesus Christ our God, have mercy on us.

*Then the Prayer of Saint Basil the Great:*[^33]

O God and Lord of hosts, and Maker of all creation, Who through the compassion of Thine incomparable mercy didst send down Thine Only-Begotten Son, our Lord Jesus Christ, for the salvation of our race, and by His precious Cross didst tear up the handwriting of our sins, and thereby didst triumph over the principalities and powers of darkness: do Thou Thyself, O Master, Lover of mankind, accept also these thanksgiving and suppliant prayers of us sinners, and deliver us from every all-destroying and dark transgression, and from all enemies, visible and invisible, that seek to do us harm; nail our flesh to Thy fear, and incline not our hearts to words or...

...thoughts of evil, but wound our souls with Thy love, that always gazing upon Thee, and guided by the light that comes from Thee, beholding Thee, the unapproachable and everlasting Light, we may send up unceasing confession and thanksgiving unto Thee, the Father without beginning, with Thine Only-Begotten Son, and Thine All-Holy, Good, and Life-Creating Spirit, both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.

*And the Dismissal.*[^34]

*Ninth Hour.*

† Come, let us worship: *(Thrice)*

*Psalm 109.*[^35]

The Lord said unto my Lord: Sit Thou at My right hand,
Until I make Thine enemies the footstool of Thy feet.
The Lord shall send Thee the rod of power out of Zion, and rule Thou in the midst of Thine enemies.
With Thee is dominion in the day of Thy power, in the brightness of Thy saints: from the womb before the morning star have I begotten Thee.
The Lord has sworn, and will not repent: Thou art a Priest forever, according to the order of Melchizedek.
The Lord at Thy right hand has crushed kings in the day of His wrath.
He shall judge among the nations, He shall fill them with ruins, He shall crush the heads over the earth of many.
He shall drink of the brook in the way, therefore shall He lift up the head.

*Psalm 110.*[^36]

I will confess Thee, O Lord, with my whole heart, in the council of the upright, and in the congregation.
Great are the works of the Lord, sought out in all His desires.
His work is confession and majesty, and His righteousness endures unto ages of ages.
He has made a memorial of His wonders; the Lord is merciful and compassionate: He has given food unto them that fear Him.
He will ever be mindful of His covenant: He will declare unto His people the power of His works,
To give them the heritage of the nations: the works of His hands are truth and judgment.
Faithful are all His commandments, established unto ages of ages, done in truth and uprightness.
He sent redemption unto His people: He commanded His covenant forever.
Holy and awesome is His name: the fear of the Lord is the beginning of wisdom.
A good understanding have all they that do it: His praise endures unto ages of ages.

*Psalm 85.*[^37]

Bow down Thine ear, O Lord, and hear me, for I am poor and needy.
Preserve my soul, for I am holy: save Thy servant, O my God, that hopes in Thee.
Have mercy on me, O Lord, for unto Thee will I cry all the day; rejoice the soul of Thy servant, for unto Thee have I lifted up my soul.

For Thou, Lord, art good and clement, and plenteous in mercy unto all them that call upon Thee.
Give ear, O Lord, unto my prayer, and attend to the voice of my supplication.
In the day of my trouble I called upon Thee, for Thou hast heard me.
There is none like unto Thee among the gods, O Lord, neither are there any works like unto Thy works.
All nations whom Thou hast made shall come and worship before Thee, O Lord, and shall glorify Thy name.
For Thou art great, and workest wonders; Thou art God alone.
Guide me, O Lord, in Thy way, and I will walk in Thy truth; let my heart rejoice, that it may fear Thy name.
I will confess Thee, O Lord my God, with my whole heart, and I will glorify Thy name forever.
For great is Thy mercy toward me, and Thou hast delivered my soul from the lowest netherworld.
O God, the lawless rose up against me, and the assembly of the mighty sought after my soul, and set Thee not before them.
And Thou, O Lord my God, art compassionate and merciful, long-suffering, plenteous in mercy, and true.
Look upon me, and have mercy on me; give Thy strength unto Thy servant, and save the son of Thine handmaid.
Work with me a token for good, and let them that hate me see it and be put to shame...

...because Thou, Lord, hast helped me, and comforted me.

Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen. Alleluia, alleluia, alleluia; glory to Thee, O God. *(Thrice)* Lord, have mercy. *(Thrice)*

*Glory: Troparion, Tone 4:*

Mary was once registered with the elder Joseph in Bethlehem as being of the seed of David, bearing in her womb the seedless conception; and the time of the birth arrived, and there was no place in the inn; but as a fair palace, the cave was shown forth to the Queen. Christ is born, to raise up the image that fell of old.

*Both now: Theotokion:*

Thou Who for our sake wast born of the Virgin, and didst endure crucifixion, O Good One, Who by death didst overthrow death, and manifested the Resurrection as God: despise not those whom Thou hast fashioned with Thine hand; show forth Thy love for mankind, O Merciful One; accept the Mother of God who bore Thee, who intercedes for us; and save, O our Savior, a despairing people.

*Then these Troparia, Tone 7:*

Herod was astonished, beholding the piety of the Magi; and overcome with wrath, he searched out the span of years: mothers were made childless, and the untimely age of infants was bitterly reaped, breasts dried up, and streams of milk were held back; great was the woe! Therefore, ye faithful, having gathered together piously, let us worship the Nativity of Christ.

*Verse:* God shall come from Theman, and the Holy One from the mountain overshadowed and densely wooded.[^38]

*Troparion, Tone 2:*

When Joseph was wounded with sorrow, O Virgin, going to Bethlehem, thou didst cry out unto him: Why art thou downcast and troubled, seeing me with child, knowing not at all the awesome mystery that is in me? Henceforth cast aside all fear, recognizing what is most glorious: for God descends to earth for mercy's sake, in my womb now, having taken flesh; Whom being born thou shalt behold, even as He was well pleased, and filled with joy thou shalt worship Him as thy Maker, Whom the angels unceasingly praise and glorify with the Father and the Holy Spirit.

*Verse:* Lord, I have heard Thy report, and was afraid; Lord, I considered Thy works, and was amazed.

*And again we chant the same Troparion.*

*Glory: Tone 6:*

Today is born of the Virgin He Who holds all creation in His hand; He is wrapped in swaddling clothes like a mortal, Who in His essence is intangible God; He lies in a manger Who established the heavens by His word in the beginning; He is nourished with milk from the breasts, Who rained down manna upon the people in the wilderness; the Bridegroom of the Church calls the Magi, the Son of the Virgin receives their gifts: We worship Thy Nativity, O Christ; we worship Thy Nativity, O Christ; we worship Thy Nativity, O Christ: show us also Thy divine Theophanies.

*Both now: we chant again the same Troparion.*

*Then the Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*

A man shall say: Zion is my mother; and a man was born in her.[^39]

*Verse:* His foundations are in the holy mountains; the Lord loves the gates of Zion.

*Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah, Chapter 9:*[^40]

A Child is born unto us, a Son, and He is given unto us, Whose government is upon His shoulder; and His name is called the Angel of Great Counsel, Marvelous Counselor, Mighty God, Potentate, Prince of Peace, Father of the age to come. For He shall bring peace upon the rulers, and His health. And great is His government, and of His peace there is no end, upon the throne of David and upon His kingdom, to order it and to uphold it with judgment and with righteousness, from henceforth even forever. The zeal of the Lord of Sabaoth shall perform this.

*Reading from the Epistle of the Holy Apostle Paul to the Hebrews, Pericope 306:*[^41]

Brethren, He that sanctifies and they who are sanctified are all of one: for which cause He is not ashamed to call them brethren, saying: I will declare Thy name unto My brethren, in the midst of the Church will I sing praise unto Thee. And again: I will put My trust in Him; and again: Behold I and the children which God has given Me. Forasmuch then as the children are partakers of flesh and blood, He also Himself likewise took part of the same; that through death He might destroy him that had the power of death, that is, the devil; and deliver them who through fear of death were all their lifetime subject to bondage. For truly He takes not hold of angels, but of the seed of Abraham He takes hold. Therefore in all things it behooved Him to be made like unto His...

...brethren, that He might be a merciful and faithful High Priest in things pertaining to God, to make reconciliation for the sins of the people. For in that He Himself has suffered, being tempted, He is able to succor them that are tempted.

*Reading from the Holy Gospel according to Matthew, Pericope 4:*[^42]

When the Magi had departed, behold, the angel of the Lord appeared to Joseph in a dream, saying: Arise, and take the young Child and His mother, and flee into Egypt, and be thou there until I tell thee; for Herod will seek the young Child to destroy Him. And he arose, and took the young Child and His mother by night, and departed into Egypt; and was there until the death of Herod: that it might be fulfilled which was spoken of the Lord by the prophet, saying: Out of Egypt have I called My Son. Then Herod, when he saw that he was mocked of the Magi, was exceedingly wroth, and sent forth, and slew all the children that were in Bethlehem, and in all the borders thereof, from two years old and under, according to the time which he had diligently inquired of the Magi. Then was fulfilled that which was spoken by Jeremiah the prophet, saying: A voice was heard in Ramah, lamentation, and weeping, and great mourning; Rachel weeping for her children, and would not be comforted, because they are not. But when Herod was dead, behold, an angel of the Lord appeared in a dream to Joseph in Egypt, saying: Arise, and take the young Child and His mother, and go into the land of Israel: for they are dead who sought the young Child's life. And he arose, and took the young Child and His mother, and came into the land of Israel. But when he heard that Archelaus was reigning in Judea in the room of his father Herod, he was afraid to go thither; and being warned...

...in a dream, he turned aside into the parts of Galilee; and he came and dwelt in a city called Nazareth, that it might be fulfilled which was spoken by the prophets: He shall be called a Nazarene.

*Then we say the Troparion:*

Deliver us not up utterly, for Thy name's sake, neither break Thou Thy covenant; and cause not Thy mercy to depart from us, for the sake of Abraham Thy beloved, and for the sake of Isaac Thy servant, and Israel Thy holy one.[^43]

*Then the Trisagion, and after the Our Father:*

*Kontakion, Tone 3:*

Today the Virgin comes to the cave to give birth ineffably to the Pre-eternal Word: dance, O inhabited earth, when thou hearest; glorify with the angels and the shepherds Him Who wills to appear as a young Child, the Pre-eternal God.

Lord, have mercy. *(40 times)*

*Prayer: Thou Who at all times and at every hour...*

Lord, have mercy. *(Thrice)*

Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.

More honorable than the Cherubim, and beyond compare more glorious than the Seraphim, who without corruption didst bear God the Word, very Mother of God, thee do we magnify.

In the name of the Lord, bless, father.

**Priest:** Through the prayers of our holy fathers, Lord Jesus Christ our God, have mercy on us.

*Then the Prayer of Saint Basil the Great:*[^44]

O Master, Lord Jesus Christ our God, Who art long-suffering over our transgressions, and hast brought us even unto this present hour, in which, hanging upon the life-creating Wood, Thou didst open the entrance into Paradise to the wise thief, and by death didst destroy death: cleanse us sinners and Thine unworthy servants; for we have sinned and committed iniquity, and we are not worthy to lift up our eyes and look upon the height of heaven, because we have forsaken...

...the path of Thy righteousness, and have walked in the desires of our hearts. But we implore Thine immeasurable goodness: spare us, O Lord, according to the multitude of Thy mercy, and save us for Thy holy name's sake, for our days have vanished in vanity. Pluck us from the hand of the adversary, forgive us our sins, and put to death our carnal desires, that, having put off the old man, we may put on the new, and live unto Thee, our Master and Benefactor; and thus, following Thy commandments, we may attain unto eternal rest, where is the dwelling of all that rejoice. For Thou truly art the true joy and exultation of those who love Thee, O Christ our God, and unto Thee do we send up glory, together with Thine unoriginate Father, and Thy most holy and good and life-creating Spirit, now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.

*Then Psalm 102:*[^45]

Bless the Lord, O my soul, and all that is within me, bless His holy name.
Bless the Lord, O my soul, and forget not all His benefits:
Who forgives all thine iniquities, Who heals all thine infirmities;
Who redeems thy life from corruption, Who crowns thee with mercy and compassion;
Who satisfies thy desire with good things; thy youth shall be renewed like the eagle's.
The Lord performs deeds of mercy, and brings judgment for all that are oppressed.

He made known His ways unto Moses, His will unto the sons of Israel.
Compassionate and merciful is the Lord, long-suffering and of great mercy.
He will not always be angry, neither will He harbor indignation forever.
He has not dealt with us according to our iniquities, nor rewarded us according to our sins.
For according to the height of heaven above the earth, the Lord has established His mercy over those who fear Him.
As far as the east is from the west, so far has He removed our iniquities from us.
Like as a father pities his children, so the Lord has pitied those who fear Him; for He knows our frame:
He remembers that we are dust. As for man, his days are as grass; as a flower of the field, so shall he flourish.
For the wind passes over it, and it is gone; and its place shall know it no more.
But the mercy of the Lord is from everlasting unto everlasting upon those who fear Him,
And His righteousness upon children's children, unto such as keep His covenant,
And remember His commandments to do them.
The Lord has prepared His throne in the heavens, and His kingdom rules over all.
Bless the Lord, all ye His angels, mighty in strength, who perform His word, to give ear unto the voice of His words.

Bless the Lord, all ye His hosts, ye ministers of His who do His will.
Bless the Lord, all ye His works, in every place of His dominion; bless the Lord, O my soul.
In every place of His dominion, bless the Lord, O my soul.

Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit.

*Psalm 145:*[^46]

Praise the Lord, O my soul; I will praise the Lord throughout my life; I will sing praises unto my God as long as I have being.
Put not your trust in princes, nor in the sons of men, in whom there is no salvation.
His spirit shall go forth, and he shall return to his earth; in that very day all his thoughts shall perish.
Blessed is he whose helper is the God of Jacob, whose hope is in the Lord His God,
Who made heaven and earth, the sea, and all that is therein;
Who keeps truth forever, Who executes judgment for the oppressed, Who gives food to the hungry.
The Lord looses those who are bound; the Lord gives wisdom to the blind.
The Lord raises up those who are bowed down; the Lord loves the righteous.
The Lord preserves the sojourners; He shall relieve the fatherless and the widow, and the way of sinners He shall destroy.
The Lord shall reign forever, thy God, O Zion, unto generation and generation.

Both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.

Only-begotten Son and Word of God, Who art immortal, and didst deign for our salvation to become incarnate of the Holy Mother of God and Ever-Virgin Mary, and without change didst become man; and wast crucified, O Christ God, trampling down death by death; Who art One of the Holy Trinity, glorified together with the Father and the Holy Spirit: save us.

*Then we say:*[^47]

In Thy kingdom, when Thou comest, remember us, O Lord.
Blessed are the poor in spirit, for theirs is the kingdom of heaven.
Blessed are they that mourn, for they shall be comforted.
Blessed are the meek, for they shall inherit the earth.
Blessed are they that hunger and thirst after righteousness, for they shall be filled.
Blessed are the merciful, for they shall obtain mercy.
Blessed are the pure in heart, for they shall see God.
Blessed are the peacemakers, for they shall be called sons of God.
Blessed are they that are persecuted for righteousness' sake, for theirs is the kingdom of heaven.
Blessed are ye when they shall revile you, and persecute you, and say all manner of evil against you falsely, for My sake.
Rejoice and be glad, for great is your reward in the heavens.

Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.

Remember us, O Lord, when Thou comest into Thy kingdom.
Remember us, O Master, when Thou comest into Thy kingdom.
Remember us, O Holy One, when Thou comest into Thy kingdom.
The heavenly choir praises Thee and says: Holy, holy, holy, Lord Sabaoth; heaven and earth are full of Thy glory.
*Verse:* Come unto Him and be enlightened, and your faces shall not be ashamed.[^48]
The heavenly choir praises Thee and says: Holy, holy, holy, Lord Sabaoth; heaven and earth are full of Thy glory.

Glory:
The assembly of the holy angels and archangels, with all the heavenly hosts, praises Thee and says: Holy, holy, holy, Lord Sabaoth; heaven and earth are full of Thy glory.

Both now:
I believe in one God, the Father Almighty, Maker of heaven and earth, and of all things visible and invisible;
And in one Lord Jesus Christ, the Son of God, the Only-begotten, begotten of the Father before all ages;
Light of Light, true God of true God, begotten, not made, of one essence with the Father, by Whom all things were made;
Who for us men and for our salvation came down from heaven, and was incarnate...

...of the Holy Spirit and the Virgin Mary, and became man;
And was crucified also for us under Pontius Pilate, and suffered, and was buried;
And arose again on the third day according to the Scriptures;
And ascended into heaven, and sits at the right hand of the Father;
And He shall come again with glory to judge the living and the dead, Whose kingdom shall have no end.
And in the Holy Spirit, the Lord, the Giver of life, Who proceeds from the Father and the Son;[^49]
Who with the Father and the Son together is worshipped and glorified, Who spoke by the prophets.
In one, holy, catholic, and apostolic Church.
I confess one baptism for the remission of sins.
I look for the resurrection of the dead,
And the life of the age to come. Amen.

*Then:*

Remit, pardon, forgive, O God, our offenses, voluntary and involuntary, in word and in deed, in knowledge and in ignorance, by day and by night, in mind and in thought: forgive us all, for Thou art good and the Lover of mankind.

*Trisagion, and after the Our Father:*

*Kontakion, Tone 3:*

Today the Virgin comes to the cave to give birth ineffably to the Pre-eternal Word: dance, O inhabited earth, when thou hearest; glorify with the angels and the shepherds Him Who wills to appear as a young Child, the Pre-eternal God.

*Then Lord, have mercy (40 times), and the Prayer:*

All-holy Trinity, consubstantial dominion, undivided kingdom, Cause of all good things: be favorably inclined even toward me, a sinner; strengthen and give understanding to my heart, and take away all my defilement; enlighten my mind, that I may continually praise, sing, and worship, and say: One is holy, One is Lord, Jesus Christ, to the glory of God the Father. Amen.

*(If there is no Liturgy, we say here: Blessed be the name of the Lord from henceforth and forevermore, thrice).*[^50]

Glory to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, both now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.

*Psalm 33:*[^51]

I will bless the Lord at all times; His praise shall continually be in my mouth.
My soul shall make her boast in the Lord; let the meek hear thereof and be glad.
O magnify the Lord with me, and let us exalt His name together.
I sought the Lord, and He heard me, and delivered me from all my tribulations.
Come unto Him and be enlightened, and your faces shall not be ashamed.
This poor man cried, and the Lord heard him, and saved him out of all his tribulations.
The angel of the Lord shall encamp round about those who fear Him, and shall deliver them.
O taste and see that the Lord is good; blessed is the man that trusts in Him.
O fear the Lord, all ye His saints; for there is no want to them that fear Him.

The rich have become poor and suffered hunger; but they that seek the Lord shall not lack any good thing.
Come, ye children, give ear unto me; I will teach you the fear of the Lord.
What man is he that desires life, who loves to see good days?
Keep thy tongue from evil, and thy lips from speaking guile.
Turn away from evil, and do good; seek peace, and pursue it.
The eyes of the Lord are upon the righteous, and His ears are open unto their prayer.
The face of the Lord is against them that do evil, to cut off the remembrance of them from the earth.
The righteous cried, and the Lord heard them, and delivered them out of all their afflictions.
The Lord is near unto them that are of a broken heart, and will save such as be of a contrite spirit.
Many are the afflictions of the righteous, but the Lord shall deliver them out of them all.
The Lord keeps all their bones; not one of them shall be broken.
The death of sinners is evil, and they that hate the righteous shall offend.
The Lord will redeem the souls of His servants, and none of them that hope in Him shall offend.

*Then we say: It is truly meet... And the dismissal takes place.*[^52]

## Vespers Together with the Divine Liturgy on the Nativity of Christ

*Nativity of our Lord, God, and Savior Jesus Christ. At Vespers together with the Divine Liturgy of Saint Basil.*[^53]

*It behooves to know that if the Eve of the Nativity of Christ falls on a Saturday or on a Sunday, the Liturgy of Saint John Chrysostom is chanted; but the Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great is chanted on the very feast day of the Nativity.*[^54]

**Priest:** Blessed is the kingdom...
**Choir:** Amen.
*Heavenly King... Trisagion... Our Father... Lord, have mercy (12 times). Glory... Both now... Come, let us worship (thrice).*

*Psalm 103:*[^55]

Bless the Lord, O my soul! O Lord my God, Thou art magnified exceedingly.
Thou hast clothed Thyself with confession and majesty, Who coverest Thyself with light as with a garment.
Who stretchest out the heavens like a curtain, Who coverest His upper chambers with waters.
Who makest the clouds His chariot, Who walkest upon the wings of the winds.
Who makest His angels spirits, and His ministers a flame of fire.

Who hast founded the earth upon the firmness thereof; it shall not be turned back unto ages of ages.
The deep, like a garment, is His vesture; upon the mountains shall the waters stand.
At Thy rebuke they shall flee; at the voice of Thy thunder they shall be afraid.
The mountains ascend, and the plains descend, into the place which Thou hast founded for them.
Thou hast set a bound which they shall not pass over, neither shall they turn again to cover the earth.
He sends forth springs in the valleys; between the mountains the waters shall pass.
They give drink to all the beasts of the field; the wild asses await them in their thirst.
By them the birds of heaven shall have their habitation; from the midst of the rocks they shall utter their voice.
He waters the mountains from His higher chambers; the earth shall be satisfied with the fruit of Thy works.
He causes grass to spring up for cattle, and herb for the service of men,
To bring forth bread out of the earth; and wine makes glad the heart of man,
To make his face cheerful with oil; and bread strengthens man's heart.
The trees of the plain shall be satisfied, the cedars of Lebanon which Thou hast planted; there the sparrows make their nests.
The habitation of the heron is the chief among them: the high mountains for the stags, the rock a refuge for the hares.
He made the moon for seasons; the sun knows its going down.

Thou hast appointed darkness, and it became night, wherein all the beasts of the forest shall prowl.
The young lions roaring to snatch their prey, and to seek their food from God.
The sun arose, and they gathered together, and shall lay them down in their dens.
Man shall go forth unto his work, and unto his labor until evening.
How magnified are Thy works, O Lord! In wisdom hast Thou made them all; the earth is filled with Thy creation.
This is the great and wide sea, therein are creeping things without number,
Living creatures small and great; there the ships sail across.
This dragon, whom Thou hast made to play therein; all things wait upon Thee, that Thou mayest give them food in due season.
When Thou givest it them, they will gather it; when Thou openest Thine hand, all things are filled with goodness.
But when Thou turnest away Thy face, they are troubled; Thou wilt take away their breath, and they fail, and return to their dust.
Thou wilt send forth Thy Spirit, and they shall be created, and Thou shalt renew the face of the earth.
May the glory of the Lord endure unto the ages; the Lord shall rejoice in His works.
Who looks upon the earth and makes it tremble, Who touches the mountains and they smoke.
I will sing unto the Lord in my life, I will chant unto my God while I have my being.
May my meditation be sweet unto Him, and I will be glad in the Lord.[^56]

May sinners disappear from the earth, and the lawless, so that they be no more. Bless the Lord, O my soul!
The sun knows its going down; Thou hast appointed darkness, and it became night.
How magnified are Thy works, O Lord! In wisdom hast Thou made them all.
*Glory... Both now... Alleluia (thrice). Glory to Thee, O God (thrice).*

*Then the Priest says the Great Litany:*[^57]
**Priest:** In peace let us pray to the Lord.
*After each petition the Choir says:*
**Choir:** Lord, have mercy.
**Priest:**
For the peace from above and for the salvation of our souls, let us pray to the Lord.
For the peace of the whole world, the good estate of the holy churches of God, and the union of all, let us pray to the Lord.
For this holy house, and for those who enter into it with faith, reverence, and the fear of God, let us pray to the Lord.
For our most reverend Archbishop and Metropolitan, Lord *[Name]*, and for our God-loving Bishop, Lord *[Name]*, for the venerable presbytery, the diaconate in Christ, and for all the clergy and the people, let us pray to the Lord.[^58]
For our most religious Emperor *[Name]*, for all his court and army, let us pray to the Lord.
That He may help him and subdue under his feet every enemy and adversary, let us pray to the Lord.

**Priest:**
For this city *(or this village, or this holy monastery)*, and for every city, countryside, and the faithful dwelling therein, let us pray to the Lord.
For favorable weather, an abundance of the fruits of the earth, and for peaceful times, let us pray to the Lord.
For travelers by water and by land, the sick, the suffering, those in captivity, and for their salvation, let us pray to the Lord.
For our deliverance from all tribulation, wrath, and need, let us pray to the Lord.
Help us, save us, have mercy on us, and protect us, O God, by Thy grace.
Remembering our most holy, most pure, most blessed, and glorious Lady, the Theotokos and Ever-Virgin Mary, with all the Saints, let us commend ourselves and one another, and our whole life unto Christ our God.
**Choir:** To Thee, O Lord.
**Priest:** For unto Thee is due all glory, honor, and worship, to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, now and ever, and unto ages of ages.
**Choir:** Amen.
*(If it be on Saturday evening, we chant: Blessed is the man, the entire Kathisma. If on Sunday evening, we chant only the first Antiphon. But if on any other day, there is no Stichology; but after the Litany we immediately chant in Tone 2:)*[^59]

*Psalm 140:*[^60]
Lord, I have cried unto Thee, give ear unto me; give ear unto me, O Lord. Lord, I have cried unto Thee, give ear unto me; attend to the voice of my supplication when I cry unto Thee; give ear unto me, O Lord.

Let my prayer be set forth as incense before Thee, the lifting up of my hands as an evening sacrifice; give ear unto me, O Lord.
Set a watch, O Lord, before my mouth, and a door of enclosure about my lips.
Incline not my heart unto words of evil, to make excuses for sins,
With men that work iniquity; and I will not join with their chosen.
The righteous man shall chasten me with mercy and reprove me; but let not the oil of the sinner anoint my head.
For yet my prayer also shall be in their good pleasures; their judges have been swallowed up near the rock.
They shall hear my words, for they are sweet; as when the thickness of the earth is broken upon the ground, their bones are scattered beside Hades.
For unto Thee, O Lord, Lord, are mine eyes; in Thee have I hoped, take not away my soul.
Keep me from the snare which they have laid for me, and from the stumbling-blocks of the workers of iniquity.
The sinners shall fall into their own net; I am alone until I pass by.

*Psalm 141:*[^61]
With my voice cried I unto the Lord; with my voice unto the Lord did I make my supplication.
I will pour out before Him my petition; my affliction will I declare before Him.
When my spirit departed from me, then Thou knewest my paths.
In this way wherein I walked, they secretly laid a snare for me.

I looked on my right hand and beheld, and there was no man that knew me.
Refuge failed me, and there was no man that cared for my soul.
I cried unto Thee, O Lord, and said: Thou art my hope, my portion in the land of the living.
Attend unto my supplication, for I am brought very low.
Deliver me from my persecutors, for they are stronger than I.
Bring my soul out of prison, that I may confess Thy name.
The righteous shall wait for me, until Thou recompense me.

*And we chant these Idiomelon Stichera, Tone 2:*
*Verse:* Out of the depths have I cried unto Thee, O Lord; Lord, give ear unto my voice.[^62]
Come, let us rejoice in the Lord, proclaiming this present mystery: the middle wall of partition is broken down; the fiery sword turns its back, and the Cherubim withdraws from the tree of life, and I partake of the food of Paradise, from which I was driven out through disobedience. For the unchangeable Image of the Father, the Image of His eternity, takes the form of a servant, having come forth from a Mother unwedded, having suffered no change; for what He was, He remained, being true God; and what He was not, He took upon Himself, having become man for love of mankind. Unto Him let us cry: Thou Who wast born of the Virgin, O God, have mercy on us.[^63]

*Verse:* Let Thine ears be attentive to the voice of my supplication.
*And we chant the same Sticheron again:*
*Come, let us rejoice in the Lord...*
*Verse:* If Thou, Lord, shouldest mark iniquities, O Lord, who shall stand? For with Thee there is forgiveness.
When the Lord Jesus was born of the holy Virgin, all creation was enlightened: while the shepherds piped and the Magi worshipped, and the Angels sang aloud, Herod was troubled, because God had appeared in the flesh, the Savior of our souls.[^64]
*Verse:* For Thy name's sake have I waited for Thee, O Lord; my soul has waited patiently for Thy word, my soul has hoped in the Lord.
*And we chant the same Sticheron again:*
*When the Lord Jesus was born...*
*Verse:* From the morning watch until night, from the morning watch let Israel hope in the Lord.
Thy kingdom, O Christ God, is a kingdom of all ages, and Thy dominion is throughout all generations. Thou Who wast incarnate of the Holy Spirit and made man of the Ever-Virgin Mary, a light has shone upon us, O Christ God, at Thy coming; Light of Light, the radiance of the Father, Thou hast enlightened all creation; every breath praises Thee: Thou Who art the Image of the Father's glory, Who art, and Who wast before all ages, and hast shone forth from the Virgin, O God, have mercy on us.[^65]
*Verse:* For with the Lord there is mercy, and with Him is plenteous redemption, and He shall redeem Israel from all his iniquities.
*And we chant the same Sticheron again:*
*Thy kingdom, O Christ God...*
*Verse:* Praise the Lord, all ye nations; praise Him, all ye peoples.

What shall we offer Thee, O Christ, because Thou hast appeared on earth as man for our sake? For each of the creatures made by Thee offers Thee thanksgiving: the Angels, a hymn; the heavens, a star; the Magi, gifts; the shepherds, wonder; the earth, a cave; the wilderness, a manger; and we, a Virgin Mother. O God Who art before all ages, have mercy on us.[^66]
*Verse:* For His mercy is confirmed upon us, and the truth of the Lord endures unto all ages.
*And we chant the same Sticheron again:*
*What shall we offer Thee, O Christ...*
*Glory... Both now... In the same Tone:*
When Augustus reigned alone upon the earth, the many-sovereignties of men ceased; and when Thou wast made man of the pure Virgin, the polytheism of idols was abolished. Under one earthly kingdom were the cities united, and in one dominion of the Godhead did the nations believe. The people were enrolled by the decree of Caesar; we the faithful were enrolled in the name of the Godhead, of Thee our God Who wast made man. Great is Thy mercy, O Lord, glory to Thee![^67]
*Entrance with the Gospel.*[^68]
**Priest:** Wisdom! Stand upright!
**Choir:** O Gladsome Light of the holy glory of the immortal Father, heavenly, holy, blessed, O Jesus Christ! Having come unto the setting of the sun, and having beheld the evening light, we praise the Father, Son, and Holy Spirit, God. Worthy art Thou at all times to be praised with holy voices, O Son of God, Giver of life; therefore the world glorifies Thee.[^69]
**Priest:** Let us attend! Peace be unto all! Wisdom, let us attend!

*The Choir chants: The Prokeimenon of the day; and the Readings in their order, with their Troparia and their Verses. If the Eve of the Nativity of Christ occurs on Friday, the Prokeimenon is chanted: Who is so great a God as our God? with its Verses (see the Synaxis of the Most Holy Theotokos)[^70], but the Prokeimenon of the day is omitted.*[^71]

*Reading from Genesis (Chapter 1):*[^72]
In the beginning God created the heaven and the earth. And the earth was invisible and unformed, and darkness was upon the face of the abyss; and the Spirit of God moved upon the face of the water. And God said: Let there be light, and there was light. And God saw the light, that it was good; and God divided between the light and the darkness. And God called the light, Day; and the darkness He called, Night. And there was evening, and there was morning, one day. And God said: Let there be a firmament in the midst of the water, and let it divide between water and water, and it was so. And God made the firmament, and God divided between the water which was under the firmament, and between the water which was above the firmament. And God called the firmament, Heaven; and God saw that it was good; and there was evening, and there was morning, a second day. And God said: Let the water which is under the heaven be gathered into one gathering, and let the dry land appear; and it was so; and the water which was under the heaven was gathered into its gatherings, and the dry land appeared. And God called the dry land, Earth, and the gatherings of the waters He called, Seas; and God saw that it was good. And God said: Let the earth bring forth the herb of grass, yielding seed according to its kind and according to likeness, and the fruit-bearing tree, bearing fruit whose seed is in itself according to its kind upon the earth; and it was so. And the earth brought forth the herb of grass, yielding seed according to its kind and according to likeness, and the fruit-bearing tree, bearing fruit whose seed is in itself according to its kind upon the earth; and God saw that it was good. And there was evening, and there was morning, a third day.

*Reading from Numbers (Chapter 24):*[^73]
The Spirit of God came upon Balaam, and he took up his parable, and said: How goodly are thy dwellings, O Jacob, and thy tabernacles, O Israel! As shaded valleys, as gardens beside rivers, and as tents which the Lord has pitched, and as cedars beside the waters. A Man shall come forth from his seed, and shall rule over many nations, and His kingdom shall be exalted, and shall increase. God guided Him out of Egypt, as the glory of a unicorn is His. He shall eat up the nations of His enemies, and shall exhaust their fatness, and with His arrows He shall shoot through the enemy. He lay down and reposed as a lion, and as a lion's whelp; who shall rouse Him up? Blessed are they that bless Thee, and cursed are they that curse Thee. A star shall dawn out of Jacob, and a Man shall rise out of Israel, and shall crush the princes of Moab, and shall spoil all the sons of Seth. And Edom shall be a possession, and Esau His enemy shall be a heritage; and Israel has wrought strength.

*Reading from the Prophecy of Micah (Chapter 4):*[^74]
In those days, says the Lord: I will assemble her that is bruised, and receive her that is cast off, and her whom I have rejected; and I will make her that was bruised a remnant, and her that was cast off a strong nation; and the Lord shall reign over them in Mount Zion from henceforth and unto the ages. And thou, Bethlehem, house of Ephrathah, art thou least to be among the thou- -sands of Judah? For out of thee shall come forth for Me a ruler, to be prince in Israel, and His goings forth are from the beginning, from the days of eternity. Therefore shall He give them until the time of her that travails; she shall bring forth, and the remnant of His brethren shall return unto the sons of Israel. And He shall stand, and see, and shepherd His flock in the strength of the Lord; and in the glory of the name of the Lord their God shall they abide, for now shall He be magnified unto the ends of the earth.

*Then we say this Troparion, Tone 6:*[^75]
Secretly wast Thou born in the cave, but heaven proclaimed Thee unto all, using the star as a mouth, O Savior, and brought Thee Magi who worshipped Thee with faith; with them have mercy on us.

*Verse:* His foundations are in the holy mountains; the Lord loves the gates of Zion more than all the dwellings of Jacob; glorious things are spoken of thee, O city of God; I will make mention of Rahab and Babylon unto them that know me.

*And we chant the ending of the Troparion:*
And brought Thee Magi who worshipped Thee with faith; with them have mercy on us.

*Verse:* And lo, the foreigners, and Tyre, and the people of Ethiopia, these were born there; Zion is my mother, a man shall say, and a man was born in her, and the Most High Himself has established her.

And brought Thee Magi who worshipped Thee with faith; with them have mercy on us.

*Verse:* The Lord shall tell it in the writing of the peoples and of the princes, of these that have been in her: As of men that are rejoicing, the dwelling of all is in thee.
And brought Thee Magi who worshipped Thee with faith; with them have mercy on us.
*Then, Glory... the ending of the Troparion. Both now... likewise the ending. Thereafter the Reader says the whole Troparion, and chants its ending himself.*
Secretly wast Thou born in the cave, but heaven proclaimed Thee unto all, using the star as a mouth, O Savior, and brought Thee Magi who worshipped Thee with faith; with them have mercy on us.
*Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (Chapter 11):*[^76]
Thus says the Lord: There shall come forth a rod out of the root of Jesse, and a blossom shall come up from his root. And the Spirit of God shall rest upon Him: the Spirit of wisdom and understanding, the Spirit of counsel and strength, the Spirit of knowledge and piety; the Spirit of the fear of God shall fill Him. He shall not judge according to appearance, neither shall He reprove according to speech; but He shall judge the cause of the humble with righteousness, and shall reprove the glorious of the earth with equity; and He shall strike the earth with the word of His mouth, and with the breath of His lips shall He slay the ungodly.
