And He shall have His loins girt with righteousness, and His ribs clothed with truth. Then shall the wolf feed with the lamb, and the leopard shall rest with the kid, and the calf and the lion and the bullock shall feed together, and a little child shall lead them. The calf and the lion shall graze together, and their young shall be together; and the lion shall eat chaff like an ox.

And a young child shall put his hand on the hole of asps, and on the den of the offspring of asps. And they shall not do hurt, nor shall they be able to destroy anyone upon My holy mountain; for the whole earth is filled with the knowledge of the Lord, as much water covers the seas. And in that day there shall be the root of Jesse, and He that rises up to rule over the Gentiles; in Him shall the Gentiles hope, and His rest shall be honor.
*Reading from the Prophecy of Jeremiah (Chapter 3):*[^77]
This is our God, and no other shall be accounted of in comparison with Him. He has found out all the way of knowledge, and has given it unto Jacob His servant, and to Israel His beloved. Afterward did He appear upon earth, and conversed with men. This is the book of the commandments of God, and the law that endures unto the ages. All they that keep it shall come to life, but such as leave it shall die. Turn thee, O Jacob, and take hold of it; walk towards the radiance, before the light thereof. Give not thine honor to another, nor the things that are profitable unto thee to a strange nation. Blessed are we, O Israel, for things that are pleasing to God are made known unto us.
*Reading from the Prophecy of Daniel (Chapter 2):*[^78]
Daniel said unto Nebuchadnezzar: Thou, O king, sawest, and behold a statue; that statue was great, and its aspect circular, standing before thy face, and the form thereof was terrible. The statue, whose head was of fine gold, its hands and breast and arms of silver, its belly and thighs of brass, its legs of iron, its feet part of iron and part of clay. Thou sawest till a stone was cut out of a mountain without hands, and struck the statue upon its feet that were of iron and clay, and ground them to pieces. Then were the clay, the iron, the brass, the silver, and the gold broken to pieces together, and became like dust from the summer threshing-floor; and the greatness of the wind carried them away, and no place was found for them; but the stone that struck the statue became a great mountain, and filled the whole earth. This is the dream, and we will tell the interpretation thereof before the king: The God of heaven shall set up a kingdom which shall never be destroyed, and His kingdom shall not be left to other people, and it shall break in pieces and crush all kingdoms, and it shall stand unto the ages. Even as thou sawest that the stone was cut out of the mountain without hands, and broke in pieces the clay, the iron, the brass, the silver, and the gold. The great God has made known to the king what shall come to pass hereafter; and the dream is true, and the interpretation thereof sure.
*Thereafter we say this Troparion, Tone 6:*[^79]
Thou hast shone forth from the Virgin, O Christ, the noetic Sun of righteousness; and a star showed Thee in the cave, containing the Incontainable. Thou didst guide the Magi to Thy worship; with them we magnify Thee: O Giver of life, glory to Thee!
*Verse:* The Lord has reigned, He is clothed with majesty; the Lord is clothed with strength and has girt Himself; for He has established the world, which shall not be moved; Thy throne is prepared from that time; Thou art from everlasting.
*And we chant the ending of the Troparion:*
Thou didst guide the Magi to Thy worship; with them we magnify Thee: O Giver of life, glory to Thee!

*Verse:* The floods have lifted up, O Lord, the floods have lifted up their voices; the floods shall lift up their waves, with the voices of many waters.
Thou didst guide the Magi to Thy worship; with them we magnify Thee: O Giver of life, glory to Thee!
*Verse:* Wonderful are the surges of the sea, wonderful is the Lord on high; Thy testimonies are made very sure; holiness becomes Thy house, O Lord, unto length of days.
*Glory:* We chant the ending of the Troparion. *Both now:* again the ending. *Then the whole Troparion:*
Thou hast shone forth from the Virgin, O Christ, the noetic Sun of righteousness; and a star showed Thee in the cave, containing the Incontainable. Thou didst guide the Magi to Thy worship; with them we magnify Thee: O Giver of life, glory to Thee!
*The ending of the Troparion the Reader chants himself, and thereafter he reads:*
*Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (Chapter 9):*[^80]
A Child is born unto us, a Son, and is given unto us, Whose government was upon His shoulder; and His name is called: Messenger of great counsel, wonderful counselor, mighty God, ruler, prince of peace, Father of the age to come; for He shall bring peace upon the rulers, and His health. And great is His government, and of His peace there is no end, upon the throne of David and upon His kingdom, to order it and to defend it with judgment and with righteousness from henceforth and unto eternity; the zeal of the Lord of Sabaoth shall perform this.
*Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (Chapter 7):*[^81]
The Lord spoke again... (see p. 24)[^82] *Thereafter the Little Litany, and after the Exclamation, the Trisagion:*[^83]
*Prokeimenon, Tone 1:*[^84]
The Lord said unto Me: Thou art My Son, this day have I begotten Thee.
*Verse:* Ask of Me, and I shall give Thee the nations for Thine inheritance, and the uttermost parts of the earth for Thy possession.
*Epistle to the Hebrews, Pericope 303:*[^85]
God, having spoken in many parts and in many ways... (see p. 8)[^86]
*Alleluia, Tone 2:*[^87]
The Lord said unto My Lord: Sit Thou at My right hand, until I make Thine enemies the footstool of Thy feet.
*Verse:* The Lord shall send Thee the rod of power out of Zion.
*Verse:* From the womb before the morning star have I begotten Thee.
*Gospel according to Luke, Pericope 5:*[^88]
In those days, there went out a decree from Caesar Augustus... (see p. 16)[^89]
*And the Divine Liturgy of Saint Basil the Great in order.*[^90]
*Instead of "It is truly meet," we chant:*[^91]
In thee rejoices, O full of grace, all creation, the angelic assembly and the race of men; O sanctified temple and noetic paradise, boast of virgins, of whom God was incarnate and became a babe, He Who is our God before all ages. For He made thy womb a throne, and fashioned thy body more spacious than the heavens. In thee rejoices, O full of grace, all creation; glory to thee!

*It should be known that if the Eve of the Nativity of Christ falls on Saturday or Sunday, the Liturgy of Saint John Chrysostom is celebrated at its proper time, and Vespers is chanted at its proper time as follows:*[^92]
**Priest:** Blessed is our God.
**Choir:** Amen. *Then:* Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee. O Heavenly King... *and the rest with the Trisagion; and after the Our Father:* Come, let us worship... *After the Exclamation, the Prooemiac Psalm:* Bless the Lord, O my soul... (see p. 44)[^93], *the Great Litany:* In peace let us pray to the Lord (see p. 47)[^94]. *After the Litany:*
Blessed is the man — Alleluia — that walks not in the counsel of the ungodly. Alleluia.
For the Lord knows the way of the righteous, and the way of the ungodly, the way of the ungodly shall perish. Alleluia.
Serve the Lord with fear, and rejoice in Him with trembling. Alleluia.
Blessed are all they that put their trust in Him, all they that put their trust in Him. Alleluia.
Lord, why are they multiplied that afflict me? Alleluia.
Arise, O Lord, save me, O my God. Alleluia.
Salvation is of the Lord, and Thy blessing is upon Thy people. Alleluia.
Glory: Alleluia. Both now: Alleluia. Alleluia, thrice. Glory to Thee, O God, thrice.
*At "Lord, I have cried," we chant the Idiomelon Stichera, Tone 2:* Come, let us rejoice... — When the Lord Jesus was born... — Thy kingdom, O Christ God... — What shall we offer Thee, O Christ... Glory... Both now... When Augustus reigned alone upon the earth... (see pp. 50–52)[^95]. *Entrance with the Gospel Book; O Gladsome Light (see p. 52)[^96]. The Prokeimenon of the day. (If on Saturday evening, the Prokeimenon: The Lord has reigned— —and the Readings according to the order, of which there shall be eight. After the Readings: Epistle to the Galatians, Pericope 67; Alleluia; Gospel according to Matthew, Pericope 53. After the Gospel, the Litany:)*[^97]
**Deacon:** Let us all say with our whole soul and with our whole mind, let us say.
**Choir:** Lord, have mercy.
**Deacon:** O Lord Almighty, the God of our fathers, we pray Thee, hear and have mercy.
**Choir:** Lord, have mercy (thrice).
**Deacon:** Have mercy on us, O God, according to Thy great mercy, we pray Thee, hear and have mercy.
**Choir:** Lord, have mercy.
**Deacon:** Again we pray for the most holy Universal Pontiff, [Name], Pope of Rome, and for our pious and God-preserved Emperor, [Name], for their realm, victory, continuance, peace, health, salvation, and the remission of their sins; and that the Lord our God may especially further and prosper them in all things, and subdue under their feet every enemy and adversary.[^98]
**Choir:** Lord, have mercy (thrice).
**Deacon:** Again we pray for our most reverend Archbishop, Metropolitan Lord [Name], and for our God-loving Bishop Lord [Name], and for our spiritual fathers, and for all our brethren in Christ.[^99]
**Choir:** Lord, have mercy (thrice).
**Deacon:** Again we pray for the people present, who await from Thee great and rich mercy, for those who show us mercy, and for all orthodox Christians.
**Choir:** Lord, have mercy (thrice).

**Priest:** For a merciful and man-loving God art Thou, and unto Thee do we send up glory, to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, now and ever, and unto ages of ages.
**Choir:** Amen.
*Thereafter we say:*[^100]
Vouchsafe, O Lord, to keep us this evening without sin.
Blessed art Thou, O Lord, God of our fathers, and praised and glorified is Thy name unto the ages. Amen.
Let Thy mercy, O Lord, be upon us, according as we have set our hope on Thee.
Blessed art Thou, O Lord: teach us Thy statutes.
Blessed art Thou, O Master: make us to understand Thy statutes.
Blessed art Thou, O Holy One: enlighten us with Thy statutes.
O Lord, Thy mercy endures unto the ages; despise not the works of Thy hands.
To Thee belongs praise, to Thee belongs song.
To Thee glory belongs, to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit.
Now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.
*Thereafter the Litany:*[^101]
**Deacon:** Let us complete our evening prayer unto the Lord.
**Choir:** Lord, have mercy.
**Deacon:** For this holy temple, and for those who enter into it with faith, reverence, and fear of God, let us pray to the Lord.

**Deacon:** That we may be delivered from all affliction, wrath, and need, let us pray to the Lord.
Help us, save us, have mercy on us, and keep us, O God, by Thy grace.
That the whole day may be perfect, holy, peaceful, and sinless, let us ask of the Lord.
*The Choir says after each petition:*
**Choir:** Grant this, O Lord.
**Deacon:** An angel of peace, a faithful guide, a guardian of our souls and bodies, let us ask of the Lord.
Pardon and remission of our sins and offenses, let us ask of the Lord.
Things good and profitable for our souls, and peace for the world, let us ask of the Lord.
That we may complete the remaining time of our life in peace and repentance, let us ask of the Lord.
A Christian ending to our life, painless, unashamed, peaceful, and a good defense before the dread judgment seat of Christ, let us ask.
Commemorating our all-holy, immaculate, most blessed and glorious Lady, the Theotokos and Ever-Virgin Mary, with all the Saints, let us commend ourselves and one another, and our whole life unto Christ our God.
**Choir:** To Thee, O Lord.
**Priest:** Through the bounties and love toward mankind of Thine Only-begotten Son, with Whom Thou art blessed, together with Thine all-holy, good, and life-creating Spirit, now and ever, and unto ages of ages.
**Choir:** Amen.

**Priest:** Wisdom!
**Choir:** Bless.
**Priest:** Blessed and most glorified is He Who is, Christ our God.
**Choir:** Establish, O God, the Catholic faith unto ages of ages.[^102]
**Priest:** Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.
**Choir:** More honorable than the Cherubim, and beyond compare more glorious than the Seraphim, who without corruption gavest birth to God the Word, the very Theotokos, thee do we magnify.
**Priest:** Glory to Thee, O Christ God, our hope, glory to Thee.
**Choir:** Glory... Both now... Lord, have mercy (twice). Lord, bless.
*The Priest says the Dismissal:*[^103]
**Priest:** May He Who was born in a cave in Bethlehem of Judea, and lay in a manger for the sake of our salvation, Christ our true God, through the prayers of His most pure Mother, of the holy, glorious, and all-praised Apostles, of our venerable and God-bearing fathers, and of all the Saints, have mercy on us and save us, as He is good and loves mankind.
**Choir:** Amen.
*Thereafter both choirs, gathering together in the midst of the church, chant together the Troparion of the Feast:* Thy Nativity... *Glory... Both now... The Kontakion of the Feast:* Today the Virgin...[^104]

---

# PART II: THE FEAST OF THE NATIVITY OF OUR LORD JESUS CHRIST (GREAT COMPLINE, MATINS, AND DIVINE LITURGY)

## Great Compline on the Nativity of Christ[^105]

**Priest:** Blessed is our God always, now and ever, and unto ages of ages.
**Choir:** Amen. Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee. O Heavenly King... Trisagion, and after the Our Father: Come, let us worship... and we begin:
*Psalm 4:*
When I called upon Thee, the God of my righteousness heard me; in tribulation Thou hast enlarged me.
Have mercy on me, and hear my prayer.
O ye sons of men, how long will ye be slow of heart? Why love ye vanity, and seek after falsehood?
Know ye also that the Lord has made marvelous His holy one; the Lord will hear me when I call unto Him.
Be ye angry, and sin not; what ye say in your hearts, repent thereof upon your beds.
Offer the sacrifice of righteousness, and hope in the Lord; many say: Who will show us good things?
The light of Thy countenance is signed upon us, O Lord; Thou hast put gladness into my heart.
By the fruit of their wheat, wine, and oil have they been multiplied.
In peace, together, will I sleep and take my rest.
For Thou, Lord, only hast made me to dwell in hope.

*Psalm 6:*[^106]
O Lord, rebuke me not in Thine anger, nor chasten me in Thy wrath.
Have mercy on me, O Lord, for I am weak; heal me, O Lord, for my bones are troubled.
And my soul is troubled exceedingly; and Thou, O Lord, how long?
Turn Thee, O Lord, deliver my soul; save me for Thy mercy's sake.
For in death there is no one who remembers Thee; and in Hades who will give thanks unto Thee?
I have laboured in my groaning; every night will I wash my bed, I will water my couch with my tears.
Mine eye is troubled through anger; I have grown old among all mine enemies.
Depart from me, all ye workers of iniquity, for the Lord has heard the voice of my weeping.
The Lord has heard my supplication; the Lord has accepted my prayer.
Let all mine enemies be ashamed and troubled exceedingly; let them turn back and be ashamed very quickly.

*Psalm 12:*[^107]
How long, O Lord, wilt Thou forget me unto the end? How long wilt Thou turn away Thy face from me?
How long shall I take counsel in my soul, having sorrow in my heart day and night?
How long shall mine enemy be exalted over me? Look upon me and hear me, O Lord my God.
Enlighten mine eyes, lest I sleep the sleep of death; lest mine enemy say: I have prevailed against him.

Those that afflict me will rejoice if I am moved; but I have hoped in Thy mercy.
My heart shall rejoice in Thy salvation; I will sing unto the Lord Who has dealt bountifully with me, and I will chant unto the name of the Lord Most High.
*Glory... Both now... Alleluia (thrice). Lord, have mercy (thrice).*
*Glory... Both now... Psalm 24:*[^108]
Unto Thee, O Lord, have I lifted up my soul; O my God, in Thee have I hoped, let me not be put to shame forever.
Neither let mine enemies laugh me to scorn; for none of them that wait on Thee shall be put to shame.
Let them be ashamed that do iniquity in vain.
Show me Thy ways, O Lord, and teach me Thy paths.
Lead me in Thy truth, and teach me, for Thou art God my Savior, and on Thee have I waited all the day.
Remember Thy compassions, O Lord, and Thy mercies, for they are from everlasting.
The sins of my youth and of mine ignorance remember not.
According to Thy mercy remember Thou me, for the sake of Thy goodness.
Good and upright is the Lord; therefore will He set a law for them that sin in the way.
The meek will He guide in judgment; the meek will He teach His ways.
All the paths of the Lord are mercy and truth, unto them that seek His covenant and His testimonies.
For Thy name's sake, O Lord, pardon also my sin, for it is great.

Who is the man that fears the Lord? He will set a law for him in the way which He has chosen.
His soul shall dwell among good things, and his seed shall inherit the earth.
The Lord is the strength of them that fear Him, and His covenant will He show them.
Mine eyes are ever toward the Lord, for He will pluck my feet out of the snare.
Look upon me and have mercy on me, for I am an only child and poor.
The afflictions of my heart are multiplied; bring me out of my distresses.
Behold my lowliness and my toil, and forgive all my sins.
Behold mine enemies, for they are multiplied, and have hated me with an unjust hatred.
Keep my soul and deliver me; let me not be put to shame, for I have hoped in Thee.
The innocent and the upright have cleaved unto me, for I have waited on Thee, O Lord.
Deliver Israel, O God, out of all his afflictions.
*Psalm 30:*[^109]
In Thee, O Lord, have I hoped, let me not be put to shame forever; in Thy righteousness deliver me and rescue me.
Bow down Thine ear unto me, make haste to deliver me.
Be Thou unto me a God of defense, and a house of refuge to save me.
For Thou art my strength and my refuge, and for Thy name's sake wilt Thou guide me and nourish me.

Thou wilt bring me out of this snare which they have hidden for me, for Thou art my defender, O Lord.
Into Thy hands will I commit my spirit; Thou hast redeemed me, O Lord God of truth.
Thou hast hated them that regard vain vanities in vain.
But I have hoped in the Lord; I will be glad and rejoice in Thy mercy.
For Thou hast looked upon my lowliness; Thou hast saved my soul out of distresses.
And Thou hast not shut me up into the hands of the enemy; Thou hast set my feet in a spacious place.
Have mercy on me, O Lord, for I am in trouble; mine eye is troubled with anger, yea, my soul and my belly.
For my life is spent with grief, and my years with sighing;
My strength has failed in poverty, and my bones are troubled.
I was made a reproach among all mine enemies, and especially among my neighbours, and a fear to mine acquaintances;
They that saw me without fled from me; I was forgotten as a dead man out of mind.
I became like a vessel that is lost; for I heard the slander of many that dwell round about.
When they were assembled together against me, they took counsel to take away my life.
But I have hoped in Thee, O Lord; I said: Thou art my God; in Thy hands is my lot.
Deliver me from the hand of mine enemies, and from them that persecute me.

Make Thy face to shine upon Thy servant; save me in Thy mercy; O Lord, let me not be put to shame, for I have called upon Thee.
Let the ungodly be ashamed, and let them go down into Hades; let the deceitful lips be put to silence,
Which speak iniquity against the righteous, with pride and contempt.
How great is the multitude of Thy goodness, O Lord, which Thou hast hidden for them that fear Thee!
Which Thou hast wrought for them that hope in Thee, before the sons of men!
Thou shalt hide them in the secret of Thy countenance, from the turmoil of man.
Thou shalt shelter them in a tabernacle from the contradiction of tongues.
Blessed is the Lord, for He has made marvelous His mercy in a fortified city.
As for me, I said in mine ecstasy: I am cast away from the countenance of Thine eyes.
Therefore hast Thou heard the voice of my prayer, when I cried unto Thee.
Love the Lord, all ye His saints; for the Lord searches out truth, and repays exceedingly them that act with pride.
Be of good courage, and let your heart be strengthened, all ye that hope in the Lord.
*Psalm 90:*[^110]
He that dwells in the help of the Most High... (see p. 21)[^111]
*Thereafter we begin the Verses with sweet melody, slowly:*[^112]
*God is with us, understand, ye nations, and submit yourselves; for God is with us.*

*Hear, even unto the ends of the earth: for God is with us.*
*Submit yourselves, ye mighty ones: for God is with us.*
*For if again ye wax strong, again shall ye be overcome: for God is with us.*
*And whatever counsel ye take, the Lord will bring it to naught: for God is with us.*
*And whatever word ye speak, it shall not abide in you: for God is with us.*
*And of your fear we will not be afraid, nor will we be troubled: for God is with us.*
*The Lord our God, Him sanctify ye, and He shall be your fear: for God is with us.*
*And if thou trust in Him, He shall be unto thee for sanctification: for God is with us.*
*And we will hope in Him, and be saved through Him: for God is with us.*
*Behold, I and the children whom God has given me: for God is with us.*
*The people that sat in darkness have seen a great light: for God is with us.*
*Ye that dwell in the land and shadow of death, a light shall shine upon you: for God is with us.*
*For unto us a Child is born, and a Son is given unto us: for God is with us.*
*Whose government is upon His shoulder: for God is with us.*
*And of His peace there is no end: for God is with us.*
*And His name is called: Angel of Great Counsel: for God is with us.*
*Wonderful Counselor: for God is with us.*

*Mighty God, Ruler, Prince of Peace: for God is with us.*
*Father of the age to come: for God is with us.*
*Glory:*
*For God is with us...*
*Both now:*
*For God is with us...*
*God is with us, understand, ye nations, and submit yourselves; for God is with us (thrice).*
*And immediately the following Troparia:*[^113]
*The day being passed, I give thanks unto Thee, O Lord; that the evening with the night may be without sin, grant me, O Savior, and save me.*
*Glory:*
*The day being passed, I glorify Thee, O Master; that the evening with the night may be without stumbling, grant me, O Savior, and save me.*
*Both now:*
*The day being passed, I hymn Thee, O Holy One; that the evening with the night may be free from snares, grant me, O Savior, and save me.*
*The bodiless nature of the Cherubim glorifies Thee with never-silent hymns; the six-winged living creatures, the Seraphim, extol Thee with unceasing voices; and all the angelic hosts praise Thee with thrice-holy songs. For before all art Thou, Who art the Father, and Thou hast Thy Son co-unoriginate, and bearing the Spirit of life equal in honor, Thou showest forth the undivided Trinity.*

*All-Holy Virgin, Mother of God, and ye eyewitnesses and servants of the Word, with the choirs of Prophets and Martyrs, having immortal life, pray ye diligently for all, for we are all in distress; that, being delivered from the deceit of the evil one, we may cry the angelic hymn: Holy, Holy, Holy, Thrice-holy Lord, have mercy on us and save us. Amen.*
*Thereafter we say:*
I believe in one God... (see p. 40)[^114]
*And immediately these Verses:*[^115]
*All-Holy Lady (Sovereign Lady) Theotokos (all-merciful), pray to God for us sinners.*
*All ye heavenly Powers, holy Angels and Archangels, pray to God for us sinners.*
*Holy and great John, Prophet, Forerunner, and Baptist of the Lord, pray to God for us sinners.*
*Holy, glorious, and all-praised Apostles, Prophets, and Martyrs, and all Saints, pray to God for us sinners.*
*Our venerable and God-bearing fathers, Pastors and Teachers of the universe, pray to God for us sinners.*
*Our father among the Saints, Basil the Great, pray to God for us sinners.*
*Holy Hieromartyr of Christ, Josaphat, pray to God for us sinners.*[^116]
*Our venerable fathers Anthony and Theodosius of the Caves, pray to God for us sinners.*[^117]
*Invincible, incomprehensible, and Divine power of the precious and life-creating Cross of the Lord, forsake not us sinners (who hope in thee).*
*O God, be merciful unto us sinners.*
*O God, cleanse our sins, and have mercy on us.*
*Thereafter the Trisagion, and after the Our Father:*
*Troparion, Tone 4:*[^118]
Thy Nativity, O Christ our God, has shone to the world the light of wisdom; for by it, they that worshipped the stars were taught by a star to worship Thee, the Sun of righteousness, and to know Thee from on high, the Dayspring: O Lord, glory to Thee!
Lord, have mercy (40 times). *Glory... Both now...* More honorable than the Cherubim... In the name of the Lord, bless, father.
**Priest:** Through the prayers of our holy fathers... *And he reads this prayer of Saint Basil the Great:*[^119]
O Lord, Lord, Who hast delivered us from every arrow that flies by day, deliver us also from every thing that walks in darkness; accept the evening sacrifice, the lifting up of our hands. Vouchsafe us also to pass the course of the night without blemish, untempted by evils; and deliver us from every trouble and fear that comes to us from the devil; grant unto our souls compunction, and unto our thoughts mindfulness of the trial at Thy dread and righteous judgment; nail down our flesh to Thy fear, and mortify our members that are upon the earth; that even in the quiet of sleep we may be enlightened by the contemplation of Thy judgments; take away from us every improper imagination and harmful lust; and raise us up at the time of prayer, confirmed in faith, and prospering— in Thy commandments, by the good pleasure and goodness of Thine Only-begotten Son, with Whom Thou art blessed, together with Thine All-Holy, good, and life-creating Spirit, now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.
*Come, let us worship (thrice).*
*Psalm 50:*[^120]
Have mercy on me, O God... (see p. 12)[^121]
*Psalm 101:*[^122]
Hear my prayer, O Lord, and let my cry come unto Thee.
Turn not away Thy face from me; in the day when I am in trouble, incline Thine ear unto me.
In the day when I call upon Thee, quickly hear me.
For my days have vanished like smoke, and my bones are dried up like firewood.
I am stricken like grass, and my heart is withered, for I forgot to eat my bread.
By reason of the voice of my groaning, my bone has cleaved to my flesh.
I am become like a pelican of the wilderness; I am become like an owl in a ruined house.
I have watched, and am become like a solitary bird upon the roof.
All the day long mine enemies reproached me, and they that praised me swore against me.
For I have eaten ashes like bread, and mingled my drink with weeping.

Because of the face of Thine anger and Thy wrath; for having lifted me up, Thou hast cast me down.
My days have declined like a shadow, and I am withered like grass.
But Thou, O Lord, abidest forever, and Thy memorial unto generation and generation.
Thou shalt arise and have compassion upon Sion, for it is time to have compassion upon her, for the time is come.
For Thy servants took pleasure in her stones, and shall have pity on her dust.
And the nations shall fear the name of the Lord, and all the kings of the earth Thy glory.
For the Lord shall build up Sion, and He shall appear in His glory.
He has regarded the prayer of the humble, and has not despised their supplication.
Let this be written for another generation, and the people that is being created shall praise the Lord.
For He has looked down from His holy height; the Lord from heaven has looked upon the earth,
To hear the groaning of the fettered, to release the children of the slain,
To declare the name of the Lord in Sion, and His praise in Jerusalem,
When the peoples are gathered together, and the kings, to serve the Lord.
He answered Him in the way of His strength: Declare unto me the shortness of my days.
Take me not away in the midst of my days; Thy years are unto generation of generations.
In the beginning, Thou, Lord, didst lay the foundation of the earth, and the heavens are the works of Thy hands.

They shall perish, but Thou abidest; and all of them shall wax old like a garment.
And as a vesture shalt Thou fold them, and they shall be changed; but Thou art the same, and Thy years shall not fail.
The children of Thy servants shall dwell, and their seed shall be guided aright forever.
*Prayer of Manasses, King of Judah:*[^123]
O Lord Almighty, God of our fathers, of Abraham, and Isaac, and Jacob, and of their righteous seed; Who hast made heaven and earth with all the array thereof; Who hast bound the sea by the word of Thy commandment; Who hast shut up the abyss, and sealed it by Thy fearful and glorious name; Whom all things fear, and tremble before the face of Thy power; for the majesty of Thy glory cannot be borne, and the anger of Thy threatening toward sinners is intolerable; yet the mercy of Thy promise is immeasurable and unsearchable; for Thou art the Lord Most High, compassionate, longsuffering, and plenteous in mercy, and repenting of the evils of men. Thou, O Lord, according to the multitude of Thy goodness, hast promised repentance and forgiveness unto them that have sinned against Thee, and in the multitude of Thy compassions hast appointed repentance unto sinners for salvation. Thou therefore, O Lord, God of hosts, hast not appointed repentance unto the righteous, unto Abraham, and Isaac, and Jacob, who have not sinned against Thee; but Thou hast appointed repentance unto me, a sinner; for I have sinned above the number of the sand of the sea. Multiplied are mine iniquities, O Lord, multiplied— —are mine iniquities, and I am not worthy to gaze and see the height of heaven for the multitude of mine unrighteousnesses. I am bowed down with many iron bonds, so that I cannot lift up my head, and there is no release for me; because I have provoked Thy wrath, and done evil before Thee, not doing Thy will, nor keeping Thy commandments. And now I bow the knees of my heart, entreating goodness from Thee. I have sinned, O Lord, I have sinned, and I acknowledge mine iniquities; but I ask, praying Thee: Remit unto me, O Lord, remit unto me, and destroy me not with mine iniquities, neither keep evils for me in hostility forever, nor condemn me into the nethermost parts of the earth; for Thou art God, the God of them that repent; and in me Thou wilt show all Thy goodness, for Thou wilt save me, unworthy though I be, according to Thy great mercy; and I will praise Thee continually all the days of my life; for all the power of the heavens praises Thee, and Thine is the glory unto ages of ages. Amen.
*Trisagion. All-Holy Trinity... And after the Our Father:*
**Priest:** For Thine is the kingdom...
**Choir:** Amen. *And sings the Kontakion, Tone 3:*[^124]
Today the Virgin gives birth to the Supersubstantial One, and the earth offers a cave to the Unapproachable One; Angels with shepherds glorify, and the Magi journey with a star; for unto us is born a young Child, the Pre-eternal God.
Lord, have mercy (40 times). *Glory... Both now...* More honorable than the Cherubim... In the name of the Lord, bless, father.

**Priest:** Through the prayers of our holy fathers... *And he reads the prayer of Saint Basil:*[^125]
O Master, God the Father Almighty, Lord, Only-begotten Son Jesus Christ, and Holy Spirit: one Godhead, one Power, have mercy on me, a sinner; and by the judgments which Thou knowest, save me, Thine unworthy servant; for blessed art Thou unto ages of ages. Amen.
*Then: Come, let us worship (thrice).*
*Psalm 69:*[^126]
O God, give ear unto my help; O Lord, make haste to help me.
Let them be put to shame and confounded that seek after my soul.
Let them be turned backward and brought to shame that desire evils against me.
Let them be turned back immediately, being ashamed, that say unto me: Well done! Well done!
Let all those that seek Thee, O God, rejoice and be glad in Thee; and let them say continually: Let the Lord be magnified, even they that love Thy salvation.
But I am poor and needy; O God, help me.
Thou art my helper and my deliverer, O Lord, make no tarrying.
*Psalm 142:*[^127]
O Lord, hear my prayer; give ear to my supplication in Thy truth; hear me in Thy righteousness.
And enter not into judgment with Thy servant, for in Thy sight shall no man living be justified.
For the enemy has persecuted my soul; he has brought down my life into the ground;

He has made me to dwell in darkness, like those that have been long dead; and my spirit was in anguish within me, my heart was troubled within me.
I remembered the days of old, I meditated on all Thy works, I pondered on the creations of Thy hands.
I stretched forth my hands unto Thee; my soul is unto Thee as a waterless land.
Quickly hear me, O Lord; my spirit has failed.
Turn not away Thy face from me, lest I be like unto them that go down into the pit.
Cause me to hear Thy mercy in the morning, for in Thee have I hoped.
Make known to me, O Lord, the way wherein I should walk, for unto Thee have I lifted up my soul.
Rescue me from mine enemies, O Lord; unto Thee have I fled for refuge; teach me to do Thy will, for Thou art my God.
Thy good Spirit shall lead me into the land of uprightness; for Thy name's sake, O Lord, Thou shalt quicken me in Thy righteousness.
And in Thy righteousness Thou shalt bring my soul out of distress, and in Thy mercy Thou shalt destroy mine enemies,
And Thou shalt destroy all them that afflict my soul, for I am Thy servant.
Hear me, O Lord, in Thy righteousness, and enter not into judgment with Thy servant.
*Likewise we say:*
*The Small Doxology:*[^128]
Glory to God in the highest, and on earth peace, good will among men.

We praise Thee, we bless Thee, we worship Thee, we glorify Thee.
We give thanks unto Thee for Thy great glory, O Lord, Heavenly King.
O God the Father Almighty, O Lord the Only-begotten Son, Jesus Christ, and Holy Spirit, O Lord God.
Lamb of God, Son of the Father,
Who takest away the sin of the world, have mercy on us.
Who takest away the sins of the world, receive our prayers.
Thou that sittest at the right hand of the Father, have mercy on us.
For Thou only art holy, Thou only art the Lord, Jesus Christ, to the glory of God the Father. Amen.
Every day will I bless Thee, and I will praise Thy name forever, and unto the age of the age.
Lord, Thou hast been our refuge in generation and generation.
I said: Lord, have mercy on me; heal my soul, for I have sinned against Thee.
Lord, unto Thee have I fled for refuge; teach me to do Thy will, for Thou art my God.
For with Thee is the fountain of life; in Thy light shall we see light.
Continue Thy mercy unto them that know Thee.
Vouchsafe, O Lord, to keep us this night without sin.
Blessed art Thou, O Lord, the God of our fathers, and praised and glorified is Thy name forever. Amen.
Let Thy mercy, O Lord, be upon us, according as we have hoped in Thee.

Blessed art [Thou], O Lord; teach us Thy statutes.[^129]
Blessed art Thou, O Master; make us understand Thy statutes.
Blessed art Thou, O Holy One; enlighten us with Thy statutes.
Lord, Thy mercy is forever; despise not the works of Thy hands.
To Thee belongs praise, to Thee belongs song.
To Thee belongs glory: to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit,
Now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.
*Thereafter we go out into the Narthex and sing:*
*Stichera of the Litya:*[^130]
*Tone 1. Idiomela:*
Let heaven and earth prophetically rejoice today; let Angels and men spiritually make festival; for God has appeared in the flesh to those who sit in darkness and shadow. Born of the Virgin, the cave and the manger received Him; shepherds proclaim the wonder; Magi from the East bring gifts to Bethlehem; and we with unworthy lips, like Angels, offer praise to Him: Glory to God in the highest, and on earth peace; for the Expectation of the nations is come, and having arrived, He has saved us from the bondage of the enemy.
Heaven and earth are joined today, Christ being born! Today God is come upon earth, and man is gone up into [the] heavens; today He Who is invisible by nature is seen in the flesh for the sake of man. Therefore we also, glorifying, cry unto Him: Glory to God in the highest, and on earth peace; for Thy coming has granted [peace], O our Savior: glory to Thee!
Glory to God in the highest, I hear today in Bethlehem from the bodiless ones, on earth peace unto Him Who was well-pleased that it should be; now the Virgin is more spacious than the heavens; for light has shone forth upon them that were darkened, and has exalted the humble, who sing like the Angels: Glory to God in the highest!
Jesus, seeing him who was in the image and likeness corrupted through transgression, bowed the heavens and came down, and took up His abode in a virginal womb without change, that therein He might renew corrupted Adam, who cries: Glory to Thy manifestation, O my Deliverer and God!
*Glory... Tone 5:*[^131]
The Persian magi kings, having clearly recognized the heavenly King born upon earth, led by a bright star, arrived in Bethlehem, bearing chosen gifts: gold, and frankincense, and myrrh; and falling down they worshipped; for in the cave they saw the ageless Babe lying.
*Both now... Tone 6:*[^132]
All the Angels dance in heaven, and men rejoice today; and all creation leaps for joy on account of the Savior and Lord born in Bethlehem; for all idol deception has ceased, and Christ reigns unto the ages.

**Priest:** *reads this litany:*[^133]
Save, O God, Thy people, and bless Thine inheritance; visit Thy world with mercy and compassions; exalt the horn of Christians, and send down upon us Thy rich mercies: through the intercessions of our all-pure Lady, the Theotokos and Ever-Virgin Mary; by the power of the precious and life-creating Cross; through the protection of the honorable heavenly bodiless Hosts; of the honorable and glorious Prophet, Forerunner, and Baptist John; of the holy, glorious, and all-praised Apostles; and of our fathers among the saints and great ecumenical Teachers and Hierarchs: Basil the Great, Gregory the Theologian, and John Chrysostom; Athanasius, and Cyril; and of our father among the saints Nicholas, Wonderworker of Myra in Lycia; and the Holy Hieromartyr Josaphat; of the holy, glorious, and victorious Martyrs; of our venerable and God-bearing fathers; of the holy and righteous Ancestors of God, Joachim and Anna; and of our venerable and God-bearing fathers Anthony and Theodosius of the Caves; and of Saint (whose temple it is, or day); and of all Thy Saints: we pray Thee, O plenteously-merciful Lord, hear us sinners praying unto Thee, and have mercy on us.
**Choir:** Lord, have mercy (12 times).
Again we pray for our most holy universal Pontiff (Name), the Pope of Rome, and for our right-believing Emperor (Name); for their dominion, victory, endurance, peace, health, salvation, and the remission of their sins; and that—[^134] the Lord our God may speed and assist them in all things, and subdue under their feet every enemy and adversary.
**Choir:** Lord, have mercy (12 times).
Again we pray for our Most Reverend Archbishop, our Metropolitan (Name), and for our God-loving Bishop (Name) (and monks [add]: and for our Most Venerable Father, our Protoarchimandrite [Name], or Superior [Name], Hegumen [Name]), and for our spiritual fathers, and for all our brethren in Christ; and for every Christian soul that is afflicted and oppressed, needing the mercy and help of God; for the protection of this holy monastery and those who dwell therein; for the peace and good ordering of the whole world; for the stable standing of the holy Churches of God; for the salvation and help of our fathers and brethren who labor and serve with diligence and the fear of God; for those who are absent and on journeys; for the healing of those who lie in infirmities; for the repose of blessed memory and the remission of sins of all our fathers and brethren who have fallen asleep before us, who lie here and orthodox everywhere; for the deliverance of captives; for our brethren in public service; and for all who serve and have served in this holy monastery: let us all say.
**Choir:** Kyrie eleison.
**Priest:**[^135] Hear us, O God our Savior, the Hope of all the ends of the earth and of those far off upon the sea; and, O Merciful One, be merciful unto our sins, and have mercy on us; for Thou art a merciful God and lover of mankind, and unto Thee do we send up glory: to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, now and ever, and unto ages of ages.
**Choir:** Amen.
**Priest:** Peace be unto all.
**Choir:** And to thy spirit.
**Priest:** Bow your heads unto the Lord.
**Choir:** To Thee, O Lord.
*And the Priest reads this prayer aloud:*[^136]
O Master plenteously merciful, Lord Jesus Christ our God: through the prayers of our all-pure Lady, the Theotokos and Ever-Virgin Mary; by the power of the precious and life-creating Cross; through the protections of the honorable heavenly bodiless Hosts; of the honorable and glorious Prophet, Forerunner, and Baptist John; of the holy, glorious, and all-praised Apostles; of the holy and victorious Martyrs; of our venerable and God-bearing fathers; and of our fathers among the saints, the great ecumenical Teachers and Hierarchs: Basil the Great, Gregory the Theologian, John Chrysostom, Athanasius, and Cyril; and of our father among the saints Nicholas, Wonderworker of Myra in Lycia; and the Holy Hieromartyr Josaphat; of the holy and righteous Ancestors of God, Joachim and Anna; and of our venerable and God-bearing fathers Anthony and Theodosius of the Caves; (and of Saint [Name], whose temple it is, or day); and of all Thy Saints: make our prayer acceptable; grant us the remission of our transgressions;

shelter us under the cover of Thy wings; drive away from us every enemy and adversary; make our life peaceful, O Lord; have mercy on us and on Thy world, and save our souls, for Thou art good and the lover of mankind.
**Choir:** Amen.
*At the Aposticha, Stichera Idiomela:*[^137]
*Tone 2:*
A great and most glorious wonder is accomplished today! A Virgin gives birth, and her womb suffers no corruption; the Word is incarnate, and is not separated from the Father! Angels with shepherds give glory, and we with them cry out: Glory to God in the highest, and on earth peace!
*Tone 2:*
*Verse:* Out of the womb before the morning star have I begotten Thee: the Lord has sworn, and will not repent.[^138]
Today the Virgin gives birth to the Creator of all; Eden offers a cave, and a star reveals Christ the Sun to those who are in darkness. With gifts the Magi bowed down in worship, enlightened by faith; and the shepherds saw the wonder, while Angels sang and said: Glory to God in the highest!
*The same Tone:*
*Verse:* The Lord said unto my Lord: Sit Thou at My right hand.[^139]
When the Lord Jesus was born in Bethlehem of Judea, Magi having arrived from the East worshipped God made man, and eagerly opening their treasures, they offered honorable gifts: refined gold, as to the King of the ages; and frankincense, as to the God of all; and as to one three days dead, myrrh to the Immortal One! All you nations, come, let us bow down in worship unto Him Who is born to save our souls.
*Glory... Tone 4:*[^140]
Rejoice, O Jerusalem! Make festival, all you who love Zion! Today the temporal bond of Adam's condemnation is loosed; paradise is opened unto us, and the serpent is abolished! For her whom he deceived of old, he now sees become the Mother of the Creator. O the depth of the riches and wisdom and knowledge of God! The vessel of sin that brought death upon all flesh has become the beginning of salvation for the whole world, for the sake of the Theotokos; for a babe is born of her, the all-perfect God, and by His birth He seals her virginity; loosing the bonds of sins by swaddling bands, and through His infancy healing the sorrows of Eve that were in travail. Let all creation therefore keep festival and leap for joy; for Christ is come to renew it, and to save our souls.
*Both now... The same Tone:*[^141]
Thou didst take up Thy dwelling in a cave, O Christ God; a manger received Thee, and shepherds and Magi worshipped. Then indeed was the prophetic preaching fulfilled; and the Angelic Hosts marveled, crying out and saying: Glory to Thy condescension, O only Lover of mankind!
*Thereafter we sing:*
Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart in peace, O Master, according to Thy word;
For mine eyes have seen Thy salvation, Which Thou hast prepared before the face of all peoples;
A light to the revelation of the Gentiles, and the glory of Thy people Israel.
*Trisagion, and after Our Father:*
*Troparion, Tone 4:*[^142]
Thy Nativity, O Christ... (3 times) (see p. 75)[^143]
**Priest:** Let us pray to the Lord.
**Choir:** Lord, have mercy.
*The Priest reads this prayer:*[^144]
O Lord Jesus Christ our God, Who didst bless the five loaves and fill the five thousand (people): do Thou Thyself (now also, O Master) bless † these loaves, the wheat, † the wine, † and the oil; † and multiply them in this holy monastery, and in this Thy world (always), and sanctify Thy faithful who partake of them. For Thou art He Who blesses and sanctifies all things, O Christ our God, and unto Thee do we send up glory, with Thine unoriginate Father, and Thine all-holy, good, and life-creating Spirit, now and ever, and unto ages of ages.
**Choir:** Amen.
*Then:*
Blessed be the name of the Lord, from this time forth and forevermore (3 times).
*Psalm 33:*[^145]
I will bless the Lord at all times; His praise shall continually be in my mouth.

My soul shall make her boast in the Lord; let the meek hear and be glad.
O magnify the Lord with me, and let us exalt His name together.
I sought the Lord, and He heard me, and delivered me from all my tribulations.
Draw near unto Him, and be enlightened, and your faces shall not be ashamed.
This poor man cried, and the Lord heard him, and saved him out of all his tribulations.
The Angel of the Lord will encamp round about them that fear Him, and will deliver them.
O taste and see that the Lord is good; blessed is the man that hopes in Him.
O fear the Lord, all you His saints; for there is no want to them that fear Him.
The rich have become poor and hungry; but they that seek the Lord shall not want any good thing.
*The Priest blesses the people, saying:*
The bless†ing of the Lord be upon you, by His grace and love for mankind, always, now and ever, and unto ages of ages.
**Choir:** Amen.
*And immediately we begin:*

## Matins on the Nativity of Christ

*The Priest begins:*[^146]
**Priest:** Glory to the holy, consubstantial, life-creating, and undivided Trinity, always, now and ever, and unto ages of ages.
**Choir:** Amen.

Glory to God in the highest, and on earth peace, good will among men (3 times).
O Lord, Thou shalt open my lips, and my mouth shall declare Thy praise (twice).
*Psalm 3:*[^147]
Lord, why are they multiplied that afflict me? Many rise up against me.
Many say unto my soul: There is no salvation for him in his God.
But Thou, O Lord, art my helper, my glory, and the lifter up of my head.
I cried unto the Lord with my voice, and He heard me out of His holy mountain.
I laid me down and slept; I awoke, for the Lord will help me.
I will not be afraid of ten thousands of people that set themselves against me round about. Arise, O Lord; save me, O my God!
For Thou hast smitten all who without cause are mine enemies; Thou hast broken the teeth of sinners.
Salvation is of the Lord, and Thy blessing is upon Thy people.
I laid me down and slept; I awoke, for the Lord will help me.
*Psalm 37:*[^148]
O Lord, rebuke me not in Thine anger, neither chasten me in Thy wrath.
For Thine arrows stick fast in me, and Thou hast pressed Thy hand upon me.
There is no health in my flesh from the face of Thine anger; neither is there any peace in my bones from the face of my sins.

For mine iniquities are gone over my head; as a heavy burden they have become heavy upon me.
My wounds stank and are corrupt from the face of my foolishness.
I was troubled and bowed down greatly; I went mourning all the day long.
For my loins are filled with illusions, and there is no health in my flesh.
I was afflicted and humbled exceedingly; I roared by reason of the disquietness of my heart.
Lord, all my desire is before Thee, and my groaning is not hidden from Thee.
My heart was troubled, my strength failed me, and as for the light of mine eyes, it also is not with me.
My friends and my neighbors drew near and stood over against me;
And my kinsmen stood afar off, and they that sought my life used violence;
And they that sought evils for me spoke vain things, and meditated deceits all the day long.
But I, as a deaf man, heard not; and I was as a dumb man that opens not his mouth.
And I was as a man that hears not, and that has no reproofs in his mouth.
For in Thee, O Lord, have I hoped; Thou wilt hear, O Lord my God.
For I said: Lest mine enemies should rejoice over me; and when my feet slipped, they magnified themselves against me.
For I am ready for scourges, and my sorrow is continually before me.

For I will declare mine iniquity, and take thought for my sin.
But mine enemies live, and are grown stronger than I; and they that hate me wrongfully are multiplied.
They that render evil for good have slandered me, because I followed goodness.
Forsake me not, O Lord my God; depart not from me.
Be attentive unto my help, O Lord of my salvation.
*Repeat:*
Forsake me not, O Lord my God; depart not from me.
Be attentive unto my help, O Lord of my salvation.
*Psalm 62:*[^149]
O God, my God, unto Thee do I rise early at dawn.
My soul has thirsted for Thee, how many times has my flesh longed for Thee!
In a barren, pathless, and dry land, thus have I appeared before Thee in the sanctuary, to see Thy power and Thy glory.
For Thy mercy is better than life; my lips shall praise Thee.
Thus will I bless Thee in my life, and in Thy name will I lift up my hands.
My soul shall be filled as with marrow and fatness, and with lips of rejoicing shall my mouth praise Thee.
If I remembered Thee upon my bed, I meditated on Thee at dawn; for Thou hast been my helper, And in the shelter of Thy wings will I rejoice; [my soul has cleaved] after Thee; Thy right hand has upheld me.
But they that vainly sought after my soul shall go into the nethermost parts of the earth; they shall be delivered into the hands of the sword; they shall be a portion for foxes.
But the king shall rejoice in God; everyone that swears by Him shall be praised; for the mouth of them that speak unrighteous things has been stopped.
*Repeat:*
I meditated on Thee at dawn; for Thou hast been my helper.
And in the shelter of Thy wings will I rejoice; my soul has cleaved after Thee; [Thy] right hand has upheld me.[^150]
Glory... Both now... Alleluia (3 times). Lord, have mercy (3 times).
Glory... Both now... Psalm 87:[^151]
O Lord God of my salvation, I have cried day and night before Thee.
Let my prayer come before Thee; incline Thine ear unto my supplication.
For my soul is full of evils, and my life has drawn near unto Hades.
I am counted with them that go down into the pit; I am become as a man without help, free among the dead;
Like the pierced that sleep in the grave, whom Thou rememberest no more; and they are cast off from Thy hand.
They laid me in the lowest pit, in dark places and in the shadow of death.
Thy wrath lies hard upon me, and Thou hast brought all Thy waves upon me.

Thou hast put mine acquaintances far from me; they have made me an abomination unto themselves.
I was delivered up, and I came not forth; mine eyes were weakened from poverty.
I have cried unto Thee, O Lord, all the day long; I have stretched forth my hands unto Thee.
Wilt Thou work marvels for the dead? Or shall physicians raise them up, that they may give thanks unto Thee?
Shall any declare Thy mercy in the grave, and Thy truth in destruction?
Shall Thy marvels be known in the dark, and Thy righteousness in the land of forgetfulness?
But unto Thee, O Lord, have I cried, and in the morning shall my prayer anticipate Thee.
Lord, why dost Thou cast off my soul, and turn away Thy face from me?
I am poor and in labors from my youth; and having been lifted up, I was humbled and brought to exhaustion.
Thy fiercest wrath has come upon me; Thy terrors have troubled me.
They surrounded me like water all the day long; they encompassed me together.
Thou hast put far from me friend and companion, and mine acquaintances from misery.
*Repeat:*
O Lord God of my salvation, I have cried day and night before Thee.
Let my prayer come before Thee; incline Thine ear unto my supplication.
*Psalm 102:*[^152]
Bless the Lord, O my soul, and all that is within me bless His holy name.
Bless the Lord, O my soul, and forget not all His benefits:

Who forgives all thine iniquities, Who heals all thy diseases;
Who redeems thy life from corruption, Who crowns thee with mercy and compassion;
Who satisfies thy desire with good things; thy youth shall be renewed like the eagle’s.
The Lord works deeds of mercy, and executing judgment for all that are oppressed.
He made known His ways unto Moses, His will unto the sons of Israel.
Compassionate and merciful is the Lord, long-suffering and plenteous in mercy.
He will not always be angry, neither will He be wrathful unto the age.
He has not dealt with us according to our iniquities, nor rewarded us according to our sins.
For according to the height of heaven from the earth, the Lord has confirmed His mercy over them that fear Him.
As far as the east is from the west, so far has He removed our iniquities from us.
Like as a father has compassion on his children, so has the Lord compassion on them that fear Him; for He knows our frame.
He remembered that we are dust; as for man, his days are as the grass; as a flower of the field, so shall he flourish.
For the spirit passes through in him, and he shall not be; and he shall know his place no more.
But the mercy of the Lord is from everlasting, upon them that fear Him, And His righteousness unto children’s children, unto them that keep His covenant, and remember His commandments to do them.
The Lord has prepared His throne in heaven, and His kingdom rules over all.
Bless the Lord, all you His angels, mighty in strength, that perform His word, to hear the voice of His words.
Bless the Lord, all you His hosts, you ministers of His that do His will.
Bless the Lord, all you His works, in every place of His dominion; bless the Lord, O my soul!
In every place of His dominion, bless the Lord, O my soul!
*Psalm 142:*[^153]
Lord, hear my prayer... (see p. 80)[^154]
Glory... Both now... Alleluia (3 times).
*After this the priest reads the Litany: In peace let us pray to the Lord (see p. 47).*[^155]
*After the Litany, in Tone 4:*
God is the Lord, and has appeared unto us; blessed is He that comes in the name of the Lord.
*Verse:* Give thanks unto the Lord, for He is good, for His mercy endures forever.
*Verse:* Surrounding me they encompassed me, and by the name of the Lord I warded them off.
*Verse:* I shall not die, but live, and declare the works of the Lord.
*Verse:* The stone which the builders rejected, the same is become the head of the corner; this is from the Lord, and it is marvelous in our eyes.

*After "God is the Lord," the Troparion, Tone 4:*[^156]
Thy Nativity, O Christ our God, has shone upon the world the light of wisdom; for by it they that served the stars were taught by a star to worship Thee, the Sun of righteousness, and to know Thee, the Orient from on high: O Lord, glory to Thee (twice).
*Then, Glory... Both now... and again:* Thy Nativity...
*After the first Kathisma reading, the Little Litany with the exclamation:* For Thine is the dominion: *then the Sessional Hymn, Tone 1. Podoben:* While Thy tomb, O Savior...[^157]
In a manger for the sake of us irrational ones wast Thou laid, O long-suffering Savior, having become an infant of Thine own will; and the shepherds sang Thy praise with the angels, crying: Glory and praise to Him Who was born on earth, and Who has deified the nature of earthborn mortals, Christ our God!
*Glory... Both now... again the same.*
*After the second Kathisma reading, the Little Litany with the exclamation:* For Thou art good: *and the Sessional Hymn, Tone 3. Podoben:* Seeing the beauty of thy virginity...[^158]
The pre-eternal and incomprehensible One, co-everlasting with the invisible Father, hast thou bodily borne in thy womb, O Mother of God, the one and unconfused Godhead of the Trinity; thy grace has shone forth, hymned in the world; therefore we cry out unceasingly: Rejoice, pure Virgin Mother!
*Glory... Both now... again the same.*
*After this, the Polyeleos:*[^159]
Praise the name of the Lord; praise the Lord, O you His servants: Alleluia (3 times).
You that stand in the temple of the Lord, in the courts of the house of our God: Alleluia (3 times).

O Lord, Thy name endures forever, and Thy memorial unto generation and generation: Alleluia (3 times).
Blessed be the Lord out of Zion, Who dwells in Jerusalem: Alleluia (3 times).
Give thanks unto the Lord, for He is good: Alleluia; for His mercy endures forever: Alleluia.
To Him Who established the earth upon the waters: Alleluia; for His mercy endures forever: Alleluia.
And has delivered us from our enemies: Alleluia; for His mercy endures forever: Alleluia.
Give thanks unto the God of heaven, for He is good: Alleluia; for His mercy endures forever: Alleluia.
*Again, the Megalynarion:*[^160]
We magnify Thee, O Christ Giver of Life, Who for our sake art now born in the flesh of the unwedded and all-pure Virgin Mary.
*Verse:* Shout with jubilation unto the Lord, all the earth; chant now unto His name, give glory unto His praise.
The Lord has sent redemption unto His people.
He shall call upon Me: Thou art My Father.
And I will make Him My firstborn, higher than the kings of the earth.
And all the kings of the earth shall worship Him.
Out of the womb before the morning star have I begotten Thee; the Lord has sworn, and will not repent.
The Lord said unto Me: Thou art My Son, this day have I begotten Thee.
Blessed be the Lord forever. So be it, so be it.
Glory... Both now... Alleluia (3 times).

Come, with angelic choirs let us cry aloud: Glory to God in the highest, to Him Who is born in the cave!
*After this, the Little Litany with the exclamation:* For holy art Thou: *and the Sessional Hymn, Tone 4. Podoben:* Joseph was amazed...[^161]
Come, O you faithful, let us behold where Christ was born; let us follow where the star goes with the Magi, kings of the East; Whom the angels hymn there unceasingly, and the shepherds pipe a worthy song, saying: Glory in the highest to Him Who is born this day in the cave of the Virgin and Theotokos in Bethlehem of Judea!
*Glory... Both now... again the same.*
*After this, the Graduals, the first Antiphon of Tone 4:*[^162]
From my youth many passions war against me; but do Thou Thyself help me and save me, O my Savior.
You that hate Zion, be put to shame from the Lord; for you shall be withered up like grass by fire.
Glory...
By the Holy Spirit every soul is quickened and exalted in purity, radiant in the unity of the Trinity mystically.
Both now...
By the Holy Spirit the streams of grace gush forth, watering all creation unto life.
**Priest:** Let us be attentive! *And the Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*[^163]
Out of the womb before the morning star have I begotten Thee; the Lord has sworn, and will not repent.

*Verse:* The Lord said unto my Lord: Sit Thou at My right hand, until I make Thine enemies the footstool of Thy feet.
Let everything that has breath praise the Lord.
*Verse:* Praise God in His saints.
*The Reading from the Holy Gospel according to Matthew (Pericope 2):*[^164]
The birth of Jesus Christ was on this wise: When as His mother Mary was espoused to Joseph, before they came together, she was found with child of the Holy Spirit. Then Joseph her husband, being a just man, and not willing to make her a public example, was minded to put her away privily. But while he thought on these things, behold, the angel of the Lord appeared unto him in a dream, saying: Joseph, thou son of David, fear not to take unto thee Mary thy wife; for that which is conceived in her is of the Holy Spirit. And she shall bring forth a Son, and thou shalt call His name Jesus, for He shall save His people from their sins. Now all this was done, that it might be fulfilled which was spoken of the Lord by the prophet, saying: Behold, a virgin shall be with child, and shall bring forth a Son, and they shall call His name Emmanuel, which being interpreted is, God with us. Then Joseph, being raised from sleep, did as the angel of the Lord had bidden him, and took unto him his wife; and knew her not till she had brought forth her firstborn Son; and he called His name Jesus.
*After Psalm 50, Glory:*
All things are filled with joy today; Christ is born of the Virgin.
*Both now:*
All things are filled with joy today; Christ is born in Bethlehem.

*Then:*
Have mercy on me, O God, according to Thy great mercy, and according to the multitude of Thy compassions blot out my transgression.
*And the Sticheron, Tone 6:*[^165]
Glory to God in the highest, and on earth peace; today Bethlehem receives Him Who sits ever with the Father; today the angels praise in godlike manner the newborn Infant: Glory to God in the highest, and on earth peace, good will among men!
*The Canons:*[^166]
*There are two Canons; the Heirmoi of both Canons are chanted twice, and the Troparia to twelve.*
*First Canon, Tone 1. Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^167]
Christ is born, give glory! Christ comes from heaven, go forth to meet Him! Christ is upon earth, be lifted up! Sing unto the Lord, all the earth, and praise Him with gladness, O you peoples; for He has been glorified!
Him who was made in the image of God, but had fallen into corruption through transgression, and having entirely fallen away from the higher divine life was subject to decay, the wise Creator renews once again; for He has been glorified!
Seeing man perishing, whom He had made with His own hands, the Creator bowed the heavens and came down; and He took all his nature from a pure, divine Virgin, having truly become incarnate; for He has been glorified!
The Wisdom, Word, and Power, being the Son of the Father and His Radiance, Christ God, hidden from the powers as many as are above the world and as many as are upon earth, and having become man has renewed us; for He has been glorified!
*Second Canon, the same Tone. Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^168]
Working marvels, the Master saved the people, of old turning the watery wave of the sea into dry land; and having of His own will been born of a Virgin, He sets for us a path leading to heaven; Whom, equal in essence to both the Father and to men, we glorify.
The sanctified womb clearly brought forth the Word, prefigured unburnt by the bush: God mingled with human form, releasing Eve’s wretched womb from the bitter ancient curse; Whom we earthborn glorify.
To the Magi the star clearly showed the Word, Who was before the sun, come to put an end to sins, in a poor cave, Thee the merciful One wrapped in swaddling clothes, Whom rejoicing they beheld as Himself both man and Lord.
*Katavasia: The Heirmos of the first Canon by the first choir, and of the second by the second choir.*
*Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^169]
Unto the Son, begotten of the Father incorruptibly before the ages, and in these last times incarnate of the Virgin without seed, unto Christ God let us cry aloud: Thou Who hast exalted our horn, holy art Thou, O Lord!
Adam, formed of dust, having partaken of the higher inbreathing, yet slipped into decay through woman’s deceit, seeing Christ born of a woman, cries out: Thou Who for my sake hast become like unto me, holy art Thou, O Lord!
Having conformed Thyself to our lowly clay, O Christ, through the mingling, and by partaking of the baser flesh having imparted of the divine nature, earthly becoming, yet remaining God; and having exalted our horn, holy art Thou, O Lord!
Be glad, O Bethlehem, being king of the princes of Judah; for He that shepherds Israel, Who rides upon the shoulders of the Cherubim, has manifestly come forth from thee, Christ; and having exalted our horn, He has reigned over all.
*Another Canon. Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^170]
Look down upon the hymns of Thy servants, O Benefactor, humbling the exalted pride of the enemy; and do Thou Who seest all things, O Good One, establish Thy singers high above sin, unmoved upon the foundation of faith.
Accounted worthy beyond understanding to behold the most bountiful childbirth of the all-pure Bride, the choir of them that piped bowed down in marvelous manner; and the rank of the bodiless hosts sang of Christ the King, incarnate without seed.
Reigning in the height of the heavens, by His tender mercy He accomplishes salvation for us, from the unwedded Maiden, being immaterial before; but in these last days the Word took gross flesh, that He might draw unto Himself fallen Adam, the first-formed man.
*Hypakoe, Tone 8:*[^171]
The firstfruits of the nations did heaven bring unto Thee, lying an infant in the manger, having summoned the Magi by a star; whom neither scepters nor thrones amazed, but the uttermost poverty: for what is meaner than a cave? What is lowlier than swaddling clothes? Yet in them shone forth the riches of Thy divinity: O Lord, glory to Thee!

*First Canon, Tone 1. Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^172]
Rod of the stem of Jesse, and blossom from it, O Christ, Thou hast budded forth from the Virgin; from the praised, overshadowed mountain, from the dense forest, Thou hast come, having become incarnate from the unwedded Maiden, Thou immaterial and God: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!
Thou Whom of old Jacob foretold, the Expectation of the nations, O Christ, hast shone forth from the tribe of Judah; and Thou hast come to overthrow the power of Damascus and the spoils of Samaria, changing deceit into faith worthy of God: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!
By the words of the diviner Balaam of old, Thou hast filled with joy the wise stargazers, his disciples; having shone forth as a star out of Jacob, O Master, and taking the firstfruits of the nations led in, Thou didst manifestly receive them: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!
As rain upon the fleece, O Christ, Thou didst come down into the Virgin’s womb, and as drops falling upon the earth; and Ethiopia, and Tarshish, and the Arabian isles, Sheba, and they that hold the whole land of the Medes fell down before Thee, O Savior: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!
*Another Canon. Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^173]
Singing of old the renewal of the human race, the Prophet Habakkuk foretold it, having been accounted worthy to behold the ineffable type: for as a young Babe from the mountain, the Virgin, came forth the Word, unto the renewal of the peoples.
Equal to men came forth the Most High, willingly taking flesh from the Virgin, to purge the poison of the serpent's head, bringing all from sunless gates unto the life-bearing light, being God.

The nations that of old were sunken in corruption, having wholly escaped the ruin of the enemy, lift up their hands with songs of praise, honoring Christ alone, as One Who has come in mercy.
Having sprung from the root of Jesse, O Virgin, thou hast passed beyond the bounds of human nature, having borne the Father's eternal Word, as He Himself was pleased to pass through a sealed womb by a strange emptying.
*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^174]
Being the God of peace and Father of compassions, Thou hast sent unto us the Angel of Thy Great Counsel, Who grants peace; therefore, guided unto the light of divine knowledge, waking early out of the night, we glorify Thee, O Lover of mankind.
Submitting to be enrolled among servants by the decree of Caesar, Thou hast set us free who were the slaves of the enemy and of sin, O Christ; and becoming wholly poor according to our condition, from the very union and communion Thou hast made earthly man divine.
Behold, as it was said of old, the Virgin, having conceived in her womb, has given birth to God incarnate, and remains a Virgin; reconciled through her to God, let us sinners, the faithful, praise her as truly the Mother of God.
*Another Canon. Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^175]
Out of the night of the dark works of deceit, as cleansing unto us who now watchfully offer song unto Thee as Benefactor, come, O Christ, granting an easy path, ascending along which we shall find glory.

Cutting off again the cruel enmity against us by Thy coming in the flesh, O Master, that Thou mightest destroy the soul-destroying ruler, uniting the world with the immaterial essences, Thou hast made the Parent accessible to creation.
The people that of old were darkened have seen after days the light of the brightness from on high; and as an inheritance unto God the Son brings the Gentiles, granting ineffable grace there where sin abounded most.
*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^176]
The sea beast spewed forth Jonah from its belly like a babe, just as it had received him; and the Word, having dwelt within the Virgin and taken flesh, came forth, preserving her incorrupt; for suffering no corruption Himself, He kept her who bore Him unharmed.
Incarnate came Christ our God, Whom the Father begat from the womb before the morning star; and holding the governance of the all-pure hosts, He lies in a manger of dumb beasts and is wrapped in swaddling bands, yet He looses the tangled bonds of transgressions.
As a young Child from Adam, a Son is born of our mingled nature and given unto the faithful; He is the Father and Ruler of the age to come, and is called the Angel of Great Counsel; He is the mighty God, holding dominion over all creation.
*Another Canon. Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^177]
Dwelling in the depths of the sea, Jonah prayed that Thou wouldest come and calm the tempest; and pierced by the dart of the tormentor, I cry aloud to Christ, the Destroyer of evils, that Thou wouldest come swiftly unto my slothfulness.
God the Word, Who in the beginning was with God, now strengthens what was feeble of old, having purposed to preserve the nature that belongs to us, wherein by a second communion He straightway makes Himself manifest, free from passions.
For our sake He comes from Abraham's loins to raise up as sons those who had fallen darkly in the gloom of sins, bowed down to the earth; He Who dwells in light is now well pleased, through His condescension, to be laid in a manger for the salvation of mankind.
*Kontakion, Tone 3. Automelon (Самоподо́бенъ):*[^178]
Today the Virgin gives birth to the Transcendent One, and the earth offers a cave to the Inaccessible; angels with shepherds give glory, and Magi journey with a star: for unto us is born a young Child, God before the ages.
*Ikos:*[^179]
Bethlehem has opened Eden, come, let us behold; we have found delight in secret: come, let us receive the things of paradise within the cave; there an unwatered root has appeared, budding forth remission; there an undug well is found, whereof David of old longed to drink; there the Virgin, having given birth to an Infant, straightway quenched the thirst of Adam and David; therefore let us go unto Him where He is born: a young Child, God before the ages.
*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^180]
The Children, brought up together in piety, despising the impious command, were not afraid of the threat of fire;

but standing in the midst of the flame, they sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!
The shepherds playing on their pipes received an awe-inspiring manifestation of light; for the glory of the Lord shone round about them, and the angel cried out: Sing, for Christ is born, the blessed God of our fathers!
Suddenly with the word of the angel, the heavenly hosts cried aloud: Glory to God in the highest, and on earth peace, good will among men! Christ has shone forth, the blessed God of our fathers!
What is this saying? said the shepherds: Having gone, let us see that which is come to pass, the divine Christ; and having reached Bethlehem, with her who bore Him they bowed down, singing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!
*Another Canon. Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^181]
Caught by love for the King of all, the Children reproached the impious, wicked-tongued raving of the tyrant that raged without measure; to them the raging fire yielded as they said to the Master: Blessed art Thou unto the ages!
Fiercely it consumed the servants, but preserved the youths with reverence, the all-devouring furnace heightened by a sevenfold burning; whom the flame crowned, the Lord bounteously granting dew for their piety's sake.
O Christ, Helper of men, possessing an ineffable incarnation, Thou hast put to shame the adversary's boast; bearing the riches of deification, Thou hast now taken form, therefore, through hope from on high, we have come into the nether darkness.
The sin which was wickedly and unrestrainedly exalted, raging impiously through the perversion of the world, which drew us down of old, Thou hast almighty overthrown;

and today from the snares Thou savest us, incarnate of Thine own will, O Benefactor.
*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^182]
The dew-bearing furnace portrayed the type of a supernatural marvel; for it burned not the youths whom it had received, just as the fire of the Godhead burned not the Virgin's womb into which it entered; therefore, singing, let us sing: Let all creation bless the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!
The daughter of Babylon draws unto herself the captive children of David from Zion; but she sends the Magi youth bearing gifts, entreating the God-receiving daughter of David; therefore, singing, let us sing: Let all creation bless the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!
Instruments turned aside mournful songs, for the sons of Zion sang not in a strange land; but Christ, having shone forth in Bethlehem, looses all the deceit of Babylon and musical harmonies; therefore, singing, let us sing: Let all creation bless the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!
Babylon received the spoils of the kingdom of Zion and its captive wealth; but Christ draws unto Zion the treasures thereof, and its kings, the stargazers, guiding them by a star; therefore, singing, let us sing: Let all creation bless the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto all ages!
*Another Canon. Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^183]
The youths burned in the Old Testament prefigure without burning the womb of the Maiden who supernaturally gives birth sealed; grace, accomplishing both as a single miracle, rouses the people to song.
Having escaped the destruction of seeking deification through deceit, all creation unceasingly praises the Word Who emptied Himself, and in youthlike manner with trembling, in fear offers an inglorious praise, being corruptible, even though wisely enduring.
Thou comest turning the strayed unto the flower-bearing pasture from the desert hills, to quell the violent power of the manslayer over the uprising of the nations, human nature; having appeared as man and God by providence.
*At Ode 9, we do not sing "More honorable than the Cherubim", but we sing the Refrains of the Feast.*[^184]
*Magnify, O my soul, her who is more honorable and more glorious than the heavenly hosts, the all-pure Virgin Mother of God.*
*And the Heirmos:*[^185]
A strange and most glorious mystery do I behold: the cave is heaven; the Virgin, the Cherubic Throne; the manger, the receptacle in which Christ God, Whom nothing can contain, has reclined; Whom singing, we magnify.
*Then the second Choir sings the same Refrain and Heirmos.*
*And we chant the remaining six Refrains, once to each Troparion.*
*Magnify, O my soul, God born in the flesh of the Virgin.*
*Magnify, O my soul, the King born in the cave.*
Beholding the extraordinary course of a strange new star newly shining, illuminating the heavens, portending Christ the King born on earth in Bethlehem for our salvation.
*Magnify, O my soul, God adored by the Magi.*
*Magnify, O my soul, Him Who was announced to the Magi by a star.*
When the Magi said: Where is the newborn Child, the King, Whom the star revealed? For we have come to worship Him: Herod raged and was troubled, the God-fighter striving to slay Christ.
*Magnify, O my soul, the pure Virgin and only Mother of God, who bore Christ the King.*
*The Magi and shepherds came to worship Christ, born in the city of Bethlehem.*
Herod inquired the time of the star, guided by which the Magi worshipped Christ with gifts in Bethlehem; and being directed by it unto their fatherland, they left the cruel child-slayer mocked.
*Then the first Choir sings the Refrain of the second Canon:*
*Today the Virgin gives birth to the Master within the cave.*
*And the Heirmos:*[^186]
Easier it were for us out of fear to choose silence as free from danger, whereas to weave harmoniously composed hymns out of love, O Virgin, is difficult; but do thou, O Mother, give us power, equal to our desire.
*And the second Choir sings the Refrain:*
*Today the Master is born as an Infant of the Virgin Mother.*

*The same Heirmos:*
Easier it were for us out of fear to choose silence...
*And we sing the rest of the Refrains to the Troparia.*
*Today the shepherds see the Savior wrapped in swaddling bands, and lying in a manger.*
*Today the Master, the Incomprehensible, is wrapped in rags like an infant.*
*Today all creation makes merry and rejoices, for Christ is born of the Virgin Maiden.*
Seeing the obscure types and shadows passed away, O pure Mother, and the Word newly appeared from closed gates, and deeming the truth brightness, we worthily bless thy womb.
*The heavenly hosts announce to the world the born Savior, Lord, and Master.*
*In place of Glory:*
*Magnify, O my soul, the dominion of the Tri-hypostatic and undivided Godhead.*
*And in place of Both now:*
*Magnify, O my soul, her who has delivered us from the curse.*
Having attained their desire and been accounted worthy of God's coming, the people adorned by Christ now comfort themselves with regeneration, for thou, O pure Virgin, givest life-bearing grace to worship His glory.
*Then both Choirs, coming together, sing the first Refrain of the Feast, and the Heirmos:*
A strange and most glorious mystery do I behold... (see p. 112)[^187] *After this, the Refrain of the second Canon, and the Heirmos:*
Easier it were for us out of fear...[^188]
*Holy is the Lord our God (thrice).*[^189]
*Exapostilarion:*[^190]
Our Savior, the Dayspring from on high, has visited us, and we who were in darkness and the shadow have found the truth; for of the Virgin is born the Lord (thrice).
*Psalm 148:*[^191]
Let everything that has breath praise the Lord. Praise the Lord from the heavens, praise Him in the highest; to Thee is due a hymn, O God.
Praise Him, all His angels, praise Him, all His powers; to Thee is due a hymn, O God.
Praise Him, sun and moon; praise Him, all you stars and light.
Praise Him, you heavens of heavens, and you waters that are above the heavens; let them praise the name of the Lord.
For He spoke, and they were made; He commanded, and they were created.
He established them unto the age, and unto the age of the age; He made a decree, and it shall not pass away.
Praise the Lord from the earth, you dragons and all deeps:
Fire, hail, snow, ice, stormy wind, fulfilling His word;
Mountains and all hills, fruitful trees and all cedars;
Beasts and all cattle, creeping things and winged birds;
Kings of the earth and all peoples, princes and all judges of the earth.
