For Thy mercy, and for Thy truth's sake, lest the heathen should say: Where is now their God?[^787]

But our God is in heaven and on earth; He has done whatsoever He pleased.
The idols of the nations are silver and gold, the work of men's hands.
They have a mouth, and speak not; eyes have they, and see not.
They have ears, and hear not; noses have they, and smell not.
They have hands, and handle not; feet have they, and walk not; neither make they a sound with their throat.
May they that make them become like unto them, and all that trust in them.
The house of Israel has hoped in the Lord; He is their helper and defender.
The house of Aaron has hoped in the Lord; He is their helper and defender.
They that fear the Lord have hoped in the Lord; He is their helper and defender.
The Lord has remembered us, and has blessed us.
He has blessed the house of Israel, He has blessed the house of Aaron.
He has blessed them that fear the Lord, the small with the great.
May the Lord add unto you, unto you and unto your children.
Blessed are ye of the Lord, Who made heaven and earth.
The heaven of heaven belongs to the Lord, but the earth He gave to the sons of men.
The dead shall not praise Thee, O Lord, neither all they that go down into Hades.[^788]

But we who are alive will bless the Lord, from henceforth and unto all ages.[^789]

*Psalm 85:*
Bow down Thine ear, O Lord... (see p. 29)[^790]

*Glory: Troparion, Tone 4:*
The river Jordan once turned back by the mantle of Elisha when Elijah was taken up, and the waters were divided hither and thither, and the wet path became dry ground for him, as a true figure of baptism, by which we pass through the fleeting course of life: Christ has appeared in the Jordan to sanctify the waters.[^791]

*Both now: Theotokion:*
O Good One, Who for our sake wast born of the Virgin and didst endure crucifixion, Who by death didst overthrow death and as God didst manifest the Resurrection: despise not the work which Thou hast fashioned with Thine own hand; manifest Thy love for mankind, O Merciful One; accept the Theotokos that gave Thee birth, who intercedes for us, and save, O our Savior, a despairing people.[^792]

*Then the Troparion, Tone 7:*
Awesome it was to see the Maker of heaven and earth standing naked in the river, receiving baptism from a servant as a servant for our salvation; and the angelic choirs marveled with fear and joy, with whom we worship Thee: save us, O Lord. (Twice)[^793]

*Then the Verse, Tone 2:*
*Verse:* Therefore will I remember Thee from the land of Jordan, and of Hermonim.[^794]

When the Forerunner saw the Lord of glory coming unto him, he cried aloud, beholding Him: Behold, He that delivers the world from corruption is come; He delivers us from tribulation; He grants remission of sins, as God upon earth; He is come from a pure Virgin for the sake of mercy, and instead of slaves makes us sons of God; and instead of darkness He enlightens mankind with the water of His divine baptism. Come therefore, let us with one accord glorify Him, together with the Father and with the Holy Spirit.[^795]

*Verse:* The waters saw Thee, O God, the waters saw Thee, and were afraid.[^796]
*And again the same Troparion.*

*Then both choirs together sing at Glory; Both now: this Sticheron:*
*Tone 2:*
Thy hand that touched the all-pure head of the Master, with which also thou didst point Him out with thy finger unto us, lift up for us unto Him, O Baptist, as one having much boldness; for thou hast been testified by Him to be the greatest of all prophets. And thine eyes that beheld the All-Holy Spirit descending in the likeness of a dove, raise thou likewise unto Him, O Baptist, and make Him merciful unto us; and come, stand with us, setting the seal upon our hymnody and leading our festive triumph.[^797]

*Then the Prokeimenon, Tone 3:*
The Lord is my light and my Savior: whom shall I fear?[^798]
*Verse:* The Lord is the defender of my life: of whom shall I be afraid?[^799]

*The Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (Chapter 49):*
Thus says the Lord: In an acceptable time have I heard thee, and in a day of salvation have I helped thee; and I have formed thee, and given thee for a covenant of the nations, to establish the earth, and to cause to inherit the desolate heritages; saying to them that are in bonds: Go forth; and to them that are in darkness: Shew yourselves. They shall feed in all the ways, and their pasture shall be in all the paths. They shall not hunger, nor shall they thirst, neither shall the heat nor the sun strike them; but He that has mercy on them shall comfort them, and through fountains of waters shall He guide them. And I will make every mountain a way, and every path a pasture for them. Behold, these shall come from afar, and these from the north and from the sea, and others from the land of the Persians. Let the heavens rejoice, and let the earth be glad; let the mountains break forth into joy, and the hills righteousness: for God has had mercy on His people, and has comforted the humble of His people. But Sion said: The Lord has forsaken me, and God has forgotten me. Can a woman forget her child, or not have compassion on the offspring of her womb? Yea, though a woman should forget these, yet will I not forget thee, says the Lord.[^800]

*The Reading from the Epistle of the Holy Apostle Paul to Titus (Pericope 302):*
Child Titus, the grace of God that brings salvation has appeared to all men, teaching us that, denying ungodliness and worldly lusts, we should live soberly, righteously, and godly in this present world, looking for that blessed hope and glorious appearing of the great God and our Savior Jesus Christ, Who gave Himself for us, that He might redeem us from all in- -iquity, and purify unto Himself a chosen people, zealous of good works. But when the kindness and love toward man of God our Savior appeared: not by works of righteousness which we have done, but according to His mercy He saved us, by the washing of regeneration and renewing of the Holy Spirit, Whom He shed on us abundantly through Jesus Christ our Savior; that having been justified by His grace, we should be made heirs according to the hope of eternal life.[^801]

*The Holy Gospel according to Matthew (Pericope 6):*
At that time, Jesus came from Galilee to the Jordan unto John, to be baptized by him. But John forbade Him, saying: I have need to be baptized of Thee, and comest Thou to me? And Jesus answering said unto him: Suffer it to be so now; for thus it becomes us to fulfill all righteousness. Then he suffered Him. And Jesus, when He was baptized, went up immediately out of the water; and behold, the heavens were opened unto Him, and He saw the Spirit of God descending like a dove, and alighting upon Him. And lo, a voice from heaven, saying: This is My beloved Son, in Whom I am well pleased.[^802]

*Then:*
Deliver us not up utterly, for Thy name's sake, and make not void Thy covenant, and take not away Thy mercy from us, for the sake of Abraham Thy beloved, and for the sake of Isaac Thy servant, and Israel Thy holy one.[^803]

*The Trisagion; and after the Our Father:*

*Kontakion, Tone 4:* Being today in the streams... and the rest as on (p. 214);[^804] *Then the Prayer:* Master, Lord Jesus Christ... and the rest as on (pp. 35–41);[^805] *The Trisagion; and after the Our Father:* Being today in the streams... (p. 214);[^806] *Lord, have mercy (40); Prayer:* O All-Holy Trinity... and the rest as on (pp. 42–43).[^807] *And the Dismissal.*

## Vespers Together with the Divine Liturgy on Holy Theophany

*Holy Theophany of our Lord, God, and Savior Jesus Christ.*[^808]
*At Great Vespers:*
*If Theophany falls on a Saturday or on a Sunday, we chant: Blessed is the man... But if on other days, we chant: Lord, I have cried, Tone 2. At Lord, I have cried, we chant 8 Stichera:*[^809]
*Tone 2:*
The Forerunner, beholding our Enlightener, Who enlightens every man, coming to be baptized, rejoices in soul and trembles in hand; he points Him out and says unto the peoples: Behold, He that delivers Israel, Who frees us from corruption; O sinless Christ our God, glory be to Thee! (Twice)[^810]
When our Redeemer was baptized by a servant, and witnessed by the descent of the Spirit, the angelic hosts were filled with awe as they beheld; and a voice was brought from heaven from the Father: This is My beloved Son, Whom the Forerunner baptizes with his hand, in Whom I am well pleased; O Christ our God, glory be to Thee! (Twice)[^811]
The streams of the Jordan received Thee, the Fountain, and the Comforter in the likeness of a dove descen- -ded; He that bowed the heavens bows His head; clay cries out and shouts unto the Maker: What dost Thou command me that is above me? I have need of Thy baptism; O sinless Christ our God, glory be to Thee! (Twice)[^812]

Desiring to save wandering man, Thou didst not disdain to be clothed in the form of a servant; for it became Thee as Master and God to take upon Thyself our nature for our sake; for when Thou wast baptized in the flesh, O Redeemer, Thou didst vouchsafe us forgiveness; therefore we cry out to Thee: O Christ our God, glory be to Thee! (Twice)[^813]

*Glory; Both now, Tone the same:*
Thou didst bow Thy head unto the Forerunner, Thou didst crush the heads of the dragons; coming into the streams, Thou didst enlighten all things, that we may glorify Thee, O Savior, the Enlightener of our souls.[^814]

*Entrance with the Gospel; Prokeimenon of the Day.*[^815]
*The Reading from Genesis (Chapter 1):*
In the beginning God created... (see p. 53)[^816]

*The Reading from Exodus (Chapter 14):*[^817]
The Lord said unto Moses: Why criest thou unto Me? Speak unto the children of Israel, that they journey forward. And take thou thy rod, and stretch out thy hand over the sea, and divide it, and let the children of Israel go into the midst of the sea on dry ground. And behold, I will harden the heart of Pharaoh, and of his servants, and of all the Egyptians, and they shall enter in after them; and I will be glorified in Pharaoh, and in all his host, and in his chariots, and in his horses. And all the Egyptians shall know that I am the Lord, when I am glori- -fied in Pharaoh, and in his chariots, and in his horses. And Moses stretched out his hand over the sea, and the Lord drove back the sea with a strong south wind all that night, and made the sea dry land, and the water was divided. And the children of Israel went into the midst of the sea on dry ground; and the water was a wall unto them on the right hand and on the left. And the Egyptians pursued, and went in after them, even all Pharaoh's horses, and chariots, and horsemen, into the midst of the sea. And Moses stretched out his hand over the sea, and the water returned at break of day unto its place; and the Egyptians fled under the water, and the Lord shook off the Egyptians in the midst of the sea. And the water returned and covered the chariots, and the horsemen, and the captains, and all the host of Pharaoh that came into the sea after them; and there remained not so much as one of them. But the children of Israel walked on dry land in the midst of the sea.[^818]

*The Reading from Exodus (Chapter 15):*[^819]
Moses led the children of Israel from the Red Sea, and brought them into the wilderness of Shur; and they went three days in the wilderness, and found no water to drink. And they came to Marah, and could not drink of the waters of Marah, for they were bitter; therefore the name of that place was called Bitterness. And the people murmured against Moses, saying: What shall we drink? And Moses cried unto the Lord, and the Lord showed him a tree; and he cast it into the water, and the water was made sweet. There God made for Moses ordinances and judgments; and there He proved him, and said: If diligently thou, O Israel, wilt listen to the voice of the Lord thy God, and wilt do what is pleasing before Him, and wilt give ear to His commandments, and wilt keep all His statutes: every disease which I brought upon the Egyptians, I will not bring upon thee; for I am the Lord that heals thee, O Israel. And they came to Elim, and there were twelve springs of water, and seventy palm trees; and they encamped there by the waters. And they departed from Elim, and all the congregation of the children of Israel came into the wilderness of Sin, which is between Elim and Sinai.[^820]

*Troparion, Tone 5:*
Thou Who madest the world hast appeared in the world, that Thou mightest enlighten them that sit in darkness; O Lover of mankind, glory be to Thee![^821]
*Verse:* God be bountiful unto us, and bless us, and show the light of His countenance upon us, and have mercy on us; that we may know Thy way upon the earth, Thy salvation among all nations.[^822]
That Thou mightest enlighten them that sit in darkness, O Lover of mankind, glory be to Thee!
*Verse:* Let the peoples give praise unto Thee, O God, let all the peoples give praise unto Thee. Let the nations be glad and rejoice, for Thou shalt judge the peoples with uprightness, and guide the nations upon the earth.[^823]
That Thou mightest enlighten them that sit in darkness, O Lover of mankind, glory be to Thee!
*Verse:* Let the peoples give praise unto Thee, O God, let all the peoples give praise unto Thee. The earth has yielded her fruit; bless us, O God, our God; bless us, O God, and let all the ends of the earth fear Him.[^824]

That Thou mightest enlighten them that sit in darkness, O Lover of mankind, glory be to Thee!
*Glory; Both now:*
Thou Who madest the world hast appeared in the world, that Thou mightest enlighten them that sit in darkness; O Lover of mankind, glory be to Thee![^825]

*The Reading from Joshua, the Son of Nun (Chapter 3):*
The Lord said unto Joshua: This day do I begin to exalt thee before all the children of Israel, that they may know that, as I was with Moses, so will I be with thee. And now command the priests that bear the ark of the covenant, saying: When ye are come to part of the water of the Jordan, ye shall stand still at the Jordan. And as the priests that bore the ark of the covenant of the Lord went to the Jordan, and the feet of the priests that bore the ark were dipped in the edge of the water of the Jordan—for the Jordan overflows all its banks as in the days of wheat harvest—the waters which came down from above stood and rose up in one heap, far off from the city of Adam, even unto the parts of Kiriath-jearim; and those that went down toward the Arabah Sea, even the Salt Sea, utterly failed; and the people stood over against Jericho. And the priests that bore the ark of the covenant of the Lord stood ready on dry ground in the midst of the Jordan. And all the children of Israel passed over on dry ground, until all the people had finished passing over the Jordan.[^826]

*The Reading from the Fourth Book of Kings (Chapter 2):*
Elijah said unto Elisha: Tarry here, I pray thee, for the Lord has sent me to the Jordan. And Elisha said:[^827]

As the Lord lives, and as thy soul lives, I will not leave thee. And they two went on, and fifty men of the sons of the prophets went, and stood over against them afar off; and they two stood by the Jordan. And Elijah took his mantle, and wrapped it together, and struck the waters, and the water was divided hither and thither, and they two went over on dry ground. And it came to pass, when they were gone over, that Elijah said unto Elisha: Ask what I shall do for thee, before I be taken away from thee. And Elisha said: Let there be, I pray thee, a double portion of thy spirit upon me. And Elijah said: Thou hast asked a hard thing; nevertheless, if thou see me when I am taken from thee, it shall be so unto thee; but if not, it shall not be. And it came to pass, as they were going and talking, behold, there appeared a chariot of fire, and horses of fire, and parted them both asunder; and Elijah was taken up by a whirlwind as it were into heaven. And Elisha saw it, and he cried: My father, my father, the chariot of Israel, and the horsemen thereof! And he saw him no more; and Elisha took hold of his own clothes, and rent them in two pieces. And Elisha took up the mantle of Elijah that fell from off him, and Elisha returned, and stood by the bank of the Jordan. And Elisha took the mantle of Elijah that fell from off him, and struck the waters, and they divided not; and Elisha said: Where now is the God of Elijah, Aphtho? And Elisha struck the waters a second time, and the waters were divided, and he went over on dry ground.[^828]

*The Reading from the Fourth Book of Kings (Chapter 5):*[^829]
Naaman, captain of the king of Syria, came with his chariots and with his horses, and stood at the door of the house of Elisha. And Elisha sent a messenger unto him, saying: Go and wash in the Jordan seven times, and thy flesh shall return unto thee, and thou shalt be clean. But Naaman was wroth, and went away, and said: Behold, I said: The prophet will surely come out to me, and stand, and call on the name of the Lord his God, and put his hand over the place of the leprosy, and remove it from my flesh. Are not Abana and Pharpar, rivers of Damascus, better than the Jordan and all the waters of Israel? May I not go and wash in them, and be clean? And he turned and went away in a rage. And his servants came near, and spoke unto him: Master, if the prophet had bidden thee do some great thing, wouldest thou not have done it, even as he said unto thee: Wash and be clean? And Naaman went down, and dipped himself seven times in the Jordan, according to the word of the man of God; and his flesh returned unto him like the flesh of a little child, and he was cleansed.[^830]

*Troparion, Tone 6:*
Unto sinners and publicans, through the multitude of Thy mercy, hast Thou appeared, O our Savior; for where should Thy light have shone, but upon them that sit in darkness? Glory be to Thee![^831]
*Verse:* The Lord has reigned, He is clothed with majesty; the Lord is clothed with strength and has girded Himself; for He has established the world, which shall not be moved; Thy throne is prepared of old, Thou art from everlasting.[^832]
For where should Thy light have shone, but upon them that sit in darkness? Glory be to Thee!
*Verse:* The rivers have lifted up, O Lord, the rivers have lifted up their voices; the rivers will lift up their waves at the voices of many waters.[^833]

For where should Thy light have shone, but upon them that sit in darkness? Glory be to Thee!
*Verse:* Wonderful are the surges of the sea, marvelous on high is the Lord; Thy testimonies are made exceedingly sure; holiness becomes Thy house, O Lord, unto length of days.[^834]
For where should Thy light have shone, but upon them that sit in darkness? Glory be to Thee!
*Glory; Both now:*
Unto sinners and publicans, through the multitude of Thy mercy, hast Thou appeared, O our Savior.
For where should Thy light have shone, but upon them that sit in darkness? Glory be to Thee![^835]

*The Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (Chapter 1):*
Thus says the Lord... (see p. 218)[^836]

*The Reading from Genesis (Chapter 32):*[^837]
Jacob looked up, and saw the host of God encamped; and the angels of God met him. And when Jacob saw them, he said: This is the camp of God; and he called the name of that place Camps. And Jacob sent messengers before him to Esau his brother unto the land of Seir, to the country of Edom. And he commanded them, saying: Thus shall ye speak unto my lord Esau: Thus says thy servant Jacob: I have sojourned with Laban, and have tarried until now; and I have acquired sheep, and oxen, and asses, and menservants, and maidservants; and I have sent to tell my lord Esau, that thy servant may find grace before thee. And the messengers returned to Ja- -cob, saying: We came to thy brother Esau, and lo, he himself comes to meet thee, and four hundred men with him. And Jacob was greatly afraid, and was perplexed; and he divided the people that were with him, and the oxen and the sheep, into two camps. And Jacob said: If Esau come to the one camp, and smite it, then the second camp will be saved. And Jacob said: O God of my father Abraham, and God of my father Isaac, the Lord Who saidst unto me: Return unto the land of thy birth, and I will deal well with thee: let me be content with all the righteousness and all the truth which Thou hast shown unto Thy servant; for with this my staff I passed over this Jordan.[^838]

*The Reading from Exodus (Chapter 2):*
The daughter of Pharaoh went down to wash herself at the river, and her maidens walked along by the river side; and when she saw the ark in the flags, she sent her maid to fetch it. And when she had opened it, she saw the child weeping in the ark; and the daughter of Pharaoh had compassion on him, and said: This is one of the Hebrews' children. Then said his sister to Pharaoh's daughter: Wilt thou that I go and call to thee a nurse of the Hebrew women, that she may nurse the child for thee? And Pharaoh's daughter said unto her: Go. And the maid went and called the child's mother. And Pharaoh's daughter said unto her: Take this child away, and nurse him for me, and I will give thee thy wages. And the woman took the child, and nursed him. And when the child was grown, she brought him unto Pharaoh's daughter, and he became her son. And she called his name Moses, saying: Because I drew him out of the water.[^839]

*The Reading from Judges (Chapter 6):*
Gideon said unto God: If Thou wilt save Israel by my hand, as Thou hast said: Behold, I will put a fleece of wool on the threshing floor; and if the dew be on the fleece only, and it be dry upon all the earth beside, then shall I know that Thou wilt save Israel by my hand, as Thou hast said. And it was so. And Gideon rose up early on the morrow, and pressed the fleece together, and wrung the dew out of the fleece, a bowl full of water. And Gideon said unto God: Let not Thine anger be kindled against me, and I will speak but this once; let me prove, I pray Thee, but this once with the fleece; let it now be dry only upon the fleece, and upon all the ground let there be dew. And God did so that night; and it was dry upon the fleece only, and there was dew on all the ground.[^840]

*The Reading from the Third Book of Kings (Chapter 18):*[^841]
Elijah said unto the people: Come near unto me; and all the people came near unto him. And Elijah took twelve stones, according to the number of the twelve tribes of the sons of Israel, unto whom the word of the Lord came, saying: Israel shall be thy name. And with the stones he built up the altar in the name of the Lord, and repaired the altar of the Lord that had been broken down; and Elijah made a trench, containing two measures of seed, round about the altar. And he put the wood in order upon the altar which he had made; and he cut the whole burnt offering in pieces, and laid it on the wood, and compassed the altar. And Elijah said: Take for me four waterpots of water, and let them be poured upon the altar, and upon the whole burnt offering, and upon the wood. And he said: Do it a second time; and they did it a second time. And he said: Do it a third time; and they did it a third time. And the water ran round about the altar, and the trench was filled with water. And Elijah cried aloud unto heaven, and said: O Lord God of Abraham, of Isaac, and of Israel, hear me this day by fire; and let all this people know that Thou art the only Lord God of Israel, and that I am Thy servant, and that for Thy sake I have done all these things; and turn Thou the heart of this people back again unto Thee. Then the fire of the Lord fell from heaven, and consumed the whole burnt offering, and the wood, and the water that was upon them, and the water that was in the trench, and the fire licked up the stones and the dust; and all the people fell upon their face, and said: Truly the Lord is God; He is God![^842]

*The Reading from the Fourth Book of Kings (Chapter 2):*
The men of the city of Jericho said unto Elisha: Behold, the dwelling of this city is good, as thou, my lord, seest; but the waters are evil, and the land barren. And Elisha said: Bring me a new cruse, and put salt therein; and they brought it to him. And he went forth unto the spring of the waters, and cast the salt in there, and said: Thus says the Lord: I have healed these waters; there shall not be from thence any more death or barrenness. And the waters were healed unto this day, according to the word which Elisha spoke.[^843]

*The Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (Chapter 49):*
Thus says the Lord... (see p. 232)[^844]

*Then the Litany:*[^845]
**Priest:** Again and again in peace let us pray to the Lord.
**Choir:** Lord, have mercy.
**Priest:** Help us, save us, have mercy on us, and keep us, O God, by Thy grace.

Remembering our most holy, most pure, most blessed and glorious Lady, the Mother of God and Ever-Virgin Mary, with all the saints, let us commend ourselves and one another, and our whole life unto Christ God.
**Choir:** To Thee, O Lord.
**Priest:** For Thou art a good God and lovest mankind, and unto Thee do we send up glory, to the Father, and to the Son, and to the Holy Spirit, now and ever, and unto ages of ages.
**Choir:** Amen.
*Exclamation for the Trisagion: and the Trisagion.*[^846]
*Then the Prokeimenon, Tone 3:*
The Lord is my light and my Savior; whom shall I fear?
*Verse:* The Lord is the defender of my life; of whom shall I be afraid?[^847]
*The Reading from the Epistle of the Holy Apostle Paul to the Corinthians (Pericope 133):*[^848]
Brethren: Though I be free from all men, yet have I made myself servant unto all, that I might gain the more. And unto the Jews I became as a Jew, that I might gain the Jews; to them that are under the law, as under the law, that I might gain them that are under the law; to them that are without law, as without law, (being not without law to God, but under the law to Christ,) that I might gain them that are without law. To the weak became I as weak, that I might gain the weak: I am become all things to all men, that I might by all means save some. And this I do for the Gospel's sake, that I might be a partaker thereof. Know ye not that they which run in a stadium run all, but one receives the prize? So run, that ye may obtain. And everyone who strives— —is temperate in all things; and they indeed do it to obtain a corruptible crown, but we an incorruptible. I therefore so run, not as uncertainly; so fight I, not as one that beats the air; but I discipline my body, and bring it into subjection, lest that by any means, when I have preached to others, I myself should be a castaway.[^849]
*If, however, it is not a fast, we begin it from the middle:*[^850]
Brethren: I would not have you to be ignorant, brethren, that all our fathers were under the cloud, and all passed through the sea; and were all baptized unto Moses in the cloud and in the sea; and did all eat the same spiritual food; and did all drink the same spiritual drink; for they drank of that spiritual Rock that followed them: and that Rock was Christ.
*Alleluia, Tone 6:*
My heart has poured forth a good word; I speak of my works unto the King.
*Verse:* Beautiful in comeliness beyond the sons of men.[^851]
*The Gospel according to Luke (Pericope 9):*[^852]
Now in the fifteenth year of the reign of Tiberius Caesar, Pontius Pilate being governor of Judea, and Herod being tetrarch of Galilee, and his brother Philip tetrarch of Iturea and of the region of Trachonitis, and Lysanias the tetrarch of Abilene, Annas and Caiaphas being the high priests, the word of God came unto John the son of Zacharias in the wilderness. And he came into all the country about Jordan, preaching the baptism of repentance for the remission of sins— —as it is written in the book of the words of Isaiah the prophet, saying: The voice of one crying in the wilderness: Prepare ye the way of the Lord, make His paths straight. Every valley shall be filled, and every mountain and hill shall be brought low; and the crooked shall be made straight, and the rough ways shall be made smooth; and all flesh shall see the salvation of God. He said therefore to the multitudes that came forth to be baptized of him, saying: O generation of vipers, who has warned you to flee from the wrath to come? Bring forth therefore fruits worthy of repentance, and begin not to say within yourselves: We have Abraham to our father; for I say unto you, that God is able of these stones to raise up children unto Abraham. And now also the axe is laid unto the root of the trees: every tree therefore which brings not forth good fruit is hewn down, and cast into the fire. And the people asked him, saying: What shall we do then? He answered and said unto them: He that has two coats, let him impart to him that has none; and he that has food, let him do likewise. Then came also publicans to be baptized, and said unto him: Master, what shall we do? And he said unto them: Exact no more than that which is appointed you. And the soldiers likewise demanded of him, saying: And what shall we do? And he said unto them: Do violence to no man, neither accuse any falsely; and be content with your wages. And as the people were in expectation, and all men mused in their hearts of John, whether he were the Christ or not; John answered, saying unto them all: I indeed baptize you with water; but One mightier than I comes, the latchet of Whose shoes I am not worthy to unloose—[^853] —He shall baptize you with the Holy Spirit and with fire. Whose fan is in His hand, and He will thoroughly purge His floor, and will gather the wheat into His garner; but the chaff He will burn with fire unquenchable. And many other things in his exhortation preached he unto the people.[^854]

*In place of "It is truly meet", we sing:*
*All of creation rejoices in thee... (see p. 204)[^855]*

*Communion Hymn:*
Praise the Lord from the heavens, praise Him in the heights.[^856]

## Great Sanctification of Water on Holy Theophany

*After the Prayer behind the Ambon, we go forth to the sanctification of water, singing: "The voice of the Lord upon the waters..."*[^857]
*If, however, Vespers is not joined with the Divine Liturgy, then after the Little Litany we do not sing the Trisagion, but immediately the Prokeimenon; then the Epistle and the Gospel, the Litany: "Let us all say", "Vouchsafe, O Lord", "Let us complete our evening [prayer]"; and after the Exclamation we go forth to the sanctification of water, singing:*[^858]
*Tone 8:*
The voice of the Lord upon the waters cries aloud, saying: Come, receive all of you the Spirit of wisdom, the Spirit of understanding, the Spirit of the fear of God, of Christ Who has appeared. *(Thrice)*[^859]
Today the nature of the waters is sanctified, and the Jordan is divided, and turns back the streams of its waters, beholding the Master baptized. *(Twice)*[^860]
As a man Thou didst come to the river, O Christ King, and dost hasten to receive the baptism of a servant— —O Good One, at the hands of the Forerunner, for our sins, O Lover of mankind. *(Twice)*[^861]
*Glory; Both now: In the same Tone.*
Unto the voice of him that cries in the wilderness: "Prepare ye the way of the Lord", Thou didst come, O Lord, having taken the form of a servant, asking for baptism, Who knewest not sin. The waters saw Thee and were afraid; the Forerunner was seized with trembling and cried aloud, saying: How shall the candlestick enlighten the Light? How shall a servant lay his hand upon the Master? Sanctify me and the waters, O Savior, Who takest away the sin of the world.[^862]
*The Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (Chapter 35):*
Thus says the Lord... (see p. 211)[^863]
*The Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (Chapter 55):*[^864]
Thus says the Lord: Ho, everyone that thirsts, come ye to the waters, and he that has no money; come ye, buy, and eat; yea, come, buy wine and milk without money and without price. Why do ye spend money for that which is not bread, and your labor for that which satisfies not? Give ear unto Me, and eat ye that which is good, and let your soul delight itself in fatness. Incline your ears, and follow My paths; hear Me, and your soul shall live in good things; and I will make an everlasting covenant with you, even the sure mercies of David. Behold, I have given Him for a witness to the people, a leader and commander to the people. Behold, nations that knew Thee not shall call upon Thee, and peoples that knew Thee not shall run unto Thee because of the Lord Thy God, and for the Holy One of Israel; for He has glorified Thee. Seek ye God, and when ye find Him— —call upon Him; and whensoever He draws near unto you, let the ungodly forsake his ways, and the unrighteous man his counsels, and return unto the Lord your God, and ye shall find mercy; for He will abundantly pardon your sins. For My counsels are not as your counsels, neither are your ways My ways, says the Lord. But as heaven is distant from earth, so is My way distant from your ways, and your thoughts from My mind. For as rain or snow comes down from heaven, and returns not thither, until it waters the earth, and makes it bring forth and bud, and gives seed to the sower, and bread for food: so shall My word be, whatsoever goes forth out of My mouth, and it shall not return unto Me empty, until it accomplishes all that I have willed; and I will prosper My ways, and My commandments. For ye shall go out with joy, and be taught with gladness; for the mountains and hills shall skip, expecting you with joy, and all the trees of the field shall clap their branches. And instead of the brier shall come up the cypress, and instead of the nettle shall come up the myrtle; and the Lord shall be for a name, and for an everlasting sign, and shall not fail.[^865]
*The Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah (Chapter 12):*
Thus says the Lord... (see p. 225)[^866]
*Prokeimenon, Tone 3:*
The Lord is my light and my Savior; whom shall I fear?
*Verse:* The Lord is the defender of my life; of whom shall I be afraid?[^867]

*The Reading from the Epistle of the Holy Apostle Paul to the Corinthians (Pericope 143):*[^868]
Brethren: I would not have you to be ignorant, that all our fathers were under the cloud, and all passed through the sea; and were all baptized unto Moses in the cloud and in the sea; and did all eat the same spiritual food; and did all drink the same spiritual drink; for they drank of that spiritual Rock that followed them: and that Rock was Christ.
*Alleluia, Tone 4:*
The voice of the Lord is upon the waters; the God of glory has thundered, the Lord is upon many waters.[^869]
*The Gospel according to Mark (Pericope 2):*[^870]
At that time, Jesus came from Nazareth of Galilee, and was baptized of John in the Jordan. And straightway coming up out of the water, He saw the heavens opened, and the Spirit like a dove descending upon Him. And there came a voice from heaven, saying: Thou art My beloved Son, in Whom I am well pleased.
*After this, the Litany:*[^871]
**Deacon:** In peace let us pray to the Lord.
*The Choir says after each petition:*
**Choir:** Lord, have mercy.
**Deacon:** For the peace from above and for the salvation of our souls, let us pray to the Lord.
For the peace of the whole world, for the good estate of the holy churches of God, and for the union of all, let us pray to the Lord.
For this holy temple, and for them that with faith, reverence, and the fear of God enter therein, let us pray to the Lord.

**Deacon:** For our most reverend Archbishop and Metropolitan, Lord *(Name)*, and for our God-loving Bishop, Lord *(Name)*, for the venerable presbytery, the diaconate in Christ, for all the clergy and the people, let us pray to the Lord.[^872]
For our most religious Emperor *(Name)*, for all his court and army, let us pray to the Lord.[^873]
That He will aid him and subdue under his feet every foe and adversary, let us pray to the Lord.
For this city *(or village, or this monastery)*, and for every city, village, and countryside, and for them that dwell therein in faith, let us pray to the Lord.
For seasonable weather, for an abundance of the fruits of the earth, and for peaceful times, let us pray to the Lord.
For them that sail, travel, are sick, weary, imprisoned, and for their salvation, let us pray to the Lord.
That this water may be sanctified by the power, operation, and descent of the Holy Spirit, let us pray to the Lord.[^874]
That there may come down upon this water the cleansing operation of the supersubstantial Trinity, let us pray to the Lord.[^875]
That it may be for the cleansing of the souls and bodies of all who need and receive it in faith, and who partake thereof, let us pray to the Lord.

**Deacon:** That there may be granted unto it the grace of redemption, the blessing of the Jordan, by the power, operation, and descent of the Holy Spirit, let us pray to the Lord.[^876]
That Satan may quickly be crushed under our feet, and every evil counsel stirred up against us may be brought to nought, let us pray to the Lord.
That we may be enlightened with the enlightenment of understanding and piety by the descent of the Holy Spirit, let us pray to the Lord.
That we may receive the gift of sanctification, for the cleansing of sins, for the healing of soul and body, and for every extraordinary benefit, let us pray to the Lord.
That it may be shown to be the sanctification and salvation of our souls, let us pray to the Lord.
That it may serve for the turning away and cleansing of every operation and snare of visible and invisible enemies, let us pray to the Lord.
That it may be shown to be a medicine and cleansing of every disease of our souls and bodies, let us pray to the Lord.
That the power of the soul-destroying adversary may be sunk in this water, let us pray to the Lord.
That we who receive the sanctification of this water may be enlightened through the grace of Christ, let us pray to the Lord.
That it may be unto all who anoint themselves therewith in faith, or partake thereof, for the cleansing of the defilement of flesh and spirit, let us pray to the Lord.
That there may come upon it cleansing by the grace of the supernatural Trinity, let us pray to the Lord.[^877]

**Deacon:** For them that draw and drink thereof unto the sanctification of souls and bodies, let us pray to the Lord.
That it may be a fountain of incorruption and for the sanctification of homes, let us pray to the Lord.
That it may be blessed as the streams of the Jordan, let us pray to the Lord.
That we may be accounted worthy to be filled with sanctification through partaking of this water, by the invisible manifestation of the Holy Spirit, let us pray to the Lord.
That we may be accounted worthy of the Heavenly Kingdom through partaking of this water, let us pray to the Lord.
That the Lord God may hear the voice of our supplication, and have mercy on us, let us pray to the Lord.
That we may be delivered from all affliction, wrath, and need, let us pray to the Lord.
Help us, save us, have mercy on us, and keep us, O God, by Thy grace.
Remembering our most holy, most pure, most blessed and glorious Lady, the Mother of God and Ever-Virgin Mary, with all the saints, let us commend ourselves and one another, and our whole life unto Christ God.[^878]
**Choir:** To Thee, O Lord.

*While these petitions are being said by the Deacon, the Priest prays, saying this prayer secretly to himself. If, however, there is no Deacon, he says it after the conclusion of the diaconal petitions:*[^879]
**Priest:** O Lord Jesus Christ, the Only-begotten Son, Who art in the bosom of the Father, true God, Fountain of life and immortality, Light of Light, Who didst come into the world that Thou mightest enlighten it: illumine our mind with Thy Holy Spirit, and receive us who bring majesty and thanksgiving unto Thee—[^880] —for Thy marvelous mighty deeds from the beginning of time; and for Thy saving dispensation in the latter ages, wherein, having clothed Thyself in our weak and poor nature, and descended to the measure of a servant, O King of all, Thou didst moreover endure to be baptized by a servant's hand in the Jordan, that having sanctified the nature of the waters, O Sinless One, Thou mightest make a way for our regeneration by water and the Spirit, and restore us unto our original freedom. Celebrating the commemoration of this divine mystery, we beseech Thee, O Master, Lover of mankind: sprinkle also upon us, Thine unworthy servants, according to Thy divine promise, clean water, the gift of Thy tender mercy, that our supplication of us sinners over this water may be acceptable through Thy goodness, and that Thy blessing through it may be granted unto us and unto all Thy faithful people, unto the glory of Thy holy and worshipful Name. For unto Thee is due all glory, honor, and worship, with Thine unoriginate Father, and Thy most holy, good, and life-creating Spirit, now and ever, and unto ages of ages.[^881]

*And after the prayer he does not exclaim aloud, but says to himself: Amen. And when: "Remembering our most holy, most pure, most blessed..." is said, the Priest begins again this prayer aloud:*
**Priest:** Great art Thou, O Lord, and marvelous are Thy works, and no word is sufficient to hymn Thy wonders. *(Thrice)*[^882]

*And he signs the water thrice with three burning candles.*

For by Thy will Thou hast brought all things out of non-existence into being, and by Thy power Thou upholdest creation, and by Thy providence Thou orderest the world. Having compounded creation out of four elements, Thou hast crowned the circle of the year with four seasons. Before Thee tremble all the intelligent powers; the sun hymns Thee; the moon glorifies Thee; the stars attend Thee; the light obeys Thee; the abysses tremble before Thee; the springs of water serve Thee. Thou hast stretched out the heaven like a skin; Thou hast established the earth upon the waters; Thou hast walled about the sea with sand; Thou hast poured forth the air for breathing. The archangelic powers minister unto Thee; the angelic choirs worship Thee; the many-eyed Cherubim and the six-winged Seraphim, standing round about and flying, veil themselves in fear of Thine unapproachable glory.[^883]

For Thou, being God uncircumscribed, unoriginate, and ineffable, didst come upon earth, taking the form of a servant, being made in the likeness of men; for Thou couldst not endure, O Master, because of the tender compassions of Thy mercy, to behold mankind tormented by the devil; but Thou didst come and save us. We confess Thy grace, we proclaim Thy mercy, we conceal not Thy benefaction. Thou hast set free the generations of our nature; Thou didst sanctify the virginal womb by Thy birth; all creation hymns Thee Who hast appeared. For Thou, our God, hast appeared on earth, and dwelt among men. Thou didst sanctify the streams of the Jordan, having sent down from heaven Thy Holy Spirit, and Thou didst crush the heads of the dragons that nested there.[^884]

Do Thou Thyself, therefore, O King Lover of mankind, come now also through the visitation of Thy Holy Spirit, and sanctify this water. *(Thrice)*[^885]

*And saying this thrice, the Priest blesses the water with his hand, dipping his fingers in the form of a cross at each verse; then he says this prayer:*
And grant unto it the grace of redemption, the blessing of the Jordan; make it a fountain of incorruption, a gift of sanctification, a loosing of sins, a healing of diseases, a destruction of demons, unapproachable by adverse powers, filled with angelic might; that all who draw and partake thereof may have it for the cleansing of souls and bodies, for the healing of passions, for the sanctification of homes, and for every extraordinary benefit. For Thou art our God, Who by water and the Spirit didst renew our nature grown old through sin; Thou art our God, Who with water didst drown sin in the days of Noah; Thou art our God, Who through the sea, by Moses, didst deliver the Hebrew race from the bondage of Pharaoh; Thou art our God, Who didst cleave the rock in the wilderness, and the waters gushed forth, and the streams overflowed, and didst satisfy Thy thirsty people; Thou art our God, Who by water and fire, through Elijah, didst turn Israel back from the delusion of Baal.[^886]

Do Thou Thyself now also, O Master, sanctify this water by Thy Holy Spirit.

*And the Priest signs it with the cross, breathing upon the water crosswise thrice; then he says:*
Grant also unto all who touch it, and partake thereof, and anoint themselves therewith, sanctification—[^887] —health, cleansing, and blessing. And save, O Lord, Thy servants, the Universal Pontiff [Name], Pope of Rome, and our most devout Emperor [Name], *(The Choir sings Many Years unto them)*, and preserve them under Thy protection in peace; subdue under their feet every enemy and adversary, and grant them all their petitions which are unto salvation, and life eternal. Remember, O Lord, our Archbishop [Name], (or our Bishop [Name]), the whole presbytery, the diaconate in Christ, and every sacred order; and the people here present, and our brethren who for a worthy cause are absent; and have mercy on them and on us, according to Thy great mercy; that also by all elements, and angels, and men, visible and invisible, Thy most holy Name may be glorified, of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, now and ever, and unto ages of ages.[^888]

**Choir:** Amen.
**Priest:** Peace be unto all.
**Choir:** And to thy spirit.
**Deacon:** Bow your heads unto the Lord.
**Choir:** To Thee, O Lord.
*The Priest, having bowed down, prays secretly:*
**Priest:** Incline Thine ear, O Lord, and hear us, Who didst deign to be baptized in the Jordan and didst sanctify the waters; and bless all of us who, by the bowing of our neck, manifest our servant-like semblance; and account us worthy to be filled with Thy sanctification through partaking of this water, and let it be unto us, O Lord, for the health of soul and body.[^889]

*Exclamation:*
**Priest:** For Thou art our sanctification, and unto Thee do we send up glory, and thanksgiving, and worship, with Thine unoriginate Father, and Thy most holy, good, and life-creating Spirit, now and ever, and unto ages of ages.
**Choir:** Amen.

*And immediately, blessing the water crosswise with the precious Cross, he immerses it straight down, dipping it into the water and raising it up, holding it with both hands, singing this Troparion:*[^890]
*Tone 1:*
When Thou, O Lord, wast baptized in the Jordan, the worship of the Trinity was made manifest; for the voice of the Parent bore witness unto Thee, calling Thee Beloved Son, and the Spirit in the form of a dove confirmed the certainty of the word. O Christ God Who hast appeared, and hast enlightened the world, glory to Thee.[^891]

*And a second time he likewise signs the water with the Cross, and a third time likewise, singing the same Troparion, the Priest himself. Then the choirs also sing the same Troparion. And when the server has poured sanctified water into the aspergillum vessel, the Priest, having turned his face to the west, holding the precious Cross in his left hand, and the aspergillum in his right, sprinkles all the people with the sanctified water; and after this the Choir sings:*[^892]
*Glory; Both now: Tone 6.*
Let us hymn, O ye faithful, the greatness of God's beneficence toward us; for having become man because of our transgression, He is cleansed in the Jordan with our cleansing, He Who alone is pure and incorrupt, sanctifying me and the waters, and crushing the heads of the dragons in the water. Let us draw water therefore with gladness, brethren; for the grace of the Spirit— —is invisibly granted unto them that draw in faith from Christ God and the Savior of our souls.[^893]

*Then:*
Blessed be the name of the Lord from this time forth and unto ages. *(Thrice)*

*Psalm 33:*
I will bless the Lord at all times... (see p. 90)[^894]
*And the Dismissal.*

*Some, after the prayer which the Priest says secretly: "O Lord Jesus Christ...", appoint the prayer: "O Trinity supersubstantial..."; yet it is found only in certain editions. We set it forth here:*[^895]
**Priest:** O Trinity supersubstantial, all-good, and transcendent in Godhead, almighty, overlooking all things, invisible, incomprehensible, Creator of intelligent substances and rational natures, supernatural goodness, Light unapproachable, that enlightens every man coming into the world: shine forth also in me, Thine unworthy servant; enlighten the eyes of my mind, that I may dare to hymn Thine immeasurable beneficence and power; and let the supplication made by me for the people here present be acceptable; and let not my transgressions prevent Thy Holy Spirit from coming hither; but forgive me, that uncondemned I may cry aloud unto Thee, and say now, O All-good: We glorify Thee, O Master, Lover of mankind, Almighty, Eternal King; we glorify Thee, Creator and Maker of all; we glorify Thee, without father from a mother, without mother from a Father—[^896] —for in the preceding feast we beheld Thee as an Infant, but in the present feast we behold Thee mature: our God Who hast appeared perfect from the Perfect. For today the time of festival has arrived for us all, and angels feast together with men, and the choir of saints gathers with us; for today the grace of the Holy Spirit, in the appearance of a dove, came down upon the waters; today the unsetting Sun has shone forth, and the world is illuminated with the light of the Lord; today the moon with its bright rays illumines the whole earth; today the light-bearing stars with the brilliance of their radiance adorn the inhabited earth; today the clouds from heaven drop down the dew of righteousness upon mankind; today the Uncreated is willingly touched by the hands of His own creation; today the watery elements take up upon their shoulders the footsteps of the Master; today the Invisible becomes visible, that He may make us beholders of Himself; today the Prophet and Forerunner draws near in love unto the Creator, and stands by with trembling, beholding God's descent unto us; today the Unoriginate bows His neck unto His own servant, that He may drown the dragon that is in the waters; today the prophetic hand touches the flame-bearing head, that with the laver of incorruption He may clothe us; today the streams of the Jordan are ordered for healing unto men by the manifestation of our God; today by mystical streams all creation is watered; today human sins are washed away by the waters of the Jordan; today Paradise is opened unto men, and the Sun of righteousness shines upon us; today the bitter water which—[^897][^898] —was for the people under Moses is transformed into sweetness by the coming of the Lord; today we are delivered from the ancient lamentation, and as the new Israel we have been saved; today we are set free from darkness and illuminated with the light of the knowledge of God; today we put off the old garments of sin, and put on new robes; today the salty waters of the sea are turned into sweetness by Thy manifestation, O Master; today the gloom of the world is abolished by the manifestation of Christ our God; today all nature is brightly illuminated from on high; today delusion is brought to nought, and the path of salvation is wrought for us all by the Master's coming; today the holy and orthodox faith of the Church has shone forth, the spiritual triumph of them that keep the feast; today the things on high feast together with the things below, and the things below are joined with the things on high; today the sacred and loud-voiced festival of the orthodox rejoices; today the Master hastens unto baptism, that He may raise mankind unto the heights; today the Unbowed bows Himself down unto His servant, that He may free us from bondage; today we have purchased the Heavenly Kingdom, for of the Lord's kingdom there is no end; today the earth and the sea of this world have divided their joy, and the world is filled with gladness.[^899]

The waters saw Thee, O God, the waters saw Thee and were afraid; Jordan turned back, beholding the fire of the Godhead descending bodily and entering into it; Jordan turned back, seeing the Holy Spirit in the form of a dove descending and hovering round about it; Jordan turned back, seeing the Invisible become visible, the Creator incarnate—[^900] —the Master in the form of a servant; Jordan turned back, and the mountains skipped, seeing God in the flesh; and the clouds gave voice, marveling at Him Who has come, Light of Light, true God of true God, beholding the Master's festival today in the Jordan; and Him Who drowned in the Jordan transgression itself, death, the sting of delusion, and the bonds of hades, and granted the baptism of salvation unto the world. Therefore I also, a sinner and Thine unworthy servant, declaring the majesty of Thy wonders, held fast by fear, in compunction cry unto Thee.[^901]

---

# PART VI: THE FEAST OF HOLY THEOPHANY AND SYNAXIS OF ST. JOHN THE FORERUNNER (GREAT COMPLINE, MATINS, LITURGY, AND VESPERS OF THE FORERUNNER)

## Great Compline on Holy Theophany[^902]

**Priest:** Blessed is our God...
**Choir:** Amen.
*Glory to Thee, our God, glory to Thee. O Heavenly King... Trisagion. And the rest as on the Eve of the Nativity:*
*We cry aloud with singing: "God is with us"; and in place of the usual Troparia: "Illumine my eyes...", we say the Troparion of Theophany:*
*When Thou, O Lord, wast baptized in the Jordan... (see p. 260)[^903]*
*And in place of: "Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us...", we say the Kontakion of Theophany:*
*Tone 4:*
Thou hast appeared today unto the inhabited earth, and Thy light, O Lord, is signed upon us who with understanding sing unto Thee: Thou hast come and hast appeared, O Light unapproachable.[^904]

*After "Glory to God in the highest", there is the Litya.*

*Litya.*
*Idiomelon Stichera, Tone 4:*
He Who clothes Himself with light as with a garment vouchsafed for our sake to become like unto us, and—[^905] —is clothed today with the streams of the Jordan, not needing these for purification, but ordering for us our regeneration through Himself. O the wonder! Without fire He refashions, and without breaking He restores, and saves them that are enlightened in Him: Christ God and the Savior of our souls.[^906]

Beholding Thee in the Spirit and with fire cleansing the sin of the world, the Baptizer, as he saw Thee coming unto him, filled with awe and trembling, cried aloud, saying: I dare not hold Thy most pure head; do Thou sanctify me, O Master, by Thy divine Epiphany, O only Lover of mankind.[^907]

Come, let us imitate the wise virgins; come, let us meet the Master Who has appeared, for He comes as a Bridegroom unto John. Jordan, seeing Thee, was afraid and halted; John cried aloud: I dare not touch the head of the Immortal One; the Spirit descended in the form of a dove to sanctify the waters; and a voice from heaven [cried]: "This is My Son, Who has come into the world to save the human race." O Lord, glory to Thee.[^908]

Christ is baptized, and comes up from the water, for He raises the world with Himself; and He beholds the heavens opening, which Adam shut against himself and them that were with him; and the Spirit bears witness unto His Godhead, for He anticipates Him that is equal, and a voice [resounds] from heaven; for from thence is He witnessed to, the Savior of our souls.[^909]

The baptizer's hand trembled when it touched Thy most pure head; the river Jordan turned back, not daring to minister unto Thee; for if it had respect unto Joshua the son of Nun, how—[^910] —should it not have been afraid before its own Creator? But Thou hast fulfilled the whole dispensation, O our Savior, that Thou mightest save the world by Thine Epiphany, O only Lover of mankind.[^911]

*Glory: Tone 8:*
O Lord, desiring to fulfill what Thou hadst ordained from eternity, Thou didst accept from all creation ministers of Thy mystery: from among the angels, Gabriel; from among men, the Virgin; from the heavens, the star; and from the waters, the Jordan, in which Thou didst wipe away the transgression of the world. O our Savior, glory to Thee.[^912]

*Now and ever: Tone the same:*
Today creation is illuminated; today all things rejoice, things heavenly together with things earthly; angels and men mingle together. For where the King's presence is, there also His retinue arrives. Let us run, therefore, unto the Jordan, and let us all see John, how he baptizes the uncreated and sinless Head. Therefore, chanting in accompaniment the apostolic voice, let us cry aloud in unison: The saving grace of God has appeared unto all men, illuminating and granting great mercy unto the faithful.[^913]

*At the Aposticha, Idiomelon Stichera, Tone 2:*
Seeing Thee coming unto him at the river Jordan, John said: O Christ God, why hast Thou come unto a servant, O Lord Who hast no defilement? In whose name shall I baptize Thee? In the Father's? But Him Thou bearest in Thyself. In the Son's? But Thou Thyself art the incarnate One. In the Holy Spirit's? And This Thou knowest how to give unto the faithful by the breath of Thy mouth. O God Who hast appeared, have mercy on us.[^914]

*Verse: The sea saw it and fled; Jordan was driven back.*

The waters saw Thee, O God, the waters saw Thee and were afraid; for the Cherubim cannot gaze upon Thy glory, nor the Seraphim look upon it; but standing by in fear, some indeed bear [Thee], while others glorify Thy power; with whom, O Merciful One, we proclaim Thy praise, saying: O God Who hast appeared, have mercy on us.[^915]

*Verse: What ailed thee, O sea, that thou didst flee? And thou, Jordan, that thou wast driven back?*

Today the Creator of heaven and earth comes in the flesh unto the Jordan, the Sinless One seeking baptism, that He may cleanse the world from the enemy's deception; and the Master of all is baptized by a servant, and grants purification through water unto the human race. Unto Him let us cry aloud: O our God Who hast appeared, glory to Thee.[^916]

*Glory... Now and ever... Tone 6:*
The bright Lamp, who is from a barren woman, seeing the Sun, Who is from the Virgin, asking baptism in the Jordan, cried aloud unto Him in awe and joy: Sanctify Thou me, O Master, by Thy divine Epiphany.[^917]

*Then: Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart... (see p. 89);[^918] Trisagion. And after Our Father: Troparion of Theophany, Tone 1:*
*When Thou, O Lord, wast baptized in the Jordan... (see p. 260);[^919]*
*And the blessing of loaves. Then: Blessed be the name of the Lord... (three times). And Psalm 33: I will bless the Lord at all times... (see p. 90).[^920]*

## Matins on Holy Theophany[^921]

*After the Six Psalms, at "God is the Lord", the Troparion of Theophany, three times; and the usual Kathisma readings from the Psalter. After the first Kathisma reading, Sessional Hymn, Tone 3. Podoben: Beholding the beauty of thy virginity...*
When Thou wast manifested in the Jordan, O Savior, and wast baptized by the Forerunner, O Christ, Thou wast witnessed to as the beloved Son; therefore Thou wast also revealed as co-unoriginate with the Father; and the Holy Spirit descended upon Thee, enlightened by Whom we cry aloud: Glory to God Who exists in Trinity.[^922]

*Glory... Now and ever... Tone 4. Podoben: Speedily anticipate...*
Thou hast sanctified the streams of the Jordan, and crushed the dominion of sin, O Christ our God; Thou didst bow Thyself down beneath the hand of the Forerunner, and didst save the human race from deception. Therefore we beseech Thee: Save our souls.[^923]

*After the second Kathisma reading, Sessional Hymn, Tone 5. Podoben: Let us, the faithful, praise the Word co-unoriginate...*
Wrapped in the streams of the Jordan, Thou Who art most gloriously clothed with light as with a garment, in them Thou hast renewed Adam's nature, crushed by evil disobedience, O Word of God. Therefore we praise Thee, and all of us glorify Thy holy Epiphany.[^924]

*Glory... Now and ever... Tone 4. Podoben: Joseph was amazed...*
O river Jordan, why wast thou amazed, beholding the Invisible One naked? I saw and trembled, it said; and how should I not have been afraid of Him and retreated? His angels were terrified as they beheld Him; heaven was dismayed, and earth trembled—[^925] —the sea was troubled, and all things visible and invisible. Christ has appeared at the Jordan to sanctify the waters.[^926]

*Polyeleos.*
*Praise the name of the Lord... (see p. 99).[^927]*

*Again, the Megalynarion:*
We magnify Thee, O Christ Giver of life, Who for our sake wast baptized in the flesh by John in the waters of the Jordan.[^928]

*Verse: God be bountiful unto us, and bless us, cause His face to shine upon us, and have mercy on us.*
*And the Lord thundered from heaven, and the Most High gave forth His voice.*
*The floods have lifted up, O Lord, the floods have lifted up their voices.*
*Therefore will I remember Thee from the land of Jordan, and of Hermon.*
*The sea saw it and fled; Jordan was driven back.*
*The waters saw Thee, O God, the waters saw Thee and were afraid.*
*Thy ways are in the sea, and Thy paths in many waters.*
*How magnified are Thy works, O Lord! In wisdom hast Thou made them all.*
*Glory... Now and ever... Alleluia (three times).*
My Jesus is baptized by a servant in the river Jordan, illuminating all creation.[^929]

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4. Podoben: Joseph was amazed...*
Come, O faithful, let us behold where Christ was baptized; let us now follow unto the river Jordan, unto the voice of one crying in the wilderness; and let us see there the Creator of Adam, having a servant's hand laid upon Him in His ineffable tender compassion, and let us cry aloud unto Him with a loud voice: Thou hast come and hast appeared in the Jordan to sanctify the waters.[^930]

*Glory... Now and ever... the same.*

*Gradual Hymns, the 1st Antiphon of Tone 4:*
*From my youth... (see p. 101).[^931]*

**Priest:** Let us attend!
*And the Prokimenon, Tone 4:*
*The sea saw it and fled; Jordan was driven back.*
*Verse: What ailed thee, O sea, that thou didst flee, and thou, Jordan, that thou wast driven back?*

*The Gospel according to Mark, Pericope 2:*
*At that time, Jesus came... (see p. 226).[^932]*

*After Psalm 50, Glory... Tone 2:*
Today let all creation rejoice, for Christ has appeared in the Jordan.[^933]
*Now and ever... the same.*

*Then, the Idiomelon Sticheron, Tone 6:*
God the Word appeared in the flesh unto the human race; He stood to be baptized in the Jordan, and the Forerunner said unto Him: How shall I stretch forth my hand and touch the head of Him Who upholds all things—[^934] —though Thou art an Infant from Mary, yet I know Thee to be the pre-eternal God; Thou walkest upon earth Who art hymned by the Seraphim, and I, a servant, have not learned to baptize the Master. Incomprehensible Lord, glory to Thee.[^935]

*Canon.*
*There are two Canons: the Heirmoi of both canons twice, the Troparia to 12. And afterwards, the first choir chants the Heirmos of the first Canon, and the second choir chants the Heirmos of the second Canon.*

*Tone 2. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
The Lord, mighty in battles, has laid bare the bottom of the deep, and leads His own across dry land, having covered the adversaries therein: for He has been glorified.[^936]
By the streams of the Jordan the Lord, the King of ages, renews fallen Adam, and crushes the heads of the dragons nesting therein: for He has been glorified.[^937]
Clothed in material flesh with the immaterial fire of the Godhead, the Lord Who was incarnate of the Virgin is enveloped in the Jordan's water: for He has been glorified.[^938]
Washing away the defilement of men, the Lord, Who enlightens those who are in darkness, is cleansed in the Jordan for their sakes, having willed to be made like unto them, while remaining what He was: for He has been glorified.[^939]

*Second Canon, Tone 2. Heirmos:*
Israel traverses the surging tempest of the sea, suddenly revealed as dry land; while the Red Sea completely submerged the Egyptian captains, a watery grave, by the mighty strength of the Master's right hand.[^940]

When the light-bearing dawn appeared unto men, coming now from the wilderness unto the streams of the Jordan, O King, Sun [of righteousness], Thou didst bow Thy neck to snatch away the dark prince from his flock, and to cleanse creation from all defilement.[^941]

Co-buried with Thee in the streams, O unoriginate Word, Thou bringest him who was corrupted by deception made new again, receiving this mighty voice ineffably from the Father: This is My beloved Child, and equal unto Me in nature.[^942]

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
The Lord Who gives strength unto our kings, and exalts the horn of His anointed, is born of the Virgin, and comes unto baptism; unto Him, O faithful, let us cry aloud: There is none holy as our God.[^943]

Rejoice today, O Church of Christ, who aforetime wast barren and grievously childless; for through water and the Spirit sons have been born unto thee, crying aloud in faith: There is none holy as our God.[^944]

With a loud voice the Forerunner cries aloud in the wilderness: Prepare the ways of Christ, and make straight the paths for our God, crying aloud in faith: There is none holy as our God.[^945]

*Second Canon. Heirmos:*
As many as have been released from the ancient snares of the lions whose teeth are broken, let us rejoice and open wide our mouth, weaving words of sweet singing from words [of praise], whereby He takes delight in the gifts granted unto us.[^946]

He who aforetime planted mortification in creation, having taken on the nature of the malevolent beast, is darkened by [Christ's] bodily coming; having rushed against the Master when the dawn appeared, that he might crush his own hostile head.[^947]

The tyrant's belly draws unto itself the nature fashioned by God, buried within its bounds; but the renewal of earthborn men is born anew, the Master mightily accomplishing the work: for He has come, desiring to cleanse it.[^948]

*Hypakoe, Tone 5:*
When Thou didst enlighten all things by Thine Epiphany, then the salty sea of unbelief fled, and Jordan flowing downward turned back, raising us up unto heaven; but by the height of Thy divine commandments preserve us, O Christ God, through the prayers of the Theotokos, and have mercy on us.[^949]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I have heard, O Lord, Thy voice which Thou didst speak, the voice of one crying in the wilderness, when Thou didst thunder over many waters, bearing witness unto Thy Son; being filled with the Spirit Who descended, he cried aloud: Thou art Christ, the wisdom and the power of God.[^950]

Who has seen the sun, which is most radiant by nature, being cleansed? the preacher cries aloud. Shall I wash Thee with waters, the Radiance of glory, the Image of the ever-existing Father? And shall I, who am grass, touch the fire of Thy Godhead? For Thou art Christ, the wisdom and the power of God.[^951]

Moses manifested the divine reverence which he possessed when he drew near unto Thee; for when he perceived that Thou didst speak out of the—[^952] —bush, he immediately turned away his face; how then shall I behold Thee clearly, or how shall I place my hand upon Thee? For Thou art Christ, the wisdom and the power of God.[^953]

Accomplishing what is of the soul wisely, and honored with reason, I am put to shame by things inanimate; for if I baptize Thee, the mountain smoking with fire will accuse me, as will the sea divided in two, and this Jordan which turned back: for Thou art Christ, the wisdom and the power of God.[^954]

*Second Canon. Heirmos:*
Cleansed by the fire of mystical contemplation, the prophet, singing the renewal of mankind, utters a voice inspired by the Spirit, declaring the incarnation of the ineffable Word, by Whom the dominions of the mighty have been shattered.[^955]

Sent forth from the Father, O all-radiant Word, Thou comest to drive away the dark and evil surge of night, and to uproot the sins of men; and by Thy baptism, O Good One, to draw forth radiant sons from the streams of the Jordan.[^956]

Foreseeing the very Word foretold, the preacher cries aloud clearly unto creation: This One, Who is after me, second according to the flesh, is made like unto us, shining forth with divine power to take away our hostile sin.[^957]

Bearing in Himself life-giving pasture, God the Word captures the nests of dragons, rushing upon them with many snares and casting them down; He trips up him who wounded the race of all the world, and having destroyed him, delivers creation.[^958]

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Jesus, the Prince of life, comes to loose the condemnation of Adam the first-formed; and though as God He needs no cleansing, He cleanses fallen [man] in the Jordan, wherein having slain the enmity, He grants the peace that passes all understanding.[^959]

When countless people came together to be baptized by John, He Himself stood in their midst; and he proclaimed aloud unto those who were present: Who has shown you, O disobedient ones, to avoid the wrath to come? Bring forth fruits worthy for Christ; for He Who stands present now grants peace.[^960]

The Husbandman and Creator, standing in their midst as one of all, searches the hearts; and taking into His hand the winnowing fan, He all-wisely separates the threshing floor of the whole world, burning up the unfruitful, and granting eternal life unto the fruitful.[^961]

*Second Canon. Heirmos:*
Washed clean by the purification of the Spirit from the dark and defiled poison of the enemy, we have attained unto a new, unerring path, leading unto unapproachable joy, accessible only unto those with whom God has been reconciled.[^962]

Seeing him whom He formed from the dust bound with inescapable cords in the gloom of transgressions, the Creator lifts him up and places him upon both shoulders; for now in the abundant flowing waters He washes away the ancient shame of Adam's wicked disposition.[^963]

With piety let us eagerly run unto the most pure fountains of the saving stream, beholding the Word from an undefiled [Virgin]—[^964] —drawing vessels of divine thirst, sweetly healing the disease of the world.[^965]

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
The voice of the Word, the lamp of the Light, the morning star of the Sun, the Forerunner, cries aloud in the wilderness unto all peoples: Repent, and purify yourselves beforehand; for behold, Christ is present, delivering the world from corruption.[^966]

Born incorruptibly from God and the Father, Christ is incarnate without defilement from the Virgin; the Forerunner teaches that it is hard to loose the strap—the union of the Word with us—delivering the earthborn from deceit.[^967]

With final fire Christ baptizes those who oppose and do not esteem Him as God; but by the Spirit He renews through water of grace those who acknowledge His Godhead, delivering [them] from transgressions.[^968]

*Second Canon. Heirmos:*
With a most blessed voice the Father made known the Beloved, Whom He brought forth from the womb; yea, He says: This consubstantial Son, being radiant with light, has shone forth from the human race; [He is] My living Word, and by providence a man.[^969]

From the belly of the deep monster, the prophet strangely tumbled within for three evenings; straightway he came forth, manifesting unto all in the last times the salvation of regeneration from the manslaying dragon.[^970]

The disciple beholds the heavens opened, [as spoken] in the all-radiant Scriptures, [the Spirit] sent forth from the Father; and the Spirit abiding upon the most pure Word, having come upon [Him] as—[^971] —a dove in an ineffable manner, is revealed unto the peoples who have drawn near unto the Master.[^972]

*Kontakion, Tone 4:*
Thou hast appeared today... (see p. 264)[^973]

*Ikos:*
Unto Galilee of the Gentiles, unto the country of Zebulun, and unto the land of Naphtali, as the prophet said: a great light, Christ, has shone forth unto those who were darkened; a radiant dawn has appeared, flashing forth from Bethlehem; yea rather, the Lord from Mary, the Sun of righteousness, shines rays upon the whole universe; therefore, all you who are naked from Adam, come, let us clothe ourselves in Him, that we may be warmed; for He covers the naked and enlightens those who are in darkness: Thou hast come and appeared, the unapproachable Light.[^974]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
When the godly youths were committed unto the fiery furnace, a rustling dewy wind preserved them unharmed, as did the descent of the angel of God; therefore, bedewed in the midst of the flame, they sang in thanksgiving: Blessed art Thou, O exceedingly praised Lord and God of our fathers.[^975]

Even as in heaven, the angelic hosts stood in the Jordan with trembling and wonder, beholding so great a condescension of God: how He Who holds the firmament of the waters above stood clothed in flesh in the waters, the God of our fathers.[^976]

Of old the cloud and the sea prefigured the wonder of divine baptism, wherein the ancient people of the Law were baptized when departing; and the sea—[^977] —was an image of the water, and the cloud of the Spirit; being perfected by them, we cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto ages.[^978]

All we the faithful, in Whom we have received perfection, unceasingly blessing Him with the angels, let us glorify the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit; for behold, the Trinity is consubstantial in hypostases, yet one God; unto Whom we sing: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto ages.[^979]

*Second Canon. Heirmos:*
He Who with a stream burned the heads of the dragons, and extinguished the high flame of the furnace holding the godly youths, has stilled the uncontrollable gloom born of sin, and washes away all things by the dew of the Spirit.[^980]

Portraying Thee in image, Thou dost render terrible the Assyrian flame turned into dew; therefore, the water now, even as the flame, O Christ, burns the hidden harmful malice that clothes [the enemy], calling [us] back from the slippery path.[^981]

When the Jordan was divided of old, the people of Israel passed through on dry ground, depicting Thee, the most mighty One Who upholds creation without delay, now in the waters, [leading] unto an incorruptible and salutary path.[^982]

We know of the all-destroying deluge of old, whereby Thou didst mercifully bring all things into corruption, O Thou Who workest most great and marvelous deeds; but now, O Christ, Thou hast drowned sin through Thy tender compassion and for human salvation![^983]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
The Babylonian furnace showed forth a most glorious mystery, pouring forth dew, because the Jordan was to receive with its streams—[^984] —the immaterial fire, and embrace the Creator being baptized in the flesh; Whom the peoples bless and highly exalt unto all ages.[^985]

Lay aside all fear, the Deliverer said unto the Forerunner, and submit unto Me as good, approach Me, for by nature I have become this; obey My command, and baptize Me Who have descended; Whom the peoples bless and highly exalt unto all ages.[^986]

When the Baptist heard the Master's words, he stretched forth his palm with trembling; yet touching with his hand the head of his Creator, he cried aloud unto Him Who was being baptized: Sanctify me, for Thou art my God, Whom the peoples bless and highly exalt unto all ages.[^987]

A manifestation of the Trinity took place in the Jordan; for the supreme divine Nature Itself, the Father, proclaimed: This One Who is baptized is My beloved Son; and the Spirit came upon His peer; Whom the peoples bless and highly exalt unto all ages.[^988]

*Second Canon. Heirmos:*
Creation is recognized as set free, and those previously darkened are [now] sons of light; the ruler of darkness alone groans; now let the inheritance of the Gentiles, formerly wretched, fervently bless the Cause.[^989]

The three godlike youths, bedewed in the fire and illuminated with three all-radiant sanctities, clearly showed forth the transcendent Nature in human communion, mercifully burning up all destructive deceit by dew.[^990]

Let all earthly nature be whitened, now being led up unto heaven from the fall; for having been washed in the flowing streams by the Word by Whom all things are preserved, it has escaped former transgressions, washed most radiantly.[^991]

*At Ode 9 we do not sing "More honorable", but we sing the Refrains of Theophany:*
Magnify, O my soul, the most pure Virgin Theotokos, more honorable than the heavenly hosts.[^992]

*And the Heirmos:*
Every tongue is at a loss to praise [Thee] worthily, and even an intellect beyond this world is dazed in singing [praise] unto Thee, O Theotokos; yet, being good, accept our faith, for Thou knowest our divine love; for Thou art the protectress of Christians: Thee do we magnify.[^993]

*Then the second choir sings the same refrain and the heirmos; the other six refrains are chanted to each troparion once:*
Magnify, O my soul, Him Who seeks baptism from the Forerunner.[^994]
Magnify, O my soul, Him Who has come to be baptized in the Jordan.[^995]

O David, come in the Spirit unto the enlightened, singing: Draw near now unto God with faith, saying: Be enlightened; this poor Adam cried in his fall, for the Lord gave ear unto him, and having come unto the streams of the Jordan, renewed him who was corrupt.[^996]

Magnify, O my soul, Him Who was borne witness to by the Father's voice.[^997]
Magnify, O my soul, One of the Trinity, Who bowed His neck and received baptism.[^998]

Isaiah says: Wash yourselves, make yourselves clean, put away your evil doings before the Lord; you who thirst, go unto living water; for Christ sprinkles with water, renewing those who draw near unto Him with faith, and baptizes with the Spirit unto unaging life.[^999]

*Refrain:* O prophet, come unto Me, stretch forth thy hand and baptize Me quickly.[^1000]
*Refrain:* O prophet, permit it now and baptize Me who desire it; for I have come to fulfill all righteousness.[^1001]

We the faithful are preserved by grace and by the seal; for as of old the Hebrews escaped the destroyer through the blood-marked lintel, so also this divine bath of regeneration shall be an exodus for us; and hence we shall behold the unwaning light of the Trinity.[^1002]

*Then the first choir sings the Refrain of the Second Canon:*
Today the Master bows His neck beneath the hand of the Forerunner.[^1003]

*Heirmos:*
O the marvels beyond comprehension of thy childbearing, all-pure Bride, blessed Mother! Having obtained all-perfect salvation through her, we worthily praise [Thee] as our Benefactor, bringing as a gift a song of thanksgiving.[^1004]

*And the second choir likewise sings the refrain and the heirmos; and the other five refrains, each once:*
*Refrain:* Today John baptizes the Master in the streams of the Jordan.[^1005]
*Refrain:* Today the Master buries human sin with the waters.[^1006]

We have come to know what was revealed unto Moses by the bush; come, let us behold what was accomplished by strange decrees: for as the Virgin, carrying fire, was preserved unharmed, [having] brought forth the light-bearing—[^1007] —Benefactor, manifested in the streams of the Jordan.[^1008]

*Refrain:* Today the Master is borne witness to from on high as the beloved Son.[^1009]
*Refrain:* Today the Master receives baptism by the hand of the Forerunner.[^1010]

Thou anointest human nature, perfecting it, O King without beginning, by communion of the Spirit, having washed it from gloom with pure streams; and having put to shame the exalted power, [Thou grantest us] now to dwell in unending life.[^1011]

*Then both choirs, having come together, sing the first refrain of Theophany, and the heirmos:*
Every tongue is at a loss...[^1012]

*Then the refrain of the second canon, and the heirmos:*
O the marvels beyond comprehension...[^1013]
